This is the detailed record of every change, kept as it was written; the short list for players is [CHANGELOG.md](../../CHANGELOG.md).

# Changelog

## Unreleased (detail)

- **Arsveil years 6-10 (ARS-LATE).** `examples/packs/arsveil/pack.json` gets 35 more world events (`late-*`, 317 in all) and 59 facts, for days 1826-3649; `arsveil.zip` rebuilt. Against the fragment: where one firing cleared and set the same condition (the engine then refuses the set), the clear is dropped or moved to the outcomes that do not set it (42 changes in 23 events); the 29 branch-gated beats are `likely` with `100% over the window`, so an untaken branch lapses without a warning; `late-third-tide-second-stage` is blocked once the tap has been shut (`fact:tap-closed` or `fact:tap-reopened`). The pack description now says about ten years.
- **Arsveil years 6-10 by ending branch (ARS-LATE-BRANCH).** 25 of those events are now one event per ending branch (`late-<topic>-<branch>`, 74 in place of 25; 366 events in all), each keyed on a fact only that ending writes, so an outcome cannot name another ending; later chain steps wait on whichever split fired. Some outcomes were rewritten, replaced or dropped to fit their branch. `late-third-tide-second-stage` stays whole, with one outcome renamed.
- **Arsveil cast (ARS-CAST).** `examples/packs/arsveil/pack.json` has ten `companion` entries with WebP portraits (in `arsveil.zip`), the event `bw-fighter-arrives`, and the fact `saintesses-reconciled` on the shared-rite outcome. The second list of lore people replaces the last twenty Ersveil ones; lore for Cassian Vell, the Sunrise Man, Ilvareth, Sigrun Coldrivet, Hob Ashbarrow and Matthias Orrel; the village Lowbell with a road to Saint Loria's Gate; the Saintess Ilvane entry is gone (the Veil Saintess is in the cast).

- **World cast text limits (ARS-CAST).** A world-pack cast entry (`kind`) may now hold `look` 800, `wears` 600, `voice` 600, `personality` 900 and `start.routine` 300 characters (were 600, 300, 300, 600, 200), within what NPC State keeps. `look` and `wears` together are at most 1,195, and the scanner's and the profile refresh's view of a cast dossier's appearance is cut at 1,200 (was 800 and 900), as the narrator's is, so the clothes at its end stay in view; an older Story Suite refuses a person over the old limits.

- **Arsveil world events assembled (ARS-ASM).** The five event slices are merged into `examples/packs/arsveil/pack.json` (281 events), with 23 powers, three scores (flame-faith, ledger-trust, wild-swarm), twelve more templates and a Thinning festival in the start world; `arsveil.zip` rebuilt.

- **World events find places by their pack ids (ARS-ASM, ARS-ENGINE-FIX).** On the host, `packs.content` gives each place the id `pack.<pack>/<slug>`, but a pack's events, start world and powers name the file's own id. `buildGeo` indexed only name, that id and aliases, so a condition set on `bluewatch-village` or `highmark-capital-crown-keep` was refused with a warning on the step's card (the pack check accepted it). The slug is now indexed too, after names and aliases, and never where it already means a region, a region's name, a realm or a route; two places with one display name each keep their own slug. The effect is wider than conditions: the review's scan of Ersveil found 28 events with a field that needed this (overlapping groups: 7 setting or clearing conditions, 17 with such a `where`, 14 moving cast by `location` / `from`). So realm caps, news origin and delay, `in:` / `near:` regions and cast moves change too, and an event with no eligible place before (`winter-passes`) now fires. The `@ref` of a `set:` / `cleared:` anchor and of a cast move's `until` is read as the place's name (`cleared:evacuated@bluewatch-village`; `cleared:fallen@auvoth` also failed on letter case alone), and a cast home written as a file id or an alias is that place for evacuation and protection. A ref the map does not know stays as written.
  - **Re-read.** New rule mark `packPlaceIds`: a narrator condition (`world … | conditions:`) naming a place by file id is taken only on a reply captured with the mark; an older reply still refuses it on Re-read. The day step runs after capture with no reply mark and is not gated (lead decision: accepted as a bug fix): a Re-read of an old reply runs its day step with the new lookup, so it can set conditions, move people and fire events that were not recorded before. Stored results do not change without a Re-read.

- **World event limit raised (ARS-ASM).** `WORLD_LIMITS.events` 200 → 400: the Arsveil design asks for about 250-280 events. Only the pack check reads the number; the daily step runs every event it is given. The 1 MB pack.json limit is unchanged.

- **World events that know where the party is (owner, 26 Nov, PROX).** World packs may write `in:<region>` / `near:<region>` (also `@region`) in `requires`, `requires_any` and as `likelier_if` / `less_if` keys, and `waits_for` on a windowed event: it waits for the party and fires anyway on its window's last day (over the caps, no Director veto; `×0` or a 0% chance still blocks a likely or possible one). Neighbouring regions come from routes. The party's region is the scene's place as the world note reads it, on the day the story is on only (a skip's earlier days have none). The pack check and the Studio's rename know the terms. Packs without them step as before.

- **Pacing, mounts and supplies review fixes (FIX-A).** The Breather, Wrap-up and Quiet scene lines are plain tail lines under the header (their room is still held when the note is built), so they take none of the shared kept-line room. An open story beat holds a chain and counts toward the five only while live: with a readable date and a story clock, while the date is at most one story day ahead or past; otherwise while made, changed, seen or engaged in the last 15 replies (`LIVE_REPLIES`). The Wrap-up names up to three open beats, oldest first. No Breather while an arc beat is on the Due now line. The Quiet scene message also says new threads are held back. Story item lines judge a mount's riders by the names held in state, never the live persona name (`gear.carry { stored: true }`); the display keeps the live name. The narrator's Mounts line says rows of one kind and pace that are with the party (saying no more than who rides) once, with every name, as it does rows left at one same `where`; a row with a condition or other words keeps its own entry. Taking a rider off a bare list keeps its other parts. The supplies grammar names bandages and herbs and says a healing potion or draught stays a consumable.
- Choices: `CHOICE_KEEP` 160 → 400 (modules/scene/extras-parts/limits.js); read at display time from the reply, so old replies show whole too; the narrator is still asked for 80.
- Feelings ring (ui/kit/ring.js): linear sweep instead of square root, with a 3° minimum for a non-zero meter.
- NPC State review fixes: the closest person's not-yet-told line sits before the opened notes in the required lines (older opened notes are cut first); an earned reveal needs the gate's `quote:` terms said; may-come-out lines come before other people's not-yet-told lines; `CONTEXT_BUDGET` 305 → 380.

- **Pack secrets the narrator knows, and a reveal the story can earn (FU145).** With world events on, each present person's next closed pack secret (the first in pack order that is neither opened nor shut by hand) is sent as `Name (not yet told, <id>): text` (one a person, three in all), and `(may come out)` lines now carry the id, so a `~ reveal` line can be written. Before, a closed secret sent nothing (the narrator invented a past) and no line named the id. New rule mark `revealNext`: the reveal accepts the pack id, and may open that next closed secret with its quote. Older replies are read as before. What's sent hides both kinds of line. NPC State's section is still held to its own token limit, so in a busy scene these lines can be left out.
- **Ersveil Guild probation ends; contracts count (owner, 26 Nov, FU149).** Pack data only (`ersveil-game` 1.0.0 to 1.1.0, `ersveil.zip` rebuilt). Standing was `probation` while `member-years` was 0, which only a Guild year end changes. It is now `probation` only while a member has had no year end, is rank F and has done fewer than 3 contracts, or has been idle a year; `good` is the exact complement while dues are paid. New fields `contracts`, `contracts-at-rank` (a promotion resets it; both stop at 999) and `exam` (set by rules, not editable): `ready for the E examination` at 5 contracts since the last promotion, D 12, C 25, B 45, A 70, S 100. The prompt tail lists them with the other fields (by field key); Story › Trackers shows them with their labels, and the pack screen lists them too. No engine change and no rule mark: a chat keeps the definition it pinned, and replays by it. One reply still counts one contract (the once-per-reply claim). New test: tests/packs/ersveil-guild.test.js. Review fixes before release: a `promote` line naming the current letter is refused ("Register first; a promotion names a new letter."), so a restated rank no longer wipes the count toward the next examination, and at S the examination reads "no further examination".
- **The dossier library with many people (owner, 26 Nov, FU152).** With 69 people the strip was one long row in the view's order with only All / ★ Following. It now orders party, here, followed, then last seen (the dead last); offers Around (default), Party, ★ Following and All with counts; searches names and aliases inside the chip; steps through the chip with ‹ › or the arrow keys; and the folded line shows the Around people with "+N more". UI only: nothing stored, no prompt text changed.
- **World news goes old; a way to remove factions (owner, 26 Nov, FU148).** News never expired, and a faction the story had finished stayed listed. Open news with no new word for 30 story days (`STALE_NEWS_DAYS`, `chronicle/domain-parts/stale.js`; counted from the later of its last change and its arrival here) is now old: `director.world` news carries `stale: true`, World › News shows "old news" under an Older filter and last under All, and the [World] tail and the world note leave it out. It is worked out on read; the stored state, the event pick and the safety net's list of open names are unchanged, so a later `~ world` line with new words brings it back (one that repeats the old words changes nothing, as before). Never old: kept or locked entries, pack timeline entries, pulse rumours, resolved news, and a dated happening whose day is still to come. No code reads a `~ faction` line's words to remove a faction (a word matcher tried before release could delete a faction on an ordinary update, so it was taken out). The faction grammar now says "- faction when it is gone or broken up (wiped out, disbanded), never ~". The World editor gained Remove for a faction (second tap; `chronicle.remove`) and Mark ended for open world news (`chronicle.edit` with state resolved).

- **"Who you are to them" updates from the story (FU151, owner, 26 Nov).** In lines mode only a pack's `withYou` set `relationshipSummary`, once, at meeting; only the old JSON scan or a Refresh changed it. A `~ npc Name | toward:` line now writes it (one sentence, clipped to 320 characters), unless the reader edited it by hand or the person has died, and stamps the dossier's last change. A new owner-approved one-pass rule asks for the line at turning points. A Re-read of an older reply never gains the write (rule mark `npcToward`). The sentence is not run through the summary calibration at the write, which can drop clauses. Review fix before release (owner: the field should reflect the story): the same write marks it (`relationshipSummarySource: 'line'`, `core/relationship-summary-source.js`), and a marked sentence is never compacted, calibrated or checked against the meters: both narrator blocks send it as written, past their per-field cuts (the whole injection budget still applies). A scan or Refresh that copies it back keeps it; any other writer of the summary (a Refresh or scan with new words, a reader edit, a pack entry, an alias merge or import) drops the mark, and the old checks apply to its text.
- **One mount per animal; mounts ridden by the party count as with you (FU150).** A mount's `where` that names the reader's character or a current member (full or first name, whole words; a title such as "Sister" is no first name), or lists only members, now travels with the party (`travelsWith`, `ridersOf`; names from `carry-parts/party-names.js`). The Bag, the Mount slot and `mounts.ride` use it at once; the carry limit for story item lines uses it only on replies with the new rule mark `mountRiders` (`gear.carry { riders: false }` otherwise). The roll guard (`gearMatches`) also accepts a load worked out with those mounts counted. The mount grammar asks for one line per animal or vehicle, `count:` only for a pack string that is never split (old lines with `count:` read as before). The party keeps up to 12 mount rows (was 6), by story line, `mounts.add` and the state check; a story `+ mount` line on a reply without `mountRiders` is still refused past 6, and the mark's line test now also covers `+ mount`. A bare list of names gives the members in it as riders and leaves the others out; changing riders on such a row rewrites its `where` as "ridden by" the members only. No split action: a reader's `mounts.add` is told to the narrator as the party getting a mount. With gear off the sheet shows only the row's own words, not "Not with you".

- **A second `+ xp` in a reply still counts its group down (FU144 round 2).** One `+ xp` pays a reply, and a later one was refused whole, so the group it named stayed standing (and, set up with no dice-step clock, never went stale). A later `+ xp` whose `group:` names a standing group that no earlier `+ xp` of the reply hit now counts it down (`left`, `fell`, touched) and pays nothing ("counted down; one xp line pays a reply"); one with no `group:`, or naming a group already hit or not standing, is still refused. New rule mark `xpCountsDown`. The narrator's `~ rest` grammar now says "not while a fight is still on" (was "not while foes stand"). A spaced hyphen (`~ rest Party - short`) already splits name from text in the shared parser; a test now pins it. New test: examples/text-rpg/tests/rest-owner.test.js.

- **Leftover foes no longer block a rest (owner, 26 Nov, FU144).** `~ rest` was refused while any foes group stood (groups only go stale after a day of story time), and nothing showed on the reply card. `~ rest` and `textRpg.rest` (which did not check foes at all before) are now refused only while a fight is still on: a standing group set up, reinforced or hit in this reply or the one before, or in a reply that began within the last 60 story minutes (`foes-fights.js blockingFoes`). It reads this from the stored `+ foes` and `+ xp` receipts (`+ xp` now lists `data.touched`), not from `group.at` or `slice.dice`, which only know replies that rolled; an `+ xp` receipt stored before this lists no groups. A `? try` whose `against:` names a standing group is a touch too (`capture.try` `data.touched`; read from the next reply on); a group the code picked for a roll with no `against:` (or a name that matched none) is not, and a `? try` receipt stored before this lists no groups. Leftover groups are not changed. A refused `~ rest` line is now a receipt that changes nothing (it was no receipt; its Problem's code is now `LIMITED`), and `reply.changes` shows any receipt's `data.card`, so both a rest and a refused rest are on the Changes tab. New rule mark `restCurrent`: a reply captured before it keeps the old behaviour (refused while any group stands, and every refused line fails with its old code and message, with no receipt). A record now keeps up to 64 rule marks (was 32; past the cap the last names were dropped with no error), and a test fails if there are more rules than that.

- **Someone who left and still holds things (FU143 round 3).** A holder the roster no longer names (Text RPG `! fall`, built-in `left: true`, a deleted join) adds no room (`picture.left`); their things stay in the load and `carry.people` shows "Name (left) n", named by `holderName` (the `party` domain, `party.actors`, `party.levels`, Text RPG's new `party.names`, else "Someone who left"), which the hand-over, drop, wear and bag summaries use too. `inventory.bag { stack: null }` now puts down the bag of someone who left, and the item sheet offers **Take back** (`inventory.transfer` to the party). The Bag's notes now say what the narrator is told ("The narrator treats … as slowed: −2 …"), name only the members who are over when any is (as the tail does), no longer claim travel pace −25% (nothing applies it) and are left out while Encumbered is off. New rule mark `carryRoster`: a Re-read of a reply captured before it judges its item lines by the old carry count (`gear.carry { legacy }`).

- **The Bag's load is the whole party's; each member's own load shows (owner, 26 Nov, FU143).** `gear.view`'s `carry.load`/`capacity`/`pct`/`level` were the shared bag's only, while the add check and the encumbrance rule read the whole party's totals and each member's own: a companion's things, strength and bag showed nowhere. They are now the totals; `parts`/`breakdown` add "Name carries n" and "Name's Bag +n" after the shared sources (`views-parts/member-load.js`), and `carry.people` lists those who hold things or a bag, drawn as a line each under the bar and as "Carrying n / m" on a companion's Party sheet. A member a roster query names (Text RPG) was called by their ID in `gear.carry` (the refusal and the encumbered tail line too): `carryPicture` takes `nameOf`, which changes names only, never a number. Round 2: `inventory.gear { owner: 'party' }` answers the same totals (a gear record made now carries `rule: 2`; one without it is still judged by the shared bag, and no current fight builds a record or applies a load penalty). Every member `party.actors` or the `party` domain names has room from the start, so a first hand-over is checked against their own limit; a member who is a Character block is counted once, with its strength (it was in the shared bag and again at the default); the main character is not a person (`carry-parts/members.js`). A note under the bar names a member over their own limit while the bar is not, and the reader's chip is named while members are listed. New tests: tests/modules/inventory/gear-member-load.test.js and gear-roster.test.js.

- **Paperwork weighs 0; Edit changes the slot of story and pack gear (owner, 26 Nov, FU142).** Goods or material whose name has a paperwork word (`GEAR.paperwork`, whole words, plurals too) weigh 0 by default; derived at read time, an own `weight` wins. `inventory.edit { stack, slot }` now works on any equipment none of whose pieces is a cast member's (`edit-parts/slot-edit.js`); kind, rarity and stats of story and pack pieces stay refused. `gear.view` rows carry `slotEdit`. The edit's replay witness (pieces and rename decision) is now read before the gear fields change anything, so a slot change that takes a worn piece off can be saved with a new name or count. Review fixes: the rename witness of a shared or pack definition is a copy, so slot plus new name saves; a Save that takes the piece off and merges it by rename is refused; a slot Edit's guard witnesses every piece of the definition (`slotPieces`; older guards unchanged); a slot change keeps the bonus (derived stats are written on the definition, or `noBonus`); the item sheet's Edit item shortcut shows for `slotEdit` pieces too; paperwork is matched on the name's head noun only.

- **Text RPG: Export carries the bag and the purse (owner, 24 Nov, FU133).** `textRpg.export` wrote `items: 0, money: 0` on purpose. It now reads `inventory.items` (owner `party` and the member's id for the reader's character, the member's id otherwise) and `inventory.wallet` (the reader's character only) through the host query the module gets, and writes the RPG file's `items` (name, qty, note, kind, rarity, a worn piece's slot and bonus, carry; same-row counts added up, 60 at most, qty 999) and `money` (1e9 at most) (`module-parts/export-things.js`, no RPG internals imported). The answer's `items` and `money` are the counts the Party tab's export dialog shows, and `inventory: false` / `money: false` leave them out of the text. New tests: examples/text-rpg/tests/export-things.test.js (round trip through Start from a saved character, the RPG's parser reads the items).
- **Text RPG: gear's sixth stat is WIS (owner, 24 Nov, FU131).** The gear's stats are [STR, VIT, AGI, DEX, INT, LUK, CHA], Text RPG's [str, vit, agi, dex, int, wis, cha], so a LUK point on a piece was lost under Text RPG. `gearOf` now counts the bonus text's stats by position (`GEAR_ORDER`), so LUK is wis in the pools, effective stats, sheet row, export and capability line. Labels: a new query `gear.statLabels` (seven names in the gear's order) is a fallback of the inventory (its own names) and answered by Text RPG while its feature is on, from the Stat names setting by position (`module-parts/stat-labels.js`). `ui/select/stat-labels.js` swaps the keys in the Bag and Party bonus text and the Bag view-model carries `gear.labels` and `repoint.labels` to the + Add form and the Change picker, which then always offers the sixth stat under its label (WIS). Stored data keeps the gear's keys; no prompt line shows gear bonuses by stat name, so the narrator text is unchanged. New tests: examples/text-rpg/tests/gear-wis.test.js, tests/ui/tabs/bag/gear-stat-labels.test.js.
- **Text RPG: every use teaches (owner, 24 Nov, FU129).** Obscure and Snap Cut rolled with `edge: +1` against vermin (easy became trivial) and got no practice. Now a trivial roll teaches 1 (`practiceGain`), `practiceFor` gives every use at least 1 even far beneath (×0 scale), and `? try` judges practice at the difficulty before an edge in the roller's favour (index plus positive edge steps, clamped; an edge against counts as rolled). Lessons are unchanged. Pinned tests changed: practice (scale, spend, roll), rules (practiceGain), try (trivial); new tests in try.test.js and foe-practice.test.js.

- **Text RPG foe ladder and battle XP caps (owner, 24 Nov, FU128).** The narrator wrote `+ foes Rats | count: 24 | threat: a fair match` and 24 rats paid a whole level: the size words (minor … great) and the threat words were two look-alike scales and the narrator took the generous one. Now the only scale it writes is the kind, `+ foes Name | count: N | kind: vermin|common|trained|elite|monster|legend`; the code rates it against the party's level (highest among members not fallen or Down; base 1..6 less 0/1/2/3 at levels 1–9/10–29/30–59/60+, kept 0..5), fixes the threat on the group when declared and uses it for the starting difficulty of tries, the matchup weight, XP and lessons. `? try … | against: Name` picks the group, else the strongest standing; with foes standing the narrator's difficulty word is ignored (the receipt says so), with none it counts as before. `+ xp Party — what [| foes: N] [| group: Name]` pays each fallen foe kind base (5/10/20/40/80/160) × threat multiplier, and a battle (a fight plus waves within 60 story minutes of it closing, or the reply right after with no clock) is capped by its strongest group's threat (0/25/50/75/100/200, counted before the XP rate); the receipt says "battle N of CAP" or "battle cap reached". Fled or ended groups pay nothing; with no foes record a roll that succeeded this reply pays 10, else 0. A reply's dice record keeps `won`, a fight's record `paid`, a group its `kind`. The Rolls card and the matchup line show "Rats: vermin, minor for you (24)". Old lines are not honoured (FU128B: the narrator copies the change lines of earlier replies, so reading them reopened the hole): `+ foes` with no (known) kind is refused ("+ foes needs a kind: vermin, common, trained, elite, monster or legend"), `threat:` on `? try` and `size:` on `+ xp` are ignored; a chat's earlier fights re-read under the kind rules, their kills paying the no-record 10 XP. New code: words/foe-kinds.js, rules/foe-ladder.js, module-parts/foe-pick.js, foes-pay.js. Pinned tests changed: foes (summaries), matchup (why texts), rolls (foes row and item line), tail, dice, host (note and grammar texts), narrator-rules (rules text, still at most 300 words), try (dice record has `won`); new tests foe-ladder, foe-battle, foe-try, foe-replay.

- **Text RPG rules redesign (owner, 23 Nov, FU127).** Roll = d20 + the skill's rank × 2 (Novice 0 … Legend 12); a `? try` naming a skill that is not on the sheet rolls −3, a plain stat try +0; stats, gear and level are no longer in the roll (the RPG check bonus is gone from `rules/roll.js`); −2 per injury hampering the try's stat and the conditions' floor stay; a companion rolls +2 for a named strength, else +0. Difficulty has six words (trivial 5, easy 10, ordinary 15, hard 20, very hard 25, near impossible 30; `normal` still reads as ordinary) and starts from the threat word (far beneath trivial … beyond near impossible; none named: the narrator's word); `| edge: +1 (reason)` / `-2 (reason)` moves it at most two steps, a missing reason ignores the edge (noted in the receipt); conditions and a drained body hold a floor (ordinary with the first); the tone (Standard the default, Gritty, Heroic) moves it a step; foe weights far beneath 0.1 and beneath 0.25 (owner, FU127C: Standard default, lighter swarms). Bands come from the margin alone; a natural 20 or 1 only marks the quality ("well done", "clumsy"), so the forced great/bad are gone. New `+ foes Name | count: N | threat: word` (up to 6 groups a reply; the same name in a later reply while some stand is reinforcements) and `- foes Name` keep a foes record in the domain (`foes.groups`, `foes.fights`; the remaining and total counts are counters); `+ xp … | foes: N [| group: Name]` pays only the foes standing in it and counts them down (no record: one foe at most; a reply with no roll pays and counts nothing). Each reply the code tells the narrator the matchup (foes weight Σ remaining × threat weight against the party's Σ health factor × stamina factor; favoured / even / outmatched / hopeless) in the tail with its rule, records it in the reply's dice step and shows it on the Rolls card with the foes row. Practice keeps +3/+5/+10 (trivial teaches nothing); new `+ lesson Who — Skill | what` teaches 5 × the fight's threat multiplier once a skill a member a fight, for a skill rolled in it, after all its groups are down or gone. Automatic gear wear (`kinds/wear.js`) is removed. The narrator rules are rewritten from the owner's dice rules (258 words; design §16). Pinned tests changed to the new numbers: rules, try, practice, train, train-sessions, train-clock, kinds, dice, tail, host, rolls, skill-cap, narrator-rules, paid-once, conditions, commands, domain, replay, xp-foes (the wear test was replaced by no-wear).

- **Text RPG rolls refused after the training update (owner's chat, 23 Nov).** FU123 kept the story clock in each reply's dice record (`dice[sid].clock`) but the domain validator did not know the field, so with the story clock running every `? try` was refused ("dice.<reply>.clock is not a known field") and `+ xp` paid 0 (no roll in the reply). validate.js accepts `clock` (whole minutes). The host test for training used `~ train` only, which never stores the record; a clocked host test now sends a `? try` and an `+ xp`. Tests: examples/text-rpg/tests/train-clock.test.js, dice-clock-valid.test.js.

- **Text RPG: rolls you can see, practice per use, gentler wear, why a roll was raised (owner, first battle).** One "Rolls" card under the reply (`text-rpg-rolls`, a row for each roll and an Earned row, up to 8 rows by the card's own `rows: 8` declaration, read from the reply's own receipts through `textRpg.rolls`) lists each roll with its die, bonus and number, the reasons it was raised, its cost, its practice and rank up, its wear, then the reply's xp and `~ spend` practice. Practice: a `~ spend` +5 once a skill a reply (a roll for it in the reply wins), a `? try` +10 or +20, all scaled by the threat word (far beneath ×0 … far above ×2); `spent` joins the domain slice for the once-a-reply mark. Wear: a bad failure in danger wears each used piece one condition step (a board exchange's wear), a success at a cost wears nothing. A module card may declare `rows: n` (5–8; built-in cards keep 4 rows of 120 characters, a declared card's rows say up to 200). The practice mark stores what a spend really added (capped), and the card matches a spend to a roll by the skill key. A raised try's receipt (`why`), its summary and the dice note's floor line name the threat and live conditions that raised it.
- **RPG blocks quiet while a feature replaces the RPG (owner, 23 Nov: "foes still function the same?").** A chat converted from the RPG kept teaching the RPG data blocks' lines (Character set-health/rest/heal, Skills learn/practice, Foes set-slot/clear/horde, the Combat rules) beside Text RPG's. With the RPG off and a replacing feature on, blocks with the roles character/skills/foes/rules teach nothing, give no tail, and a story line for them is a quiet FEATURE_OFF; the `! dm` kind stays enabled so a Re-read of a reply kept under the RPG still replays (modules/data-modules/part/replaced.js, factory.js, act.js, views-parts/grammar.js, prompt-parts/summary.js; core/runtime-parts/create.js `rpgReplaced`). Reviews REV-FU116-A/B, REV-FU116C. Tests: tests/modules/data-modules/rpg-replaced.test.js.
- **Text RPG skill practice, the owner's numbers (FU122, owner 23 Nov).** Gains: +1 for `~ spend` or the new `~ train Who — Skill` (a training scene: no cost, no roll; one gain a skill a reply between them, and a roll of the same skill in the reply wins), +3 a failed roll (failure or bad), +5 a success or a success at a cost, +10 a success or great at hard or harder (none when easy); the threat scaling is as before, no foe-count scaling. Ranks start at practice 0, 40, 100, 180, 280, 420, 600 (Novice … Legend), no level gates: `words/ranks.js` gives each rank its `at` (the old `PRACTICE_PER_RANK` is gone) and the rank bonus, the sheet's next-rank `from`/`at`, the capability words, the card's rank-up text, the wizard's file import (a rank → the practice it starts at) and export (practice → rank) read it. The grammar line `~ train Who — Skill (a training scene: practice, no roll)` is in the kinds' static text. Tests: examples/text-rpg/tests/train.test.js, practice.test.js, rules.test.js, words.test.js.
- **Text RPG pools (owner, 23 Nov).** MP = 8 + level÷4 + (INT+WIS)÷4 and SP = 8 + level÷4 + (AGI+DEX)÷4 (adapted from modules/rpg/combat/bars.js), from the effective stats with worn gear capped by rarity, read (never stored) so level roll-backs and unequipping move the size and `now` reads clamped. Text RPG sheet rows carry their own `bonus` (capped) and the Party view-model uses it instead of the Bag's (ui/select/party.js). Review REV-FU118. Tests: examples/text-rpg/tests/pools-level.test.js, pools-gear.test.js, tests/ui/select/party.test.js.

- **Text RPG wizard as the RPG's (owner, 23 Nov).** Stats start at 5 with 10 points, 10 at most (the RPG's createPoints/Min/Max; total 45); the Who step takes the persona's name (new core query `persona.name`) and a starting level (the level's points are placed in the wizard and kept as `granted`; no rank text while the RPG is off: `rpg.rank` is gated with it); "Start from a saved character" reads RPG and Text RPG character files (stats held to 5..15, types mapped, ranks as practice; inventory and money added after create through `inventory.add` / `wallet.set`); Export for Text RPG rows (`commands.export` row seam → `party.export` asks the named query; `textRpg.export` writes the RPG file shape plus `textRpg` extras, points clamped to 60). Levels are 100 XP each up to 99 (xp held to 9999); skill slots min(8, 5 + floor(level/10)); Level cap and XP rate settings recorded with the reply's dice step and read back by `+ xp` only from that record. Old members over 8 skills still validate (stored limit 38) but cannot learn more. Old saved XP keeps its number (150 was level 3, is now level 2). Reviews REV-INT4-A/B; shots scratch/shots/fu113.

- **Start page after a replace (owner, 23 Nov).** The Settings window drew Start from the switches it read when it opened; a plug-in page's `replaces` write left them stale, so Text RPG still read on. ui/settings/page.js reads the switches again when the reader moves to another page (a redraw of the same page keeps them; Reset reads them itself); the plug-in card's "(built-in)" sub is removed (the Rules page's Instead row stays). Rendered check scratch/shots/fu114. Tests: tests/ui/settings/plugin-instead.test.js.

- **`persona.portrait` query (owner, 23 Nov).** core/persona-portrait.js reads the reader's persona avatar as ui/wizard/world.js does (user_avatar, else the one avatar whose persona name is name1), checked by isSafePortraitUrl; a query of the host module only (no state, no replay). Text RPG's wizard summary draws it (wz-pic). Review REV-FU112 (follow-up: core imports the URL check from ui/kit; move it to core). Tests: tests/core/persona-portrait.test.js, examples/text-rpg/tests/wizard.test.js.

- **Text RPG follow-ups (owner, 23 Nov).** New character: a copy of ui/wizard (who, stats, skills, review) in examples/text-rpg/src/wizard and src/kit, adapted to its rules (one RULES for the wizard and `textRpg.create`). A plug-in's Rules page opens with "Instead of this plug-in" switches for the features it `replaces`, and its Start card lists them (ui/select/replaced.js); the Settings side list refreshes after such a switch. Text RPG answers `party.pc` (plain query) so the Bag and the shared bag follow its character. The RPG switch itself was never missing: it is the last row of the Story Block card's Features list. Guide: docs/dev/writing-a-module.md and docs/dev/ai-readme/plugin.md (AI-README part). Reviews REV-INT3-A/B; rendered checks scratch/shots/fu108, fu109, fu111.

- **Text RPG playable in the real view (owner, 23 Nov: no Party tab, no RPG toggle, no gear, no icon; wear and an editable combat prompt asked for).** The Party tab shows while `party.newCharacter` names a screen (ui/select/build.js flags). Plug-in screens open in the view's shared sheet (core/boot/screen-source.js as `StoryBlock.screens`, plug-in modules only; ui/wizard/plugin-screen.js, attach.js). Gear, carry and slots follow `registry.featureOrReplacementOn('story-block.rpg')` (core/runtime-parts/create.js gearOn/marked, modules/inventory). The inventory's `gear.wear` also accepts a caller whose on feature replaces the RPG (`ctx.callerReplaces`); Text RPG wears the weapon (physical tries) or the armour (wounds) on a bad failure (2 steps) or a cost (1 step) in danger, recorded with the reply. Plug-in Rules pages carry the Bag and gear switches for a module replacing the RPG, a long-text `prompt` row (Text RPG's Narrator rules, ≤ 2,000 characters, Reset to default; '' = the built-in text), and the module's `icon` (definition key, `fa-…`) on its Start card and in the Rules menu. Feature `replaces` cascades only when the replacer is really on, before listeners hear it; plug-in setting writes are type-checked (text up to the field's max, else 200); plug-in page ids are `plugin-<id>`. The Party sheet hides the soak chip without soak and shows the module's `xpLabel`. Fishing's widget is `fishing-activity.fishing`. Reviews REV-FU106, REV-FU107-A/B; rendered checks in scratch/shots/fu107, fu107b, fu107d. Tests: tests/core/features-replaces, tests/ui/wizard/plugin-screen, tests/ui/settings/plugin-*, tests/ui/tabs/party/plugin-sheet, tests/modules/inventory/gear-wear-replacer, examples/text-rpg/tests (wear, narrator-rules).

- **Text RPG: purchases and skills follow the level (owner, 23 Nov: "take it back automatically, it should just roll back to the previous turn state").** Stat points are an ordered keyed record `bought` (ctx.id, seq); effective stats = base + the purchases the current level pays for in seq order (one that does not fit is skipped), points = earned − counted; pools read from effective stats and costs are taken from the clamped value. Skills learned past the level's slots (5 + floor(level/3), up to 38 at level 99; creation skills always count) are inactive: `? try` refuses them. The narrator's line and dice table list the 8 best active skills plus any named in the last message or reply. Reviews REV-TRSB, REV-TRSB2. Tests: examples/text-rpg/tests (replay, spend-back, skill-cap).
- **A feature may replace others; plug-in Rules pages.** A feature declaration may carry `replaces: [ids]` (≤ 4); switching it on switches those off and switching a replaced one on switches its replacers off (core/features-parts/store.js `set`). Text RPG replaces `story-block.rpg`. A non-built-in module whose settings have a `section` and a feature on gets a Rules page (ui/select/plugin-pages.js; writes through ui/port/plugin-settings.js). The RPG switch itself stays in the Story Block card's Features list. Reviews REV-TRUIS-A/B. Tests: tests/core/features-replaces.test.js, tests/ui/settings/plugin-page.test.js.

- **Text RPG example module (owner, 23 Nov).** examples/text-rpg: a separate SillyTavern extension using only the public sdk (the second reference module after fishing). A narrated RPG with no fight board: stats, skills, injuries, conditions and gear told to the narrator as words (stat, rank, pool and threat tables); three d20 rolled before each reply from its seed and reported with `? try Who | skill or stat | difficulty`; the code works the band (success, at a cost, failure, bad, great) from the die, the bonus and the difficulty raised to a floor (threat, numbers, drained stamina, conditions) and shifted by the tone preset; wounds as a stage counter with Down only from `~ health`; mana and stamina as numbers; practice per skill keyed; level and points derived from XP and points spent; an XP guard (size cut by the hardest roll); healing over days; gear bonuses capped by rarity. It answers the Party tab through fallback `rpg.sheet` / `party.levels` and plain `party.actors` / `party.newCharacter` queries while the RPG feature is off. Design: scratch/design/text-rpg.md; reviews REV-TRSEAMS-A/B, REV-TRINT-A/B/C/D. Tests: examples/text-rpg/tests (170).
- **Seams for a plug-in sheet module.** ui/port/aliases.js and ui/tabs/party/skills.js send a sheet row's own `commands.spend` / `commands.skillEdit` (names checked against /^[A-Za-z][\w.-]{0,79}$/); the Party tab's Create character opens the screen `party.newCharacter` names; modules/director/module/create.js sets `env.textRpg` (feature `text-rpg.on` on and `story-block.rpg` off) and modules/rpg/checks/capture.js withholds the `? check` grammar then, so one dice system is taught. RPG chats are unchanged (static prompt golden untouched). Tests: tests/ui/select/party-seams, tests/ui/tabs/party/party-seams, tests/ui/port/aliases, tests/modules/rpg/checks/capture.

- **The pre-check's foe name may differ by a short ending (owner, 23 Nov: "6 hound size rats … I attack them" opened no fight board).** The pre-check answered `{"groups":[{"name":"Hound-sized river-rats","count":6,"level":2,"tier":"minion"}]}`, and `vetGroups` refused it: `namedInMessage` (modules/rpg/board/precheck-parts/shared-words.js) wants every distinctive word of the name in the message or the reply before, and "sized" was not there ("size" was), so the send went out with no board. Two words now match when one is the other plus an ending of at most three letters, both at least four letters long (size/sized, hound/hounds, swarm/swarming); a different word that only starts the same (Ratcatchers for rats, Houndmasters for hound) is still refused, so the model still cannot add a foe the message and the reply never named. The horde leader's name check uses the same rule. Tests: tests/modules/rpg/board/precheck.test.js.

- **A new place the story tells the way to is put on the map there (owner, 22 Nov: "the direction to a new place should be post narrative: the narrative says how far and which way, then it gets saved, then follows the rule of named places").** The route line takes `| way: north` (where the second place lies from the first, "only when the story says the way"; `| km:` optional) and the landmark line `| at: 12 km east of Place` (as the crow flies, "only when the story says how far and which way"); the atlas grammar names both (static prompt golden regenerated: one line). capture-parts/told-point.js places the new end from the other: the route's km (else its time: pre-modern a day's pace by kind, foot 20, river 35, sea 110 km, an hour a fifth of a day; on a modern map km an hour by kind, road by car 70, rail 90, river ferry 30, sea ship 35, trail on foot 5, a day eight hours), at most 5,000 km (more, or a number that is not one, leaves the place unplaced) less the road factor, at the pack's road scale (where.js `kmScale`), turned by the reader's north/east setting, rounded to 0.001 unit, with no `auto` mark (it is the story's point, so the map never moves it beside a parent). Only a place with no point the code vouches for is placed (no point, or the map's guess), never a pack place, and only from a place whose point is vouched for; a way word the code cannot read (`sideways`) leaves the route as it was. The point is worked out when the line is read and stored, so a reload, a middle delete and a rebuild replay it; a swipe or Re-read reads the line again under the map settings of that moment, as a trip's plan does, and a told point whose anchor's reply is deleted keeps its point (reviews REV-102A/B, accepted). The view's `chat.lastReply` is the story without its change block (stripBlock), so a reply's own lines ("~ trip Wolfmere Outpost") do not count as places it names. From then on the place is like any vouched-for place: its direction in the route line and "Where places lie" comes from the code. Tests: tests/modules/atlas/told-point.test.js.

- **"Where places lie" line (owner, 22 Nov, after the Bluewatch "east road": "if they have coordinates, that's enough to determine the direction").** The atlas's tail gains a second optional line after the routes, with its own 100-token room (PLACES_TAIL_BUDGET, note-parts/places-line.js): "Where places lie from Bluewatch Village, as the crow flies: Shackles City about 1,650 km south-west · Auvoth about 50 km west · Shorn about 210 km south-west · …". First the places the reader's message and the last reply name (whole words written with their capital, so "we rest" is not the village Rest; an other name two places share names neither; a town's parts not looked for; one on another plane says so: "Deepwalker Hall (underground)"), then the nearest city or capital in each of the eight directions on the scene's plane, nearest first. Hidden places are never told; a place within a kilometre (the same town) is left out. Distances are straight lines rounded as a traveller says them (1 km under 20, 5 under 100, 10 under 1,000, then 50), at the scale the pack's roads give (median road km ÷ straight distance, less the road factor: where.js `kmScale`, the Map's `roadScale` moved there); without three measured roads the reader's Km per map unit when it is not the default 1, else no km at all (directions only). The view gives modules `chat.lastReply` (core/sdk/queries-parts/view.js): the narrator's message before the reader's last one. Only points the code can vouch for give a compass word (where.js `trustedPoint`): a pack's, the reader's, one the story set with coordinates, or one the map set beside a town that is itself vouched for; a point the map guessed (the middle of a region, or beside the last place) gives none, in the route line too (drop 100 gave one). Tests: tests/modules/atlas/places-line.test.js, tests/modules/atlas/note.test.js, tests/core/sdk.test.js.

- **Roads name their direction in the narrator's route line (owner, 22 Nov: "east road from Auvoth, although it's on the west").** In the owner's Bluewatch chat the narrator called the road to Auvoth "the east road" and put the convoy trouble at Bluewatch's east gate; Auvoth (59, 110) lies west of Bluewatch Village (64, 110). The tail line "Routes from …" (modules/atlas/atlas/note-parts/tail.js `routesLine`) listed each road's far end, kind and time but no direction, and the only direction in the lore ("on the frontier road east of Auvoth") is from Auvoth's side. Each item now carries the compass word from the two places' coordinates (`bearing`, places-parts/geometry.js, eight points, y growing north, following the reader's Map & travel north/east setting): "Auvoth (west) by frontier road, …". A place without coordinates (one the story made) gets none. The line keeps its 80-token budget (far roads drop first, as before). Tests: tests/modules/atlas/note.test.js, tests/modules/atlas/commands.test.js.

- **Soak takes off only whole points (owner, 22 Nov: "is this roll a little off? and damage?", Boars 2 → Astra 3 damage through 6% soak dealt 2).** `takeHit` (modules/rpg/combat/damage.js) rounded the damage left down, `⌊raw × (100 − soak) ÷ 100⌋`, so any soak took at least a point off a small hit (3 at 6% was 2). With the new fight mark `soakWhole: 1` (set by v1-kind.js on every fight opened from now; play.js and exchange.js pass it to the foe's hit) it is `raw − ⌊raw × soak ÷ 100⌋`: soak takes off only the whole points it adds up to (3 at 6% stays 3; 6% takes one from 17 damage); the minimum of one and the 50 cap are unchanged. A fight opened before keeps the old rounding, so replay equals live. Board fixtures whose fixed dice now end differently were given more HP, AGI 30 or level-5 foes (v1-kind, v1-lifecycle, v1-horde-size, v1-soak). The guide's mark table lists `soakWhole`. Tests: tests/modules/rpg/combat/soak-whole.test.js, tests/scripts/ai-readme-examples.test.js.

- **A new person's fill-in keeps their live state (owner's chat 22 Nov, Nolven Pye).** The fill-in of a `+ npc` stub (reason new-person) was queued without `preserveLiveState`, so the backfill model's row (`present: true`, mood "unmoved") went into the merge: Nolven, met at the Stray Oar on the way and given `where: the Stray Oar` and mood shrewd by his line, showed as present at the Guild office while the scene list (Astra, Rorke, Dora) was right. `queueFills` (npc-lines-parts/fill-in.js) now asks for `preserveLiveState: true`, as the missed-participant and new-admission repairs do: presence, place, mood, status and goal stay the scene's and the lines'; the fill-in writes the profile only. A fill-in's `lastSeenTurn` is the reply's turn (they were in it), so someone met away from the scene does not turn dormant early (runtime-targeted-backfill.js). Tests: tests/people/fill-in-stubs.test.js, tests/people/npc-lines-derived.test.js.

- **Two different events of one reply that share a few words stay two (REV-dfx2b F1).** `sameReplyRetelling` (core-record-merge-parts/memories.js) meant to leave names out of the shared words but compared the raw lowercase name with the tokenizer's stems, so a name ending in s ("Hollis" → "holli") still counted; names now go through `durableRefinementTokens` too. It takes `{ minShared }` (3 by default, the scan merge's second pass unchanged), and `saveMemory` (npc-lines-parts/memory.js) asks for 4: a reply's own lines are written on purpose, so "Pulled Hollis Drane out from under the wrecked charcoal wagon…" and "Watched Hollis Drane sell the wrecked charcoal wagon…" (3 shared) are both kept, while Rorke's Delivered/Listened report (5 shared) is still one memory. Test: tests/people/memory-same-reply.test.js.

- **One `memory:` a person a reply; `memory:` on `+ npc` (DOSSFIX2 item 6; REV-dossa F4, F5).** A `memory:` label's memory is now stamped with the reply's sid as `l` (kept by `normalizeMemoryStamps`, like `~ memory`'s `s`, so the runtime's rewrite and a Continue keep it); a second `memory:` for the same person in the same reply is not stored and the line gets the problem "Name: one memory: a reply; this one was not stored." (NPC State's problems, the receipt and its data). It is untagged as before, so the two-a-reply `~ memory` cap is not spent, and why: memories are not limited. A `+ npc Name | role | look | memory: …` line's `memory:` part is no longer read as part of the look (`readStub` returns it as `memory`) and is saved as the new person's first memory (`withMemories` → `wordsOf`), with the same rules; lines off, unchanged. Test: tests/people/memory-label-limits.test.js.

- **Retention requests match memories by `memorySimilarity` (DOSSFIX2 item 5; REV-dossa F3).** The last two memory sites on the old shared-cluster rule, `selectCanonical` (core-record-merge-parts/memories.js) and `chooseOrdinary` (memory-fact-store-parts/merge.js), named a scan's retention request's memory with `durableSemanticSimilarity` (≥ 0.56), so a request for Colvin counted the granary memory (0.62) as requested and kept it over a newer one. Both now use `memorySimilarity` at the same threshold; an exact match is unchanged. Only which memory keeps a slot changes, never a merge. Test: tests/people/memory-retention-request.test.js.

- **A same-reply retelling is one memory (DOSSFIX2 item 4; REV-dossa F1).** With `memorySimilarity` (DOSSFRESH) a why: and a memory: (or a `~ memory`) telling one event in other words scored apart (Rorke #19 of the owner's 20 Nov chat: "Delivered a steady … report on … wrecked charcoal wagon" and "Listened to Astra's steady report on … wrecked charcoal wagon …", 0.43) and both were stored, so the read-time cap of five could push out an older, distinct memory. `saveMemory` (npc-lines-parts/memory.js) now also refuses a memory that `sameReplyRetelling` (core-record-merge-parts/memories.js, the scan merge's rule: three or more distinctive shared words, a quarter of the shorter, never a hand-written one) finds told already by this reply's own line memories; the first stays. This reply's memories are found by its sid, not its index (an index is taken again after a middle delete): every line memory's stamp keeps the sid (`s` for `~ memory`, `l` for `memory:`, `r` for any other), and `normalizeMemoryStamps` keeps `r`. Memories of different replies are never compared by it, so Rorke's #8 and #14 stay two. A refused `~ memory` reads "already stored", as a repeat did. Test: tests/people/memory-same-reply.test.js.

- **Every line field moves the dossier's last change (DOSSFIX2 item 3; REV-dossb N2, REV-dossa 6).** `stampChanged` (domain/change-stamp.js) maps each line label to the record key it writes (learns → abilities, keepsake → keepsakes, aka → aliases) and stamps the tracked fields plus the line-only ones, `LINE_STAMPED_FIELDS` (core-birthday-parts/field-change-normalize.js: gender, level, abilities, keepsakes, aliases, knows); `normalizeFieldChangeMessages` keeps those keys, while `fieldChanges` and `fieldChangeDays` (Refresh's turn and day counts) stay the tracked fields only, so Refresh is unchanged. A `~ know` line that changed the person's knowledge (stored on their meter record) calls the new module-only `people.stamp { id, fields }` to stamp `knows` on their dossier when it has stamps. A line that changes nothing (the same alias, a lower Level) stamps nothing. Not stamped: `birthday:` (the dossier does not show a birthday; whether it should is the owner's call). Tests: tests/people/line-stamp-coverage.test.js; tests/modules/scene/domains/module.test.js lists the new command.

- **Stub people get the change stamps container (DOSSFIX2 item 2; REV-dossb N1).** A person a `+ npc` line made (or a pack person's dossier a line made, cast-first) and no scan touched had no `fieldChangeMessages`, and a line never makes it (change-stamp.js), so their line writes never moved the dossier's last change. `stubRecord` (npc-lines-parts/derived.js) now starts the record with an empty `fieldChangeMessages` (cast-first puts it back after its normalizer drops it), made in the same op as the person (it lives and dies with them), and the lines-mode keep step (`keep-containers.js`) restores it, like `memoryStamps`, when the runtime's normalizer drops the empty container before its write. Lines still never make the container, so a scan after a line still survives a middle delete of the line's reply. Stamps keep the reply index they were written at (a middle delete does not renumber them), as before. Test: tests/people/stub-change-stamps.test.js.

- **A middle delete of the reply that first made a person's meter record (DOSSFIX2 item 1; REV-dossb pre-existing, REV-dossa F2).** NPC State's first change for a person (any labelled `~ npc` line, a meter line, a scan's move, a cast meeting) makes `people.bonds.npcs.<id>` whole; every later change is a leaf under it. Deleting that first reply made every later block for that person fail on replay (`…/turn/at does not exist`) and be skipped whole, goal, meters and memories with it. New replay rule (i), `rules:i` (SPEC §2.2, REPLAY.md "Keyed holders", `core/ops/holders.js`): a domain may name `holders` (`{ 'bonds/npcs': make(key, slice) }`); on replay a write under an entry that is not there first finds it remade by the maker, and every replayed op inside an entry is fitted as a scratch field's is (objects on the way made, a list for an append, a replace of something absent an add, a remove of something absent passed over); ops that fit replay exactly as stored and live commands are never touched. The people's maker (`modules/people/domain/bond-holder.js`) gives what a first change makes, from the slice alone: the dossier's name, its relationship numbers as the built-in meters, empty stages, ladder and milestones; no dossier, no remake. The store gets the makers through `createStore({ holders })` (runtime, the matrix state check, the fuzzers). Meter numbers are stored as set, so after the delete the remade record holds the later reply's number (as after any middle delete); a Re-read of a later reply tells it on the state after the delete. Stored chats heal on the next rebuild; nothing new is written. Tests: tests/core/replay-holders.test.js, tests/people/bond-middle-delete.test.js; tests/core/prefix-memo.test.js now pins `rules:i`.

- **Dossier freshness (DOSSFRESH; owner's chat 21 Nov, Dora and Rorke after they left and came back).** Traced on the export: the dossier view model matched the store head at every reply (#8 to #16) and the open dossier redraws on each reply's port events; what lagged was the dossier's last change (`lastReply`), because a line's `people.fields` write never stamped `fieldChangeMessages` (only a scan's merge did), and a person with no meter line wrote no memory. Now `people.fields` and `people.memory` stamp `fieldChangeMessages[field]` with the reply's index for each tracked field they change, into a person's existing stamps only (NPC State's merge makes the container; a line never does, so a later scan's plain stamp writes never depend on a line's reply) (`modules/people/domain/change-stamp.js`; the ops are marked `ctx.ifThere` inside the stamps, so a middle delete of the reply that made the container passes the stamp over and keeps the rest of the block); `fieldChanges` (NPC State's turn counter, read by Refresh) is unchanged. New `memory:` label on a `~ npc` line (lines mode; owner-approved prompt sentence after the mood sentence): stored as a why: is (same cleaning, limit, stamp and duplicate check; not counted against the two `~ memory` lines a reply; never for someone who has died). Memories are now told apart by `memorySimilarity` (the durable similarity without its shared-cluster floor) in the stored-list rule, the scan merge, the scan row and the stamp inheritance: Rorke's #14 memory no longer replaces her #8 one. Goldens regenerated: `tests/suite/static-prompt.golden.json`, `tests/people/fixtures/prompt-lines.golden.json` (the one sentence). Tests: tests/people/memory-distinct-events.test.js, tests/people/npc-lines-memory-field.test.js.

- **Sealed history (RETBUILD; retention design C, owner answers 21 Nov 09:47).** Host setting `sealHistory` (bool, on). After a reply or when a chat is opened, at a quiet moment (no generation, hold, open interaction or pending save), at most once per 25 replies, the replies older than the last 50 are sealed (`core/store/write/seal.js`): v3 records with ids, marks and receipts (display data and `data.v2` kept; `seed`/`replan` dropped) and no ops, spans or checkpoints; the state after the last of them is the chat's base (`chat_metadata.storySuite.base`, with the chain/bare/spine hashes at the edge so no hash in the window changes). A seal is written only when the chat rebuilt from the base reads the same (head, Problems, replan point, proofs, receipts, untold, window hashes, kept checkpoints); else nothing changes and a quiet Problems row says so. A sealed reply deleted or edited keeps its changes (the reader is told); Re-read and Return in time past the edge are refused; a SillyTavern branch cut before the edge opens read-only with an explanation; older builds open a sealed chat read-only. Settings › Tools › Story history shows the edge and the switch. One-time clean-up of the old pre-merge NPC State files: `node scripts/people/old-files.mjs <user files folder>` (SillyTavern cannot list the folder from the page). Behaviour change: after a guard owner's version changes, sealed replies keep the outcome they had (replay goes back to the base, not the greeting). Tests: tests/core/seal*.test.js, sealed-*.test.js, seal-edge.test.js, tests/suite/qa/seal-fuzz.test.js, tests/scripts/old-npc-files.test.js.
- **Sealed history review fixes (RETFIX: REV-retb, REV-reta).** The base keeps the chat id it was made in (`base.chat`): a sealed reply lost counts as deleted (`drift`) only when the chat id and SillyTavern's `integrity` both match, so a branch or checkpoint cut before the edge on SillyTavern before 1.19 (which copied the parent's `integrity`) opens read-only instead of taking the parent's later state; a renamed file takes its new id while it reads `ok`. A chat with sealed replies and no base is found from the first message that holds a record (a system note in front no longer hides it). A sealed reply deleted asks for a save. The open-time seal is armed after the chat's modules load. The Tools card says tidied replies stay tidied when the switch is off; "reaches 75" replaces "passes 75". The old NPC State file clean-up prints each file's date and flags files changed after the merge (`--since <date>` to move the line). Tests: tests/core/sealed-chain.test.js, seal-edge.test.js, tests/scripts/old-npc-files.test.js.

- **Key people follow-ups (KEYPEOPLE; the three parts the 21 Nov TODO listed).** Settings → RPG: `rpgAwayWait` "Companions who are away wait" (bool, off) and `rpgAwayXp` "XP for companions who wait" (0 to 100 %, 0). `rpg.sheet` rows now carry `away: { where, lastSeen, short }` from `director.away.view` (`modules/rpg/sheet-away.js`; null without the Director); the Party tab shows it only while the switch is on (`ui/select/party-away.js`), and the sheet note no longer ends in "…". With the switch on, a fight's open (`board/v1-kind.js`) records the standing companions who have an open absence in the `away` domain as `setup.waits: [{ id, label, level }]` (never everyone: if all would wait, nobody does) and `setup.awayXp`; `board/v1-away.js` takes them off the board input, and `board/v1-award.js` pays each a won fight's XP at their own level times that percent (at least 1). Recorded in the setup, so a swipe, a middle delete, a Re-read, a rewind and a reload settle the same fight (tests `v1-away.test.js`). A fight opened with the switch off is unchanged.

- **Journal witnesses (WITNESSES; owner yes 11 Nov).** Switch `story-director.witnesses` (default off). The per-line witnesses were already in the day digests (`who`, the scene at each receipt) and in the narrator's [Earlier days] block (unchanged, no prompt change, no golden change). New: query `journal.witnesses` (`modules/world/world/journal-parts/witnesses.js`; `{ on, days }`, or with `{ name }` the lines that person was there for, newest first, at most 8), derived on read from `knownDay` (hidden people stay out; nothing stored; a day stored before `who` has none). The Journal day panel shows "with A, B, C +N" under a line (`happenedWho`, optional contract field); a dossier's Past appends those lines (date, no reply: the digest keeps none) after NPC State's memories, skipping any day on which NPC State stamped a memory.
- **Encounters, engine milestone (switch `story-director.encounters`, default off; design and mock scratch/design/encounters/mock.html; the note wording is proposed and needs the owner's approval).** New part `modules/world/encounters/` in Story Director's parts after the pulse. Before a reply, the prelude `director.encounter { at }` builds the place's table (quiet weighted by the place's engine class; the pack's backdrop rows, proposed `content.backdrops` with `at`/`kinds`/`when`/`weight`/`likelier_if`/`less_if`/`recurring`, read through `packs.content` and empty until the pack kind is approved; built-in setting-agnostic backdrops by place group; met, living cast here and not in the scene), rolls it with one seeded draw (World events' campaign seed or a fixed one, the story day, the place and part of the day) and records the result in `director.encounters` (results, sightings of recurring backdrop people, promotions; validated in the director validator). At most one encounter a place and part of a day; none with no clock or place, in a fight, beside the pulse's chance meeting or arrival for the same reply, or beside the cast's meeting. The line `Encounter this reply: …` rides the reply's receipt (a prelude: previewed for the prompt, stored with the reply), so replay applies the record. After the reply's lines, `director.encounter.promote` binds a recurring backdrop person the reply names to the one NPC State dossier whose role fits (made by the narrator's `+ npc` line); later rolls and lines use the person, and a swipe, Re-read or rewind of that reply takes the binding back. Tests: tests/modules/world/encounters (engine, command, host replay and promotion). Off: nothing runs, nothing is stored, the static prompt is unchanged.
- **World events part 2, "the Director directs" (WORLDEV2, 21 Nov; all behind the world-events switch, default off).**
  - Story world lines judged (new chronicle setting `chronicle.judge`, default off): a new entry from the story's world line is marked `judge: { state: 'pending', key, conditions }` (key: hash of its words, place, factions, start; at most two a reply) and is heard as rumour everywhere until judged (`world.heardHere`). The arc part's hooks (`modules/arc/judge/world-hooks.js`) ask in the background after the reply (`world-ask.js`: one batched Quick call for up to 4 entries, prompt `world-prompt.js`, the plan judge's reader and secret scrub reused); the verdicts are stored before the next reply by the internal command `chronicle.world.judged` (`modules/world/chronicle/world-judge/judged.js`): accept → fact; reshape → read again by code (same name, a place `atlas.place` knows) else the line is kept; refuse → the entry is resolved, heard nowhere, and the conditions its line set (still by the narrator, since its start) are lifted with `by: 'director'`. A timeout is asked once more; a failure or unreadable answer is stored as `kept`; busy/aborted/no model is asked again later. Calls count in `chronicle.directorCalls` (ledger `judged`).
  - Tomorrow's roll judged: the daily step reads stored marks `ledger.marks["<event>|<day>"]` (`modules/world/daily/director-mark.js`): `veto` discards that day's roll (stays eligible), `weight` 0.5-2 scales the odds; never set-in-motion. The arc hooks dry-run tomorrow's step (`world-judge/look-ahead.js`) and ask once a story day (`roll-ask.js`, `roll-prompt.js`): with `chronicle.judge` the likely/possible events the roll will fire (accept / refuse with a reason), with the new arc setting `arc.dailyPass` (default off) every eligible one (also likelier ×2 / less likely ×0.5). Stored by the same command.
  - Arc plan `EVENT:` lines: `EVENT: Title — what | where: | on: | because:` read only while the plan reader is given the world (`plan/event-world.js`, null with the switch off); the place must be one the map knows, each cause a record the world holds (world event, condition, Journal entry, tracked thread); kept as a BEAT whose words end "(because: …)", so the plan judge judges it and accept reads it as a beat. At most two a plan. Taught in the world brief (`EVENT_LINE_RULE`).
  - WORLDEV finding 1: the readers (`chronicle.conditions` and its scope walk, the arc brief, the news routes) count an event hold only while the step's state says the event is Active (`conditions/live-holds.js`).
  - New model-facing wording (judge system texts, the EVENT rule) awaits the owner's approval; all of it is sent only with the switch and the settings on.
- **Horde fight board (HORDE; owner answers 21 Nov 09:47 to scratch/batch/SMALL/night/HORDEPLAN.REPORT.md).** `modules/rpg/combat/horde.js` (new: `hordeOf`, `hordeStart`, `refill`, `leaderDown`) holds a horde's rules; `setup.horde = { v: 1, reserve: [{ name, count, level, tier, leader? }], hold?, leaderSlot? }` is the setup mark (a fight without it plays byte for byte as before). `exchange.js`: the clear win waits for the reserve (`end: 'cleared'`), a fallen leader ends it after the party phase (`'leader'`), a held gate after the exchange (`'held'`), else `refill` at the exchange's end (no dice; arrivals `<slot>.<wave>.<n>`, appended to `order.foes`, line `['arrive', wave, slot, name, count, level, tier, left]`). `play.js` carries `{ next, wave, leader }` in `start.horde` (checked), `record.js` keeps `horde` and `end`, `input.js` carries arrivals of a segment. Openers: `board/horde-build.js` (`wordsOf`, `hordeFrom`: the front copied `waves - 1` times within 48 reserve / 60 fight foes / 12 groups, leader as a boss last or the named front group) used by `v1-kind.js openV1` once at open, from the pre-check's words (`precheck-parts/prompt.js HORDE_LINE`, `parse.js parseHorde`, `runPrecheck({ withHorde })`, leader only when named in the message, `opening.js` records the words, `view.js open` passes them) or the narrator's Foes `kind: horde` line (`templates-parts/v1-foes.js`: fields `waves`, `hold`, `leader`; every `clear-N` forgets them; `board/horde-block.js`; `data-modules/board-static.js` offers the kind in the static grammar, golden regenerated, one line; `board/prompt.js` START line says how). Pay: `combat/horde-pay.js` (per wave on kills, `winXp` each, no cap over waves); `v1-award.js XP_TOP` = 300 × 13 waves. `resume-v1.js` never resumes a horde. View: `fight-view-v1-horde.js` (latest wave per slot, strip), `FIGHT_SHAPE.horde` optional, `ui/tabs/fight/foes.js` strip, log `Reinforcements` rows; note: arrival lines and held/leader END lines (length line unchanged). Review fixes (HORDEFIX, REV-hordeb): `core/interaction/session.js stageInteraction` refuses a step whose preview result passes `limits.resultBytes` (`TOO_BIG`, "The <title> holds all one reply can save. Send to go on.", `contract.js` now passes a `TOO_BIG` detail through); `board/auto-run.js runAuto` stops `'full'` on that refusal, `autoNote` says to send, `ui/port/auto-fight.js` answers it when nothing was played; `horde-build.js hordeFrom` no longer copies a leader standing on the front into the reserve; `v1-award.js` holds an ordinary fight's award to 300 again and a horde's (`award.horde: 1`, set by `paidOf`) to 300 × 13; `v1-settle.js` reads a won horde's legend from every fallen foe's own level (REV-hordea 1).

- **Dossier rework (DOSSBUILD; owner-approved mock scratch/design/dossier-v2/full.html, 19 Nov 22:23).** `ui/tabs/people/dossier/` is split by job: `dossier.js` composes; `top-bar.js` (‹ origin, NPC DOSSIER, name, Reply #N, Dossier / Map, ⋯ anchored menu with Edit first, Close; two lines under 600 px), `hero.js` (ring r 46.7 in a 258 px box, labels clear of it, name, ★ Following as a 44 px tap round the chip, mood chip, who-line, coloured meter line, where), `cards.js` (parts list, cards with the mock's title colours, the open card outlined, Mood's since-line), `part.js` (header ‹ title "name · i of n" ›, ‹ Dossier below 1,100 px), `library.js` (strip folded or open, All / ★ Following, 3-wide grid on a phone; dedupes the port's id and name keys), `map-tab.js` (People map via `castMap({ card: false, legend: false, tools, allBonds })` plus the picked person's panel), `reply-words.js`. Layout by `@container su-root`: <600 one column (a part replaces everything), 600-1079 portrait 300 + cards (a part replaces the cards), ≥1080 290 | cards | 360 (1,080 rather than the brief's ~1,100 so the owner's 1143 px window, whose floating window is 1,095, gets the desktop layout). `wide-watch.js` removed (backed up in scratch/backups/dossbuild). Another person (library, tie, map) is shown in place (`ctx.onPerson` keeps the live redraw on them). CSS: `ui/css/dossier.css` rewritten (always `--d-flat`, no sky), new `ui/css/dossier-map.css` (in turn-on STYLES and the harness).
  - Data (`ui/select/dossier.js`, all from stored records, so replay == live): `past[].date/reply/dayRef` from NPC State's `memoryStamps` keyed by `normalizeName` (unstamped memories: no date); the list now runs newest first. `changes[].reply` from the meters' recent rows `n`, `changes[].note` the held-back reason. `moodChanges` from the npc.cast row's `mood.log` (reply `n`, date, v/a/d moves, `why`; the log keeps no mood word, so rows show the axes moved and the reason). `lastReply` = highest of stamp `m`, `fieldChangeMessages`, recent `n`, mood-log `n` (`lastSeenTurn` is a scan counter, never shown). All new contract fields optional.
  - NPC State's desktop editor (`style-editor-desk.css`): flat base, fields on `--su-panel` at 400 13.5px. Tests: dossier unit, sweep (now also Map tab and open library), values (flat with Panel background on and off), desk (three columns / page below 1,080, the scroll and focus kept on a redraw), pane, mount (Map tab, library, live redraw after a swap), selector stamps.
- Now tab removed; the suite opens on Party (RPG off: Bag). `open('now')` is an alias for the first tab. Needs you and the card's pack offers moved to a slot under the character card on every tab; Today, This turn, You, Here and pinned trackers are not drawn (You is one line under the Party strip; Today's due items are in Journal; trackers are on Story › Trackers). Desktop layout A (left card column, Here now, 820 px column, `--su-col`) removed; the dossier covers the suite at every width; the Show on Now switch is off Story › Trackers.

### Changed

- Text RPG: a chat read again or rebuilt under the new rules sees new difficulties (5 to 30 in six steps, the tone default Standard), new XP (foes counted from the foes record) and new verdicts; stored dice floors keep their old index.

- **One card under each story reply (REPLYCARD, owner-approved mock Version C, 19 Nov 20:16).** `ui/chat/reply-box.js` (with `reply-box-top.js`, `reply-box-tabs.js`, `reply-box-actions.js`, `here-card.js`) draws the card in the page only, never in the message text, so it is never sent. Its data is `ui/select/reply-log.js`: each reply's state asked with `sdk.query(name, { asOf: index })` (time, place, weather, `party.pc`/`rpg.sheet` pools, `scene.you` condition, `reply.changes`, `people.list` here), and the pools before it from `asOf: index - 1`, so a swipe, a middle delete, a rebuild and a reload give the same cards. A new Story Block query `choices.replies` (beside `chatter.replies`) reads each reply's `? choice` lines from its own text.
  - Top: line 1 time · place · weather (wraps; on the newest it clears the ⧉ corner); line 2 HP · MP · SP with 64 px bars (24 px in a phone-wide reply), the value, the change (−7) and the faint part where the value was; line 3 the condition whole. No money.
  - Tabs (equal width, at least 96 px / 84 px phone, 42 / 44 px high, words centred; the strip swipes with no scrollbar, snaps, fades at the edge while more lie past): Changes (the ✦ sheet's old list, with a Why on a held-back line), Chatter (moved from `chatter-block.js`), Here (slim ring cards; a tap goes through `pageHandles.openPerson`, else People), Choices (numbered rows as the mock; a tap fills `#send_textarea` and fires its input event). One open at a time; older replies rest shut; the newest rests with Choices open; a tapped tab is kept per reply until a new reply comes.
  - Newest reply only: the ⧉ Open Suite corner button and the action line (`ui/select/reply-actions.js`): ⚔ Fight open › (Fight tab), Pick up: names › (opens the existing loot picker; it now rests folded), N points to spend › (Party), N overdue › (Journal), Name asks to join › (their page), N pack offers › (Now; kept from the old Needs you), and No changes recorded — Re-read. Full-width buttons in a phone-wide reply.
  - Settings › Chat extras › Tabs (`ui/settings/sections/reply-tabs.js`): the four tabs, ⠿ drag or ↑ ↓, a switch each; stored as Story Block's new `replyTabs` text setting (`"changes chatter -here choices"`, read back whole by `ui/select/reply-tabs.js`) through `settings.set extras.tabs`; the Chat extras reset restores it.
  - Held-back feeling moves: `reply.changes` marks a `~ npc` move NPC State held back as `held` with its why in `detail`: a cap (`data.changes[].held`, "held at 22, at the <stop>") or the day's allowance (the pacing words in the receipt's summary, "today's major is used on …"; a move it stopped entirely is a "Name: nothing moved" line). The Changes tab shows "held back" and a Why button; a line not applied shows "not applied" and its Why.
  - Cost: `store.at` keeps 64 states, so the cards are cached per port by a chained key of every message up to the reply (`ui/select/reply-keys.js`: object, record, swipe, text length) plus the switches and settings; the value before a reply is the previous story message's cached state. Measured on a 71-reply chat: about 1.2 s for the first build after a chat opens, then 3-4 ms a redraw, 24 ms after a new reply, 5 ms after a swipe (without the cache: 2.6 s every redraw).
  - Layout follows the reply column, not the window: under 600 px (a phone, or SillyTavern's chat column at 50-60 % of a laptop) the bars shorten, the tabs are 44 px and line 1 flows; under 480 px each action is a full-width button.
  - Gone: the inline row (`ui/chat/inline-row.js`, `loot-part.js`, `selectInlineRow`), the Here drawer (`people-drawer.js` and its host above the message box), the Inner chatter fold (`chatter-block.js`, `ui/select/chatter.js`) and the Choices chip. The ✦ sheet keeps Re-read and Discard repair and gains Open Suite; its list moved to the Changes tab. The modules' cards stay under the head reply, below its card.
- **The suite is a centred floating window on desktop (FLOATWIN, owner 19 Nov).** From 821 px the layer centres the root: width `min(1280px, 100% - 48px)`, height `min(940px, 100% - 48px)`, radius 16, a 1 px inside line (an outline, so the container width is the window's), the existing shadow; the chat stays dimmed around it and the open fade stays. Sheets, Edit, the wizard, Settings and the dossier were already fixed inside the transformed root and now frame to the window. Layout A (from 1100 px of root), the 1,040 px dossier cap and the wizard's 920 x 720 keep their rules; they read the root's container width, so layout A starts from a 1148 px window and the wizard is 704 px high in an 800 px window. The text-size zoom scales the window's lengths, so it grows with the text while the screen has room. Phones and windows up to 820 px are unchanged.

### Fixed

- **A typed attack on a crowd of mobs or minions starts the fight board (FIGHTCHECK, owner 19 Nov: "a dozen of aggressive rats").** On a Combat Rules v1 sheet the pre-check answered `{"groups":[{"name":"aggressive rats","count":12,"level":1,"tier":"mob"}]}` and the size rule (`board/precheck-parts/v1-sizing.js`) let mobs and minions keep any count, but the v1 Foes block holds 1 to 6 a slot (`V1.foe.groupMax`). The opening's `set-slot-N` refused count 12 ("Field value is outside its schema"), `interaction/start.js` dropped the offer with a quiet `INTERACTION_START` problem, and the reply went on with precall's "No foes are set" line. Mobs and minions are now cut to `groupMax` (12 rats → 6), never refused, as that file's rule already said. The recorded opening carries the cut count, so replay is unchanged. Tests: `tests/modules/rpg/board/v1-precheck.test.js` (the cut) and `v1-precheck-crowd.test.js` (whole host, a level 1 hero, the answer as JSON text, as parsed content and in the proxy's shape: the send is held with the opening recorded).
- **A person brought in with `+ present` and no `+ npc` gets an NPC State record (NPCPRESENT, owner 19 Nov, live play on drop 20).** The owner's reply at Bluewatch had `+ present Bina Kelm` and `+ present Watch-Captain Edda Rorke`, and no `+ npc` line. Neither got a dossier. A `+ present` line never made a record: a pack person's dossier came only from the runtime after the reply (`npc.tick`, then `castSync`). The owner's reply has no `npc.tick` at all. A replica with NPC State off gives exactly the owner's receipts, and its narrator prompt has no `+ npc` rule. So NPC State was most likely not active in that session, and with NPC State on a narrator is told to write `+ npc`. Even so, nothing in the reply's own step turned a `+ present` into a record. A new person needed `+ npc`. Now NPC State has an `afterCapture` hook (`modules/people/meters/npc-lines-parts/present.js`, step `npc.arrive`, stored on the reply). It takes the names the reply's story `+ present` lines added, and a `+ hook` line's `giver:`:
  - A pack person (met, present here, or, for a giver, named exactly) gets their dossier from the pack through `castFirst`.
  - A new named person in the scene gets the `+ npc` stub with their name only. It is stamped with the reply, so the existing targeted fill-in (`stub-fills.js`) completes it from the prose. Nothing is invented.
  - The `+ npc` rules hold: not the player, not Manual only, not a removed name, not a short form that also fits a pack person, four new people a reply.
  - A title, group, faction's people or role makes nobody: `clerk`, `the guard`, `Guard`, `Tired Clerk`, `Two Watchmen`, and `Red Cord Enforcers` while the chronicle knows the faction Red Cords. NPC State's own identity rules decide this (`inferNpcIdentityKind`, `isInterimNpcLabel`, `isRoleDescriptionLabel`).
  - A pack dossier made by `castFirst` in a step after the lines is stamped with its reply (`ctx.reply`), not 1.
  - A same-reply `+ npc` line runs first, so its role and look stand.
  - Lines mode only. Replay == live: swipe, swipe back, edited Re-read grown and shrunk, middle delete, rebuild and reload are tested in `tests/people/npcpresent.test.js`.
  - The narrator's prompt already says `+ npc` brings a new person in, so the static prompt is unchanged.
  - `npc-lines-cast-first.test.js` changed on purpose: a swipe with only `+ present Torka` now makes her pack dossier in the step.

### Removed

- **Old smoke scenarios cut.** `tests/people/runtime-smoke.mjs` lost its scenarios after the host part (sidecar files, branch safety, OOC removal, chat cleanup, group ownership, same-filename isolation): no script ran the file and it already failed before the cut. The full file is kept in `scratch/backups/CLOSE3/tests/people/runtime-smoke.mjs.full`.
