- NPC State close-out 1: `modules/people/legacy/` is gone. The start (`bootstrap.js` → `runtime/runtime-start.js`), the runtime controller (`index.js` → `runtime/runtime-entry.js`), `runtime-persist.js`, `runtime-npc-lookup.js` and `runtime-cast-refresh.js` moved to `modules/people/runtime/` and the six sheets to `modules/people/css/` (`git mv`, unchanged apart from paths). The old roster markup (`present-scene*`, `runtime-present-cast.js`), the public `presentCastHtml` / `fillPresentCast` and thirteen present-card CSS classes went: nothing draws the World widget that asked for them. The JSON one-pass prompt went (`buildNarrationCapturePrompt`, `buildNarrationCaptureParts`, `capture-text.js`, the scanner's `narrationCapture` / `dossierAbove` options and the capture-only Stage 4, birthday and story-event texts); the ordinary scanner, backfill and Refresh prompts are byte-identical (background-prompts golden: only its five JSON one-pass entries left). Tools › "What's sent" and the host's prompt size now show the one-pass lines a hosted reply carries (rules in the system prompt, context at the end), built by the same function `updateInjection` sends. The lineage-keyed capture journal went (`journal-convert.js`, `journal-lineage.js`, `convertCaptureJournal`, the lineage branch of `journal-keys.js`): an entry is keyed by the reply's sid with the same text fingerprint; without a host nothing is journaled and an old lineage-keyed entry is never matched. Backup: `scratch/backups/close1/`; rows in `docs/merge/b4-removals.md`.

### Added

- **Bags and mounts from the Bag.** The Bag item Edit form and + Add have a Carries field (`ui/tabs/bag/carry-field.js`): Save sends `inventory.edit` first, then `inventory.carry` on the stack the edit leaves; + Add sends `inventory.carry` on the new stack after `inventory.add`. The field shows on every item gear.view has a row for except a slotted piece with no carry; it is read-only with the engine's reason, gear.view's new row field `carryWhy` (bound piece, pack item, money). `inventory.carry` now refuses a bound piece (`refuseBound`, before the pack check). The Party Bag slot's note now says "Edit › Carries". A bag-like item's sheet (gear.view `carriers.packs`) has Carry as bag… (member picker with portraits, `inventory.bag`; a refusal stays in the sheet) and, while carried, Put down (`inventory.bag` with no stack). Bag › Transport has + Add while the mounts switch is on, even with no mount (`assets.canAdd`), through the new reader-only `mounts.add` (a name in use is refused; the pace defaults from the kind), and a mount's sheet has Edit (`mounts.edit`) and Remove (the new `mounts.remove`), both in `modules/scene/mounts-parts/reader.js`.
- **Party › Mount and Bag slots can be filled (GEARMOUNT).** New reader command `mounts.ride { who, mount | null }` (`modules/scene/mounts-parts/ride.js`, feature `story-block.mounts`): writes the rider's name into the row's `where` ("ridden by A and B", keeping a "led by" verb), takes them off any other mount, refuses a mount left behind (`travelsWith`), a full one (riders ≥ count, mountsPace's rule) and a too-long `where`; each changed row goes through `apply` (row-scoped `ifThere`). New command `inventory.bag { owner, stack | null }` (`modules/inventory/commands/bag.js`): a bag-like item (`carry` > 0, or a slotless item named bag/pack/satchel/sack/pouch…; `gear/carry-parts/bag-like.js`) is filed under `equipped: 'bag'` (`BAG_PLACE`, one per owner; `slice.js` accepts it on any kind); a companion's bag is handed to them (`inventory.transfer`, load checked) and the old one goes back to the party; the main character's is only marked. `gear.view` adds `ride`, `bagSlot`, `carriers.rides` and `carriers.packs` (`views-parts/slot-carriers.js`); `selectParty` fills the Mount and Bag slots and `carriers`; `ui/tabs/party/carrier-picker.js` is the two sheets.
- **World › Map sub-areas, selection ring, map clear of the sheet** (owner 19 Nov). `ui/tabs/world/map-groups.js` folds a town's places: a "Town / Ward" name under that town (within a tenth of the closest zoom's width), and any place within a sixtieth of that width of a place of the same or a higher kind (capital, city, town/port/village, fort/outpost/camp, the rest), so Auvoth takes its six wards and Auvoth Depths while Bluewatch Village (5 units off) keeps its own marker and guild office. A folded town is one marker named "Auvoth · 7" carrying its sub-places' here/visited/news/selected flags; its sheet lists them ("Places here"), and a sub-place's sheet has a "‹ Auvoth" back button. At the closest zoom (or once the ring clears every other place, within four closest-zoom widths) the sub-places fan out on a px ring left open on the town's right for its name (dots at least 48 px apart, dashed spokes; a lone sub-place sits above) under their short names; the town and its fanned sub-places are must-show labels in the same planner (Ersveil at 1280 px: all 65 fanned sub-places and 17 towns named). Find for a sub-place zooms to its town and selects it. The selected (or keyboard-focused) dot gets a 2.5 px accent ring 4 px out; the browser focus outline on the map's place buttons is off (it scaled with the map into the big black pill). When a place sheet opens, the map pans the place to the middle of the strip above the sheet when the sheet covers it (`panAbove`).
- **World › Map readable on big worlds** (320-place Ersveil). Labels are planned per view in screen px (`ui/tabs/world/map-labels.js`): where you are always; then the trip's places and the selected or found place; cities and capitals; towns, ports and villages; the rest, visited and then the chosen region's places first within a tier. A label covers no other label, no dot and no edge count, flips left or moves inside at the edges, and otherwise waits (its dot stays; hover, focus or picking the place shows it; a closer zoom places it). Shown names are drawn above every dot. Region names are one per region at its places' median, drawn faint behind, only where they touch no shown label and lie over at most three dots. Views (`ui/tabs/world/map-view.js`): the map opens centred on where you are, wide enough for the dozen nearest places (and a short trip); Fit leaves far-off outliers out (gap test from the middle 80 %, at most a tenth per side) and shows "N places off the map ↑" at that edge, a tap fits every place; a region fits all of that region; zoom limits come from the places. Dots, names, strokes and the you-are-here ring keep their screen size at any zoom. With no trip card beside it, the map takes the card's whole width. A pan redraws on release so the names follow the view.
- A world pack's lore as a SillyTavern lorebook: `packs/lorebook-make/convert.js` turns `content.lore` into a World Info book named "<pack name> (Story Suite)" (hidden entries left out, protected titles tagged `[protected]`, stable uids, a disabled `[storysuite:lorebook]` marker with the pack id and version).
- **Settings › Packs: Create the lorebook from this pack.** An installed world pack with lore gets a Lorebook row: **Create the lorebook from this pack**, then **Update the lorebook** (with the version it was made from, after "This replaces the book <name>; edits you made in it are lost.") or **Up to date**. The book is saved through SillyTavern's own World Info save as "<pack name> (Story Suite)"; a book of that name the suite did not make is never written over. **Use with this character** (on) attaches it to the open chat's character as SillyTavern's own code does (`packs/lorebook-make/plan.js` characterStep, `packs/st-adapter-parts/lorebook-write.js`): a card with no lorebook of its own gets it as its linked lorebook, the card's `data.extensions.world`, through the context's `writeExtensionField` (the merge-attributes save SillyTavern's lorebook rename uses for that field; the panel's hidden World field is set too, so the next card save keeps it and embeds the book then). The character World Info dialog's own pick does a full card save, which remakes the greeting of an untouched chat and drops the story's opening record with it, so it is not used. "Linked to <name>."; a card with its own lorebook (linked, or carried as `data.character_book`, which a link would write over) keeps it and the book is added to the character's Additional lorebooks through `charUpdateAddAuxWorld` (`world_info.charLore`), "Added to <name>'s lorebooks (<name> keeps its own)."; a book already there says "Linked to <name>.". Each write is read back from SillyTavern and told as a failure when it did not keep. The chat lorebook (`chat_metadata.world_info`) is never set: it is commonly MemoryBooks' book. With no character chat open, or in a group chat, the tick is off with a line saying why (a group chat says to pick a character chat; members are never all attached). A chat whose lorebook is this pack's own book (made by drop 7, which set the chat lorebook) gets **Move it from this chat to the character**: the book's name and marker are checked again, it goes with the character, then the chat lorebook is unset; any other chat lorebook is left as it is. Command `packs.lorebook.make`.

### Changed

- **Three stances (STANCES, owner 19 Nov; Combat Rules v1 changed in place, no rule mark, no migration).** The stance types are `defence`, `speed` and `focus` (`combat/numbers.js`). **Speed** merges Evasion and Movement: half mastery, rounded up, on the evasion and the tempo roll (`rolls.js`); Movement's +1 extra-action cap is gone (`tempo.js`, `V1.tempoStanceBonus` removed). **Focus** replaces Support: always Mana (`pool: 'mp'`), its stat must be INT or WIS, and while on the damage and healing of INT and WIS skills × (100 + 5 × mastery) ÷ 100, rounded down, before the area 60 %, decisive and soak (`exchange.js focused`); the normal attack and other stats are untouched. Aliases (`evasion`, `movement`, `agility`, `swift` → speed; `support`, `channel`, `auxiliary` → focus) are read by `skills.js`, `core/runtime/skill-slots-parts/stances.js` (`stanceTypeOf`, `typeKey`, learn lines in `data-modules/part/act.js`), character files, pack words and skill sets. A focus skill on another stat is refused on a learn line (`J.focusStat`, also when a learn line rewrites a focus skill's stat), by the pack skill-set check, held at the wizard's Review, offered only INT/WIS in the wizard's Stat list, and moved to WIS when a skill is turned into Focus. Built-in sets: Swimming is Speed, Calm is Focus on WIS (Mana). The join pull writes Speed and Focus (a CHA lead's Rally rolls with WIS). The fight board's stance gives `adds` (what Speed or Focus adds), shown under the switch; the Party sheet rows carry `stance` ("Speed stance"). Narrator: the Skills sentence and the `~ dm skills` type list changed (static prompt golden: those two lines). Test fixtures: the design simulator gained rule set v8.2 (Speed), and `sim-traces.json` and `band.fixture.js` were regenerated from it (two stance trace cases renamed; band counts 47 → 50 and 89 → 84 in band); the fight-note allowance re-measured at 1048 tokens (seed 212) → 1260.
- **Gear stats you choose, and the Party portrait** (owner 19 Nov: "when I create the gear, i cannot choose the stats it has? when equipping, pc portrait circle is empty"). UI only over the existing commands, plus the accessory default. (1) Bag › + Add with Gear in fights on and Kind = Equipment shows Slot (the slots GEAR knows, or "From the name"), Rarity (all five while the starting kit is open; after it a fixed "Common" with the engine's kitClosed sentence) and a Stats picker (`ui/tabs/bag/gear-stats.js`: the Party stat sheet's `.ps-st` −/+ rows, at most `GEAR.maxStats` stats, "n of N points" = the rarity's budget). An untouched pick, or one equal to `defaultStat`'s, sends no `stats`, so the engine's default applies as before; in the kit a changed pick goes with `inventory.add`; after the kit (when the add refuses stats) the add is sent plain and the pick goes to the new stack (the receipt's `data.stacks[0]`) as the repoint edit `inventory.edit { stack, stats }`. (2) The Bag item sheet and the Party slot sheet have a Stats row ("+1 DEX · +1 AGI") with Change: the picker on the piece's `gear.view` `repoint` budget and stats, Save only with every point placed; a piece with no `repoint` (bound, Gear off, a budget of 0) sends the edit with no stats so the engine's own refusal shows in the note line (every such path refuses before reading the stats). `selectBag` carries `gear: { on, kit }` and per-item `repoint` / `bound` (BAG_SHAPE, optional). LUK is not offered (Combat Rules v1 reads no LUK; gear's list has no WIS); a piece that still has LUK points shows the row so they can be moved. (3) The paper-doll's centre circle: `partyPortraits` (`ui/select/gather.js`) gives You the people row of the party name, else the persona avatar (`personaPortrait`), and a companion their people row by name or alias; the initial stays the fallback. (4) Owner decision (GEARUI.DECISION): rings, amulets, pendants and the accessory slot default to CHA instead of LUK (`stat-choice.js`, `GEAR.slotStat`); a stat-less accessory already held reads CHA from now on. New stylesheet `ui/css/gear-stats.css` (in `turn-on.js` STYLES and the harness). (5) An empty bag keeps the Items card ("Nothing here." and + Add, as the mock's bag.html); before, `itemsCard` returned null, so a new chat's starting kit had no + Add.
- **Bars from stat pairs** (Combat Rules v1, changed in place: no rule mark, no migration, as there are no existing characters). `combat/numbers.js` holds each bar as `{ base, levelDiv, pair, pairDiv }`: HP 5 + level÷3 + (STR+VIT)÷4 (was VIT÷2), SP 8 + level÷4 + (AGI+DEX)÷4 (was STR+AGI), MP 8 + level÷4 + (INT+WIS)÷4 as before; `combat/bars.js` and the v1 Character's size rules (`templates-parts/v1-character.js`) read it. At every stat 5 each bar is unchanged (a test pins levels 1 to 99). The Party stat sheet preview and the Party fixture follow; the companion stat spread keeps its order. The 4 v 6 fixture fight note is shorter with the new bars (seed 56 still the longest of 300: 1026 tokens, was 1071), so the settled-note allowance `INTERACTION_NOTE_TOKENS` follows its rule to 1240 (was 1290) and the tight tail golden's budget moves to 1085 (was 1130) to keep its shape; the flag-off identity recordings, the fight note and tail goldens and the captured state and payload hashes were taken again, their diffs checked to be only the bar rules, bar values and what follows from them.
- **Desktop sheet frame** (approved mock `scratch/design/redo/sheets-desktop.html`). New `ui/css/sheet-desk.css`, loaded after settings.css: under `@container su-root (min-width: 1100px)` the three sheet hosts (`.sh-ov > .sh-panel` from `kit/sheet.js`, `.pty-ov > .pty-sheet` for Party and Bag, `.wov > .wsheet` for World's place) become a centred 640 px panel, radius 24, the panel colour over the flat one (opaque, as the wizard's), over a dim covering the whole window (`--su-scrim-desk` .58 dark / rgba(30,26,60,.34) light, `--su-blur-desk` 3 px; both new theme tokens that Glass strength moves). The panel scrolls; the head (18/22/12) and the last action row (hairline, 12/22/18) are sticky on the panel's fill, so only the middle moves. The wizard (`.sh-ov.wz`) is left to its own desktop dialog. In the sheets: Date · Time · Weather and Kind · Region, State · Place, Area · Stance share rows; Edit money is four labelled columns; the Party ⋯ menu buttons are 260 px; the main button comes first in Scene; Not now and Send without the RPG sit apart; World's place sheet hides while its editor is open. Under 1100 px nothing changes.

### Changed

- NPC State close-out 2: the old npc-state-delta names are gone from the people module's UI and code, by script (`scratch/batch/SMALL/night/close2/rename.mjs`, `bridge.mjs`) plus hand review. CSS classes `npc-state-delta-…` and `npc-state-delta-v2-…` are `ppl-…`, `data-npc-state-delta-…` attributes are `data-ppl-…` (with their `dataset.ppl…` reads), element ids `npc_state_delta_…` are `people_…`, the DOM events `npc-state-delta:settings-mounted` / `editor-mounted` / `cast-rendered` / `enabled-changed` / `v2-host` are `people:…`, the prompt keys are `story_suite_static_people`, `story_suite_tail_4_people` and `story_suite_tail_5_people_capture` (the prefix that orders them in SillyTavern is kept), and the in-page flags `__npcStateDelta…` are `__ppl…`. Reader text says "NPC State" (toasts, refusals, loading and off notes, the Tools preview label, console prefixes). The Story Suite v2 bridge identifiers are named for what they are: `createPeopleAdapter`, `startPeopleAdapter`, `rt.peopleAdapter`, `adapterMeters`, `suiteHosted`. Two test fixtures that still set the removed `npcStateDeltaStoryBlockV2` flag (nothing reads it; the globals gate forbids it) no longer set it. Every UI name moved in one go, so selector and markup stay paired: People, a dossier, Edit and Settings › People are pixel-identical before and after at 390 and 1280 in a replica SillyTavern, and the Edit dialog saves and reads back as before.
- Kept on purpose: the Settings › People fold memory key `npc_state_delta_settings_open_groups`; the module id `npc-state-delta` and what is keyed by it (the settings bucket `storySuite.modules['npc-state-delta']`, the feature ids `npc-state-delta.*`, the screen ids `npc-state-delta/cast` and `/meters`, the card-settings keys, the public-paths owner and the timeline's participant), because the id `people` is already the hidden start module and the registry refuses a second module with one id; the live chat data names (the sidecar file `npc-state-delta-….json`, its format `npc_state_delta_chat_data`, the portrait file prefix, the birthday hash seed and the export file name), because renaming them drops the dossiers of chats already played; the model-facing prompt text ("You are NPC State Delta's …") and the `<!--npc_state_delta …-->` capture tag, which the prompt budgets and the reply reader pin; the conflict guard (`OLD_COPY` still names "NPC State Delta" so the reader knows what to disable) and everything that finds the old extension (its folders, `NPC_STATE_DELTA_PATH`, the globals gate); and `modules/people/storysuite-v2.js` keeps its file name, because it is on the legacy exports list and a moved entry counts as an added one.
- Static prompt golden: only key names changed (`story_suite_static_npc_state_delta` → `story_suite_static_people` in six scenarios; the scenarios `npc_state_delta_off` / `npc_state_delta_one_pass_off` → `people_off` / `people_one_pass_off`); every prompt text is byte-identical.
- NPC State close-out 3 (the shrink-only lists): `docs/merge/legacy-exports.txt` 7 → 0 and `legacy-oversize.txt` 3 → 0. Split into one-job files with re-export facades at the old paths: `modules/scene/constants-parts/you.js` (`you-short.js`), `testkit/fake-st.js` (`fake-st-parts/`: names, events, messages, context), `modules/people/storage/storage.js` (ten `storage-*.js` parts: self, names, inspect, locks, retry, undurable, encode, write, retire, read), `modules/people/storysuite-v2.js` (`adapter/names-*.js`, `adapter/create.js`, `adapter/start.js`), `modules/people/view.js` (`view-parts/`); `core.js` and `core-prompts.js` only re-export (the NPC State version is `core/core-parts/version.js`); `tests/bench/load.js` → five `load-*.js` parts. Removed: the old host's pack checker fixture (`tests/packs/fixtures/old-host/`, no test used it), and the long scenario of `tests/people/runtime-smoke.mjs` (no script ran it; it failed at its inline-card step), which keeps its host and the load check.
- `docs/merge/core-module-refs.txt` 72 → 13: the pre-port fight cleanup (`core/turn/pre-port-fight.js`) and the pre-port board take-back message (`FIGHT_TAKEN_BACK`) are gone (fresh start; a board fight is taken back as an interaction, by its kind's title); the turn's held reply, its abort, the watch offs and the settle stop lose their board names (`heldReply`, `abortInteraction`, `offWatch`, `stopChangedInputs`, `resumeHeldReply` / `restoreHeldReply`, `view.interactionStop`); `RPG_LOAD_WAIT_MS` is `PREPARE_WAIT_MS` (it waits for the RPG's code and the pack checker); the registry's interactions example names no module's kind. `docs/merge/layout-old-files.txt` 5,465 → 3,990 (the files gone since the move).

## 0.1.3 — 19 Nov 2026

**Pack format 4, the Pack Studio and the overnight bug hunt.** A pack is now the world only (lore, cast, places, routes, events) with a separate game pack (rules, calendar, money, climate); the example pair is `examples/packs/ersveil` + `ersveil-game`, and format 3 packs are refused. The Pack Studio opens from **Settings › Packs › Open Studio**. B4-4 to B4-12 are in (old view removed, NPC State settings, "away" on Follow, rule weather kept). Desktop layout A puts the card beside the tab page from 1100 px. Two bug-hunt passes in replica SillyTavern and four rounds with a real model led to the fixes listed below; among them: met pack cast get their pack profile and the narrator uses canon names, hidden lore gates open, trips start from narrator places, weather follows the game's climate, a fight the reader does not play lapses after two replies and is never told blow by blow, the party pill shows the active limit (x/4), and the prompt build, an edited Re-read and a middle delete no longer freeze.

Since 0.1.2: documentation, and the Settings, Story › Director and settings-walk changes below.

### Fixed

- Batch 3 look fixes (seen in a replica at 390, 1143, 1280 and 1920, dark and light): the New character Review step has Create in its footer beside Back, so a long review no longer hides half of it; the Export sheet's choices are rows (the tick, then the name over its help) instead of a loose checkbox and run-on text; on a card offer, clicking the "Also apply" words flips the switch; the slow pace chip ("20 km/day") is warn text on a light warn tint as drawn in the mock, not orange on orange with SillyTavern's dark shadow under it; Here now cuts a place name over 40 characters with "…" (it stopped at "…House of the" with no mark).
- Batch 3: a worn weapon or offhand's slot sheet says what it gives ("Gives +2 STR"; before, always "Weapon"/"Offhand", as `slotsOf` dropped gear.view's bonus). The weather of a place the pack does not name takes the climate of the pack place its own words name (the name or its town part) and no longer that of the atlas's saved parent or the last pack place seen in its region, so those two atlas records (which an edited Re-read keeps from the old text) no longer decide it. The region fallback and the atlas's current place name are still read, so an edited Re-read can still differ there. An unmapped building in a pack town with its own climate now takes its region's climate unless its name carries the town ("Auvoth / Lantern Inn").
- Batch review 2: the Inspector lists an invented pulse event as "World this reply: <kind> (<intensity>): the narrator invents it" (LF4-7 read a field the Inspector's receipt list never carried); a block pinned on Now keeps the fields the story is told but the reader may not edit (a skill's rank); four messages still named the old extension (a life-state toast, two confirm dialogs and the RPG checks' setting label) and now say NPC State; the first-send hold shows a persona or character name with `$` in it as written; docs/v2/RPG.md had merge conflict markers in the `+ party` section.
- NB-1 (LIVEF4B): a fight the narrator opens and the reader does not play no longer turns into narrated combat. While foes stand and this reply plays no board fight, the narrator keeps "A fight starts when you set a foe slot; the reader plays it on the board, so tell no blows yourself." and is told "The foes set stand, but this reply plays no fight: a message that is not played on the board moves the story on. The foes may press, block or follow; resolve no exchange, roll no check for a blow, and tell no wound, HP or other change of state."; it no longer gets the Combat rules text then (before, it got "[COMBAT SYSTEM] … Exchanges: tell a fight exchange by exchange …" and lost the start line, so five replies told a mauling while HP stayed full). A reply that tells the board's play gets the Combat rules text without the sections the board owns (`Exchanges:`, `Rolls:`, `XP guide:`, left out by their section label, also in the one-line text a chat pins; an edited text without those labels is sent whole), so the narrated-combat rule is never sent. In the reply to the reader's second message that does not play the fight, the fight lapses: the foes are cleared (why `gone`) by a stored prelude whose note tells the narrator "The fight with Fen-hound ×2 was left unplayed on the board, so it lapsed: they withdrew or were left behind." (the Fight open chip goes with them). It is counted from the stored replies (from the latest reply whose receipts set one of the standing groups or settled a board fight), so swipe, swipe back, an edited Re-read, a middle delete, a rewind and a reload agree; foes whose latest change was the reader's own (set on the Foes block), a fight open on the board, a held send and a typed attack on the foes never lapse. The Combat rules setting's help now says so: "The narrator reads it when it tells a fight played on the board; its Exchanges, Rolls and XP guide parts are left out, because the board plays those." A prelude receipt may now carry `data.chain` (docs/v2/SPEC.md, Kept cues). Not changed: the static prompt; the card preset's own fight-continuation lines (outside the suite).
- LPB (live-perf bug hunt 5, 6, 7). A reply's queued NPC State backfills (the dossier fill-ins for new people) run up to three at a time instead of one after another, so a reply settles sooner, and the reader gets one toast for the batch ("NPC State: backfilled 3 people (…) from recent story context."), not one each; a failed one is still counted and retried on its own, and anything else that scans waits for the batch as before. A `~ know` line whose `how:` the rule refuses now shows a Problem on that line ("Know line not used: know needs about, fact and how (saw or heard).") and writes nothing for it; before it was only a console warning. Another person's know line in the same reply was never lost (each line is its own command); the live run's Henn line was stored. `how:` also reads physical discovery as saw ("pulled the parchment from her coat", "searched the coat", "frisked", "retrieved", "uncovered", "examined", "opened", "lifted"), for new lines only (a stored reply replays as it was read). Toasts and messages say "NPC State", never "NPC State Delta". Not changed: the `AbortReason` console error at a new chat is SillyTavern's own (its main-API status check is cancelled when the chat opens, script.js `cancelStatusCheck`); Story Suite never logs it and cannot stop it.
- Live perf hunt (LIVEPERF findings 1, 3 and 4; 2 in part): less work on the main thread when a prompt is built, when an edited reply is Re-read and when a middle reply is deleted (and in a capture's pack reads). `packs.content` hands out the packs' own frozen records instead of a copy of them on every ask (it was asked thousands of times a reply, the whole world's places and cast each time); a pack person's signature is hashed once per pack row; the lore digest is not hashed again while its text is the same. Replay: a state the store made is known frozen, so a command or a step planned again no longer walks or copies it again; the head replay keeps the state after each reply it passed, so a Re-read or the reconcile after a delete that follows a rebuild starts from the state before its reply instead of replaying from the checkpoint again, and the reconcile takes the head replay's Problems instead of replaying the whole chat once more. Replay, swipe, delete, rewind and reload give the same state as before, byte for byte. Not changed: NPC State's reads of an earlier reply during a capture (`asOf`) still replay from the checkpoint, so a capture is only a little lighter, and the world-events day step's place checks are as they were.
- LA fixes (live arcs run): World conditions of a region keep reaching the narrator at a place the narrator made up in that region ("~ place Auvoth Frontier Road | region: Bluewatch Reach" no longer reads the conditions of the pack town its name starts with, and a region given by name finds conditions stored under its id). The world's open-recovery slots show the Director only what code accepts there: no ladder direction without a rung, no clear of a condition that does not hold, so a batch no longer costs a refused answer and a retry for that. A `!` line whose kind word is not the kind of the thread its key names ("! clock Grey Ledger Sweep" for a hook) is still applied to that thread, and now lists a Problem: `"! clock Grey Ledger Sweep" names a hook, not a clock; the line was applied to the hook.` Not changed: a `!` that only paraphrases a beat under another key still reads as a new thread.
- A trip the narrator starts from a place of their own that is not on the pack map sets out from the pack place its name stands for (the name itself, its town part as in "Auvoth / Guild Grand Hall", or the place it names as in "Frontier Road south of Bluewatch"), so the pack route is used ("on the road x of 70 km") instead of "not on the map". The start is read from the name and the pack only, never from the map's visits, so an edited Re-read gives the same trip as the story told the edited way; a made-up name that names no pack place ("The Weir") is still "not on the map".
- LF4E (live bug hunt LF4-3 rest, LF4-7, LF4-8 rest, NB-2). A trip from a narrator road put inside a region ("Bluewatch Reach / Frontier Road") sets out from the one pack place in that region a pack road of that kind joins (read from the name and the pack alone, so a Re-read tells it the same); with no region or more than one place it guesses nothing. The Inspector's "What code did" now lists what a receipt says it showed ("World this reply: …") instead of the narrator's instruction: the reply view keeps `data.shown`. The open-template helper is told the region's name, not its id, in its Where line and in the conditions held there. A refused story route no longer repeats the quoted line in its message.
- A story `+ route` between two pack places the pack does not join is refused ("the pack has no road between …"); roads to places the story made up still work.
- Live bug hunt LF4-4, LF4-7, LF4-8. Weather: a place the pack does not name takes the climate of the pack place its own words name (the name itself or its town part; Batch 3 dropped the saved parent and the last pack place in its region), else of its region, found by id, name, alias or the start of its name (the narrator's "Auvoth" is the pack's "Auvoth Compact"), so cold Auvoth keeps cold weather at an unmapped hall and a story `~ weather` the same day in the same town is no longer replaced. The Tools inspector's receipt for an invented world-pulse event reads "World this reply: mood swing (noticed): the narrator invents it", not the narrator's instruction. The cast planner is told the party's region by name ("Bluewatch Reach"), not its id.
- The world's own people at the scene's place reach the narrator (LA-2), and a first meeting's cue gives the person's name (LF4-2). A `[People here]` tail line names at most 4 format 4 cast entries (`figure` / `companion`) not yet met who are at the scene's place or its parent place (the place's own first, then the parent's; never a sibling district, a district under it or a grandparent), each with their role and a short look (never a secret, history or anything gated), and tells the narrator to use that person by this name rather than making up someone new for their role, and that the narration uses a name only after someone in the story says it. The `[Cast]` cue of a first meeting (by place, routine, goal or a reader's request) now gives the name too ("the ferrywoman comes into the scene in this reply; their name is Fenna. … the narration calls them the ferrywoman and uses the name only after someone in the story says it."), instead of hiding it (LAB2-6); its descriptive fields still call the stranger by the alias. Before, the cue brought Edda Rorke into Bluewatch as an unnamed stranger and nothing else named her, so the narrator made up "Sanne Hult" or "Hesta Bolm", the meeting was taken back and a second watch-captain followed. Someone this reply's meeting brings in is named by the cue and left off the line; people met before, in the scene or followed, the dead and the player's character are left out. Both are prompt lines only: no receipt, card, Journal entry or toast names an unmet person. The cast feature's description now reads "Unmet cast is hidden from you; the narrator is told who is here." (it said unmet cast was hidden from the narrator's text). No new state: a swipe, delete, rewind, Re-read or reload sends the same lines. The static prompt is unchanged. LF4-1: a `+ npc` line for a pack person met or present here already made their dossier from the pack (S2PA B2; the live shapes are now pinned by a test); a `+ npc` that writes a pack person's full name or alias while they are off-scene and not met (no `+ present`) made a plain record that the pack never filled, even after the meeting, and now makes the dossier from the pack too (the place is their own; they are not met by it; a first name, surname or role alone still makes a plain record).
- World events (LF4-5): a hidden lore entry's gate now opens it. With the World events switch on, an entry whose gate holds (a recorded `fired:`, `fact:`, `learned:` … anchor, or You at its `place:` or a spot under it) is opened by the reply that met it and stays open (a swipe, delete, edited Re-read or rewind of that reply takes it back, until a later reply's day step has recorded it); from the next prompt the narrator gets it as `Lore (now open) — <title>: <text>`, an optional tail line that only uses the room left (at most 2 entries, 600 characters each, 1000 in all). Before its gate it is in no prompt, as before. `stage`, `meter` and `quote:` terms in a lore gate are not read yet, and opening is not learning: `learned:lore/…` and the entry's `writes_fact` still have no source. Nothing changes with the switch off.
- Two wordings: a refused clock time reads `"25:00" is not a clock time` (it no longer says the line "has" itself; a time inside a longer line still reads `"…" has "25:00", which is not a clock time`), and the prompt's know-line for a fact that opens with a verb reads `Astra knows: Torka hunts the singing orc` (it was `Torka's hunts…`).
- Settings window: it is a modal window now, like the suite's sheets: Tab stays inside it, the suite behind it is inert, the × takes the focus, and Escape gives the focus back to the ⚙. Escape with a Prompts editor open closes the editor first, not the whole window.
- Story › Director with Story Director off: the view is one card, "Story Director is off", instead of a plan bar, an arc planner button and cards that do nothing. Trackers still work.
- First Send of a chat with the RPG on, after the persona changed: when the story already has a character but none named like your persona, the hold says "…you are playing as Lucien Noctis" and offers only Send without the RPG (before, it said there was no character and offered to make a second one that could not be created).
- Second UI bug hunt (SOAK2UI 3, 6, 7, 10–18): a companion who asked to join is kept in Now › Needs you ("Pell asks to join the party", opens People) until you answer Add to party or No, however many replies came after (it is read from the replies' receipts, so a swipe, a delete, an edited Re-read, a rewind or a reload bring it and take it away with its reply; the card under the latest reply is as before). People › Map spreads a cast of 3, 6 or 9 people round the centre (they were all drawn on one spot). The dossier's Edit window no longer says "LIVE DOSSIER" or "NPC State Delta's extension-owned JSON data" (it says "Edits save to this story's NPC State."); it is still the old window, not a suite sheet. The new character window's footer is solid and no longer covers Create. Here now: room under its hint, and a long where-line ends in "…". The desktop tab strip sticks at the scroller's top edge, so nothing scrolls through a gap above it. World › Places is a card, and its here / been marks are chips. A chat with no scene draws no lone "›" in the header. A lead says "lapses January 5", not "January 5, 1", while the story has no date. A weapon in a slot sheet says what it gives (its stat bonus) or "Weapon", not "No soak points"; the Equip button, the points badge and the pace chip no longer carry a dark text shadow. A pinned tracker on Now leaves out the fields the block keeps for its own rules (Rise, Level cap, Down, the largest bars) and keeps each label on one line. The card offer's "Also apply" tick is the suite's switch, not a native checkbox. Not changed: the ⌃⌃ float at the left edge (source not found), "Worth here 0 gold" on an unpriced item, and the dossier Edit window staying a SillyTavern popup.
- Chat ✦ "this reply" button stays on the latest reply after a delete or a rewind even when that reply made no change lines (its sheet then says no changes and still has Re-read).
- Party: **Export <name>** is built. It is a button under your own sheet and an entry in a companion's ⋯ menu; it saves `<name>.character.json` (level, points, stats, skills, and, as you choose, what the character carries and, for you, the purse). Before, there was no Export at all.
- The death card says who died ("Astra has died."), not "This character has died."
- The party pill and the Party tab show the active limit the engine holds a party to, 4 ("3/4"), and offer no Invite at four active (a `+ party` line then asks who steps out); an older chat with more active members is listed up to eight.
- Story › Director: the "?" on a roll that the hidden plan covers now says why (hover and screen reader: "Hidden while the plan is hidden"), so it no longer reads as a missing icon.
- Docs (RPG.md): a joining companion's `best:` list ends in wis (not luk); a companion starts with every stat 5, the best stat 9 and one point per level above the first spread on the best stat and DEX, and Edit first opens that spread with no points left (before, it said 10/14 and "spend the rest"); Export is described as built.
- SOAK2PEOPLE fixes. Journal › quest Edit no longer draws an empty Current step box for a quest with no step list; a given-up quest reads "given up" once and has **Remove** in Closed. A dossier that is open follows the story (and an Edit save) without closing it; its last-change card skips a change the day's allowance held back; the Profile page shows Role, Tastes and what they Know; the corner numbers sit 8 px in from the ring. People › Map spreads three (or six, nine) people round the circle instead of stacking them on one spot. The Director arc card no longer says "no plan yet" beside a waiting plan, and an ended arc shows no plan and no End button. `+ faction Name — about | area | stance:` stores the area from its second part (replies from now on); World keeps the view you chose after an Edit is saved. Now › Today leaves out the Director's off-screen agendas, clocks and beats while the plan is hidden. People › Here with no record gets **+ Add <name>** (an empty dossier), and Here now names and counts them. Settings › People › Re-read shows its answer under the list. The Journal day record says "began today" for a quest taken and ended the same day. The dossier Edit window says "Dossier" and "NPC State" (not "LIVE DOSSIER" and "NPC State Delta").
- People (second soak): a `~ memory`, `~ reveal` or tie `with:` line about a pack person met or present in the reply now makes their dossier from the pack first, as a `~ npc` line already did, instead of "has no record yet". A `+ npc` line naming a present or met pack person (`+ npc Rika | the young mage | …`) gives the pack's Rika (role, species, home, Level, abilities, keepsakes), not a thin stub. A pack's gated hidden note can now open: the narrator's STORY EVENTS list names the note by its own id, which `~ reveal` takes (it named `s1`, which was refused), and the dossier's Secrets count includes the pack's gated notes ("Opens when the story earns it"). A swipe after a middle delete no longer meets someone that branch never showed: a planned meeting finds its reply by the record it is stored on, not by a chat index the delete moved (a withdrawn person's "Paths crossed" lines all go). People left behind when the scene moves keep the place they were left at (People › Elsewhere and Right now). A pack person's starting `status` is what they were doing before they were met: while it is still the pack's words it is not shown as Right now or told to the narrator as their state; and someone who comes back into the scene (`+ present`) loses the status the story gave them while they were elsewhere (a status line in the same reply still lands). Someone away and a full day past their back date is "overdue (due back <date>)" to the narrator, not "due back".
- Soak UI fixes: Bag item sheet no longer shows a raw record id after "From"; the wizard's Create is held (with "Start the chat first…") in a chat that has no first message instead of doing nothing; the card offer says "NPC State" (not "NSD") and its tick is styled; the suite sits above floating buttons from other extensions (z-index 10000); the phone mini bar has an opaque backing; light-theme muted text is darker (4.5:1 or better); the wizard's Back / Next stay in view off the sheet's rounded edge; Journal › Leads show the giver, the summary and a letter disc from the title when there is no giver; the dossier's Past card fills the row when there are no Ties, and a Right now card with only a thought or wearing has a line; a blank place or time no longer leaves a stray comma in a news "heard" line; a scan cancelled on purpose is no console error.
- Chat ✦ "this reply" sheet: it now has **Re-read this reply** (after you edit a reply's changes block, this is how you read it again); each line shows its icon, not the icon's name; a reply whose lines are only quest, time or weather says "No item, money, place or people changes" instead of "0 changes".
- A payment that is exactly an open quest's stated reward (from its giver, when it names one) no longer raises the "more than 5 times the purse" Problem. Other far-above-purse payments still do.
- The "The story before some steps changed… planned again" note after a delete is one row (the newest), and the Tools card no longer counts it as a problem to look at.
- World › Edit: when the engine refuses a change (a faction name already taken, a news row that cannot be changed), the editor stays open and says why ("That change was not kept." when the engine gives no reason); before, it closed as if saved.
- The arc card shows an automatic plan and "Planning…" at once (the page follows the arc planner), not at the next redraw. A money-guard reward written in a larger coin ("2 silver") and paid as "+ coin 20" is no longer flagged, nor is a payment whose quest the same reply closed first. The Now tab's empty line spans both columns on a desktop.
- World: places the story made up that have no map position now show under **Places** (the present one first) instead of "Nothing is known about the world yet."
- People: a scene with people here but no NPC State record says "Here with no record yet: …" and no longer also says "No one here."
- Docs: MODULES.md now says what Story Director's weather does (the story's own weather stays while you are in that day and place; leaving brings the day's rule weather back).
- Bag › Wear on… and Give to… now act on the member you pick (before, Wear on… put the piece on the main character whoever you chose). The Party slot sheet lists the matching unworn gear from the Bag with an Equip button, Equip hands the piece to that member, and its Unequip takes the piece off (it sent nothing before).
- Bag: **+ Add** opens a form (name, kind, count, your note) and adds the item. "In bag" and the kind chips count only the items the grid shows, not the worn ones. An item sheet shows the stats a piece adds, and the Party stat tiles mark what worn gear adds (a small +n).
- Party sheet: a skill the Skills block types as defence, area, evasion or heal shows as a Stance, Area attack or Heal with its own cost; before, they all read as attacks. The wizard's Review names Mana for INT and WIS skills and heals, as the sheet does; it says "Adept (works from Lv 10)" while the level is lower; and it will not let Create go with two stance skills.
- A companion who joins with `class: I` starts at Level 1 (best stat 9, the others 5), as the rules say; before, they started at Level 5.
- Chat ✦ "this reply" button: it sits on the head reply's own action bar (not on a reader message after it) and moves on a swipe, a delete or an edit. "This reply" now lists a companion joining ("Pell joined the party") and a level-up from a won fight ("Astra reached Lv 3 (+1 point)"); a fight paid before this change has no level-up line.
- Settings › Start: "Roll checks before the reply" is drawn locked, with the reason, while the RPG has a Character (the fight board rolls the checks).
- A story clock that began at the calendar's default start (no date known) shows the time alone, not "January 1, 1, Monday", on the card, in Now and in the "Story time" receipt.
- Level points: the wizard says "each level you gain later gives 1 more point"; RPG.md now names the Combat Rules v1 rule (1 per level) apart from the earlier Character (every third level), and states the Combat Rules v1 fight XP (tier factors; the earlier 15-per-foe rule is the marked Character's).
- Settings › RPG "Over the load limit" reads "Built in"; the item sheet no longer shows a receipt id as "From"; the Bag's load line says "Astra carries 34" (not "strength"); the New character sheet is opaque on a phone.
- World › News, Factions and the place sheet: **Edit** now opens an editor (title or name, summary, state, place, area, stance, kind, region) and saves only what changed.
- World › News: with World events off, happenings the world made that you have not heard of are no longer listed; the Rumours filter counts the items labelled rumour; a news line with no place or date no longer reads "heard , …"; world event titles end on a whole word.
- World › Map: the place sheet says visited or here and lists its routes; Find (Enter) opens the place or says nothing matched; the region picker keeps its width. The tab strip on wide screens has a panel background so labels do not draw over the cards. (The map's label crowding at default zoom is not changed.)
- Fight board (bug-hunt batch): **Discard fight** asks first (Discard / Keep it); a fight started from the standing-foes offer and discarded gives the offer back, so the Fight tab stays while the foes stand and a typed attack is held again. The board's **Auto** plays up to Most exchanges Auto plays (3 unset; 9 = until the end), stopping on a win, a loss or a badly hurt hero, like Auto-fight. **Send with this** is shown only while your message waits. The end card says the loot line only on a win and no longer states a Practice number; a disabled heal says "nobody is hurt"; the Flee tile says it is for the whole party and a failed flee says the others acted on Auto (Flee stays one pick for the party, by design); the header reads "Fight · place" and the pill follows the exchange being chosen; the battle log tells Down and Stamina words and puts the side that won initiative first in the turn order.
- Second core bug hunt (RPG and core): a companion's Party sheet shows the gear they wear (before, every member showed the main character's slots). A typed attack with a count or which-one word before the foe ("I attack the two raiders at the gate", "both raiders", "the 3 wolves") is held for the board like "I attack the raiders". A companion a `+ party` line makes from its class starts at that class's level held to the party's highest level (class II joins a level 1 party at level 1, not 15, and a level 7 party at level 7); the join card, Edit first, Add to party and Automatically all give that level. In a new fight (the `fleeIdle` mark), a member who picked nothing pays no stance Stamina or Mana and gets no stance practice when the party's flee gets away before their turn; a fight opened earlier replays as recorded. The fled end card now says what happens: "You got away. The foes are off the board; if the story brings them back, they return as you left them." (the foes leave the Foes block, as before). "This reply" lists a companion taken from the join card (Add to party, or Edit first then Create) as "Mara joined the party".
### Fixed (soak, People and Now)

- Scene spoilers on Now stay on after a reload or a chat switch: the choice is read from the chat each time, not kept from the first look.
- A receipt for a tie, taste, know or lie line, or a `~ npc` place, goal, status or gender label, says what was stored (`nothing moved; tie stored`) instead of only `nothing moved`.
- Now › This reply (and the ✦ menu) also lists the feeling moves of `~ npc` lines.
- People search finds a person by an alias; People names a person the scene has here but NPC State has no record of (`Here with no record yet`).
- Journal › a quest's giver opens their dossier when the name or an alias matches a stored person.
- Now says `Nothing to show yet…` in a new chat instead of a blank panel.
- `hidden` is a known module field: the console no longer logs `"hidden" is not a module field` on every load.
- With SillyTavern's No Blur, glass panels keep a solid colour under their fill so Settings and the dossier stay readable; the People map is taller on a phone so names spread out.
- Not changed: a removed feeling keeps its stages on purpose (Settings marks them "Not used"); an edit of a reply's change block in SillyTavern does not re-read it (an edit never captures; Re-read is the way, docs/dev/edit-prose-only.md); lines refused by a reply are still not kept for the card; a story hook (`+ hook`) stays on Story with the plan shown, as the plan is hidden by design; a secret's `who:` is not stored, so it does not link.

### Fixed (soak, keyboard, focus and Back)

- Opening the suite puts the focus in it, and SillyTavern behind it is inert (Tab and a screen reader no longer reach the hidden drawers, the chat-name list or the message buttons); a native dialog and the toasts stay usable. Closing the suite gives the page back and returns the focus to what had it before.
- The Party and Bag sheets (stats, gear slot, member, skill, item, asset) keep Tab inside them, as the other sheets do. A sheet with no × button (scene, tracker editor and templates, quest and World editors) now takes the focus on its panel when it opens, so its Tab trap holds from the first press.
- A person's dossier is modal: it takes the focus when it opens, keeps Tab inside it, and on close gives the focus back to the person card that opened it (or that person's card after a redraw, else the suite).
- Settings: a page picked from the side list, the phone list, a link or the footer opens at its top (it kept the last page's scroll); going back to the phone list opens it at its top too.
- The browser's Back (a phone's Back button or gesture too) walks the same stack as Escape: one Back closes the top window (a menu, a sheet, the dossier, Settings), the next one the suite; only then does Back leave the page. The suite keeps one history entry while it is open and takes it off when it is closed another way.
- After **Create** in the New character wizard opened by the first Send, the suite closes with the focus in the message box (on a touch screen it goes back to the Send button, so no keyboard pops up).

- **Pack Studio, the last review items (pack writers; STFE, REVSTUDIO3 items 1-4).** The Dry run's Holds tab lists each condition at each place with the runs of days it held (a condition held in many places is one row per place, not one run-on line), as bars on a dated ruler with "Show all N" past 15 rows and "By condition"; Curves is a plot card for each score and power that moved (a step line, where it started dashed, first → last, the changes and the biggest) with the ones that did not move listed under them; Cast has Deaths, Moves, Status changes and As they began; a day card folds to its first line (the first two start open). The map's realm names are drawn last, over the places, with a halo and without the " · REALM" suffix, so they stay readable and do not crowd at fit-all. The play build (`scripts/play/dist.mjs`) now carries `studio/` and what it reaches, as the release build does, so Open Studio opens there. Pairing's nav row now shows its count (`0` for a fit pair, `✕ n` problems, `! n` warnings) without checking the pair on an edit: the check runs once the packs stand still for 600 ms and is kept per pack hash (`studio/pair-count.js`). Tests: `tests/ui/studio/timeline.test.js`, `pair-count.test.js`, `tests/scripts/play-dist.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, review fixes (pack writers; REVSTUDIO3).** The Dry run's two "Start ›" links opened Home; they now open the world's Start (Conditions). After a run, an error in the world pack left "Run again" live; it is now held, and the form opens with "Fix the N problems first" and Go to the first, as before a run. Places, routes, regions, realms, powers and scores now ask before Delete with the red card the other record pages use (mock MK-2), saying how many records name the one going. The Dry run's day chips and result tabs are 44 px tall below 900 px. The Overview's Parts rows say what each part holds in words ("320 places · 352 routes"), as the page lines do, not content keys ("worldEvents 103"). The header switch's ✕ count stays on one line beside the pack's title on a phone, and on the lit half in light it is pale, not dark red on the accent.
- **Pack Studio, page drifts to the mocks (pack writers; STFB, REVSTUDIO2 C6).** The map's legend sits right under the map (it was pushed below the region list on a phone), and a region row has its colour at the left and › at the right. The map zoomed out draws each place as a dot in its region's colour (not a ring with a hole) and draws a realm's name only where it covers no bigger realm's name, kept inside the map. The event graph opens on whole columns (as many as fit, the centre's among them) instead of cutting a box at each edge; a line past the centre rounds it through the gap above or below its box, and a line back to the left comes into the other box's right side; the line keys are line samples, and a key that is on is tinted. The id picker keeps its search at the top and its "N of M · Esc closes." line at the foot while the list scrolls, and a row's kind is a tag. Short words get small boxes (`fieldRow` `size: 'short' | 'narrow'`): Era, the currency's Name, Plural and Short, Its field, Shown as, the dry run's Seed, a culture's Id and the Rank label. The Rules ranks are a table with # / Rank / From / To heads (a row of its own per rank on a phone), and a rank's × is plain. A culture's regions sit under its id in the Names table. A table's column heads line up with their columns (the coin table's sat off them: the head and each row size their own grid, so no column sizes to its content now). A filtered lore list says "N of M entries", and when the open entry is not in it, "The open entry isn’t in this list." with **Show it ›**. The nav counts what a part holds: records, a calendar's months, the coins, the weather tables and the name cultures (kind tables and the starting world are not counted; Rules and a game's Start show no number). Names and Trackers stay one page each, as in their mocks. Test: `tests/ui/studio/look-pages.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, the wide layout, the header switch and the nav counts (pack writers; STFB, REVSTUDIO2 C2, C6, C22).** Every record part now uses the wide layout (mock MK-2): at 1280 px and wider the list and the open record sit side by side under the nav, each scrolling on its own, and the record side says "No … open" with the list's New while nothing is open; below that one shows at a time, as before. Lore, cast, places, routes, regions and realms, events, templates, powers and scores, and items and foes all draw their list and their open record together (`studio/form/panes.js`); the shell joins a part's sections into one list beside one record (`studio/wide.js`), so Places & routes lists places, routes and regions beside the open place or route. The open record's row is marked, opening a record leaves the list where it was, and an edit keeps both sides' scroll. Go to, F8 and the problem list move only the box holding the record or field, and bring the field itself into view; Go to to a number box (an entry's Order) no longer stops with an error (a number box has no caret to set). With the list beside the record, a Go to opens its lore entry once: picking another entry and editing it no longer jumps back to the Go to one; and a click on a row while a field is being typed in now commits the field and opens the row (the change is drawn after the press, so the click is not lost under a redrawn list). The phone's parts list says the nav's counts and a short line for Home; and a long nav (both packs open) scrolled down to Pairing or Problems keeps its search at its top. With both packs open the header has a World / Game switch (with a pack's error count) and the search says "Search both packs…"; the nav starts with Home (the start page) and each row shows its count, or its problems in red (`✕ 2`) or warnings (`! 1`); Problems shows `errors · warnings`, Pairing `—` while a pack has errors. Page lines say counts in words ("320 places · 352 routes") instead of content keys, the foot bar's problem count is red, and with no pack open there is no empty nav column or "‹ Parts" and the start page ("Open or start a pack") is a centred column with the mock's wider buttons. Tests: `tests/ui/studio/wide.test.js` (now the real editors on the shipped Ersveil pair), `tests/ui/studio/frame.test.js`; editor tests now expect the list beside an open record.
- **Pack Studio, function fixes (pack writers; REVSTUDIO2 C4, C5, C8–C14, C17, C18, C21).** A new world or game lists every part of its kind as "Nothing yet" (nav count 0) and each part draws its editor's empty state; the first edit there writes the part's empty value in the same undo step, and looking at a part writes nothing. New world and New game open on Overview (on a phone too), which says "New in the Studio, not exported yet." Picking a portrait is one undo step (the picture, the field naming it and the old picture no one else names), and so is removing one; a refused step keeps no picture. Editors set what must outlive the redraw (the open stage or secret, a weather cell or new line, the map's note) before they commit and put it back on a refusal; a note that follows a change (a route's key warning, "the other open pack names this", "Made smaller to fit") goes to the note line above the page (`ctx.say`). A refused edit that the editor does not show by its field is said in the note line. Renaming a place kind carries templates' and world events' `where.kinds`, and a kind they name cannot be removed; a secret's id is renamed with the `learned:<person>/<secret>` terms of gates and odds (`renameId(…, 'secret', 'person/old', 'person/new')`); blanking a lore id that a record names is refused. Go to and F8 reach the field: an event's and an item's record holds its fields, a cast tie's row is marked, a gate in builder mode hands the focus to "Edit as text", and places, routes and regions mark the field instead of opening the JSON row; a chip box keeps the focus after each Enter. A season whose months are numbers can be edited (numbers stay numbers); with own weather tables a cell shows the "Any climate" table it reads. The nav no longer draws Pairing to list it (it checked both packs in full on every change). An old-format pack's subtitle ends "· Read-only", Overview says "Opened read-only: an old-format pack.", its foot bar says "Old format: not checked", Export (Ctrl/Cmd+S too) is off with no note, and its Pack card reads Name, Id, Version, Author in words; its Problems page still lists what the checker says. Esc closes the open sheet (id picker, gate term, New world).
- **Pack Studio, small editor fixes (pack writers; STFA, REVSTUDIO2 C20).** A place, region, realm, power or score with no id is no longer given a blank one: a blank id is refused with "… needs an id". A blank Lowest or Highest on a score clears its range (the default), as the hint says, instead of being ignored. A condition with no name can now be named (a plain set, as nothing can name it yet), and a blank name is refused. A condition or market record that is not an object no longer vanishes from its list: it gets a card ("#n · not a condition", what it is) with its JSON row to fix or remove it (`oddRecordCard`, `studio/views/json-row.js`). A gate's "Edit as text" now belongs to one lore entry, not to every entry. The cast editor takes its kinds from the checker's `CAST_KINDS` instead of a copy. Tests: `tests/ui/studio/edit-nits.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, record lists and heads to the mock (pack writers; STFA, REVSTUDIO2 C1, C7, C23).** The lore, cast, event, item, foe and template lists are the mock's rows: left-aligned filled rows with no icon column, the title with its tags as pills (hidden, protected, always sent, a tier, open), the line under it, the problem count in red and ›; the filters are pill chips (`.chips3`, the pressed one lit, readable in dark) and the search is the filled settings box beside New. An open record's head has its back button at the left and Duplicate and Delete at the right on one row, then the title at the page heading's size; the page's own title and the phone's "‹ Parts" no longer stay above it, so a phone shows one back button. Delete no longer swaps the head's buttons: it opens a red card under the head ("Delete “…”?", whether anything else names it, "Undo brings it back", Delete and Keep it) on lore, cast, events, items, foes, templates, cultures and tracker presets. A record page's groups stand apart, its JSON row is a card, and an opened cast or culture page has no lavender ring round it. Below 900 px the filter chips, the table and sub-record row buttons (↑ ↓ ×) and the graph's ◎ are 44 px targets. Light theme: graph boxes keep a frame on the white canvas, a frozen place on the climate map keeps a grey ring, the road samples in the map legend take the light road colour, and warning text (Problems, the footer's ⚠ Check, a gate's warning, the dry run's warnings and holds) uses the dark amber `--su-warn-t`. New `studio/form/reclist.js`, `studio/form/confirm.js`, `studio/css/records.css`. Tests: `tests/ui/studio/records-look.test.js` (Chromium, the shipped Ersveil pair, 1280 and 390, dark and light).
- **Pack Studio, the Dry run page matches its mock (pack writers; STFC).** The form has a seed with **New seed**, a number box for the tempo (0.25 to 4), the days with 30 days / 1 year / 3 years chips, the start date and what the run begins with (each with a link to Start), and a wide **Run N days** button. With an error in the pack the button is held and the page says "Fix the N problems first" with **Go to the first**; warnings do not stop a run. After a run the form folds into one line (Seed · tempo · days, the dates, the calendar) with **Change** and **Run again**, and the page shows "What happened" tiles, the Timeline / Holds / Curves / Cast tabs, kind filters, a day-number Jump to (and a link to the first death), and dated day cards with "N quiet days" between them, 30 at a time. The engine's notes (hundreds when many open templates fall back) sit behind one Show button. The dry run also reads a calendar whose seasons name months by number as the chat does, instead of using the built-in quarters (`studio/dryrun/seasons.js`). Not built: day cards do not fold, and Jump to takes a day number only, not a date. Tests: `timeline.test.js`, `timeline-form.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, list fixes (pack writers; REVSTUDIO2).** The lore list (and a filtered one), the cast and event lists and the name chips printed a stray "null" (a list ending with no "Show all" button handed the page a null, which the browser writes as text); they no longer do. Place, route, region and template rows sit in a settings list whose rows begin with a 30 px icon; having none, their text was squeezed into that column one word per line. A row with no icon now gives its text the row (`studio/css/edit.css`). Test: `tests/ui/studio/lists-render.test.js` (Chromium, on the shipped Ersveil pair).
- **Pack Studio, review fixes (pack writers; REVSTUDIO2).** Lists and record pages: the dry run's hold bar used the class `st-bar`, which every editor also uses for its search, filters and New bar and its record head, so in the Studio page those bars were squashed to a 10 px orange strip; the hold bar is now `st-holdbar`. The lore gate and a person's secret gates read the pack's content from the editing context as the Studio passes it (the pack itself), so their pick lists are filled and a place or person of the pack is no longer called unknown; the "what NPC State seeds" panel names places again. Lore: Delete, New entry and Duplicate set what the page shows before the change, since the Studio draws the page again inside `commit`; before, the next entry showed with Delete still armed. The dry run reads a format 4 pack's `placeKinds` and `routeKinds` (one object each), as play does; it read them as empty. The release refuses an output folder in or around `studio/`. `docs/dev/studio.md` no longer calls the editors stubs. Tests: `tests/ui/studio/timeline.test.js`, `edit-lore.test.js`, `tests/packs/studio/dryrun.test.js`, `tests/scripts/release-guard.test.js`. A refused New entry restores the page’s open entry, search and filter exactly (each key captured, set or not).
- **Authoring kit points at the Pack Studio (pack writers; ST-RETIRE).** `authoring-kit/README.md` now tells a pack writer to use the Pack Studio (Settings › Packs › **Open Studio**, or `/studio/` from a served checkout) and says it replaces the old Story Studio, whose repository is retired. Only that README changed; the archive note for the `Story_studio` repository is a separate text, not part of this repository. Test: `tests/authoring-kit/readme-studio.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, the "Open Studio" link and the release copy (players and pack writers; ST-LINK).** Settings › Packs has an **Open Studio** button (format 3 and format 4 pages) that opens `studio/index.html` of the installed extension in a new tab. The release build (`node scripts/release.mjs`, also `--check`) now copies the Studio page and every file it reaches (`studio/`, and the `packs/`, `core/`, `ui/` and `modules/` files it imports, the dry-run worker, the style sheets and the Nunito fonts) into the extension's output folder at the same relative paths (`scripts/release/studio.mjs`), so the page works where SillyTavern serves it; a reference to a file that is missing fails the release. The Studio stays out of the extension's bundle and its request count. Tests: `tests/scripts/release-studio.test.js`, `tests/ui/settings/packs-studio-link.test.js`. A single-file `studio.html` is not built.
- **Pack Studio, Go to and F8 reach a lore entry's fields (pack writers; STPAGEFIX).** The lore editor marked only the entry's heading as the record, so Go to and F8 opened the entry but never put the caret on the problem's field or its JSON row. The whole entry (bar, heading, form and JSON row) is now one record, as on the cast, places and powers pages, and its cards line up with theirs. The whole-page tests follow the editors that replaced the generic sections: the edit-loop tests open a lore entry from its list first, the two-pack test edits the game through the Money form's Short field, and the page tests expect the Pairing part for any world and the Dry run part for a format 4 world. Tests: `tests/ui/studio/edit-lore.test.js`, `edit-loop.test.js`, `studio-page.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, the dry run starts where a chat does (developer; ST-DRYFIX).** Two gaps in `simulate` are closed. It stepped the calendar from day 1 of year 0; it now starts on the world's start date (`content.start.date`, month as a number or a name) on the run's calendar, as a chat's first step reads it (`today = floor(story minutes / day)`), so seasons, `date:` events and month-gated events run on the right dates. A pack with no readable start date there begins on day 1 as before. The result gains `start: { day, date }`. And the world's start state is read from `content.start.world` (pack format 4), else from the older top-level `start.world`; before, a format 4 world's start conditions and facts were never applied. New `studio/dryrun/start.js` holds both reads. The Dry run page's summary row says the start date (`From 1 October 611, the world’s start date, on the built-in calendar.`, or `From day 1, since the world gives no start date, …`). Studio only; nothing else changes. Tests: `tests/packs/studio/dryrun.test.js`, `tests/ui/studio/timeline.test.js` (the mini world's start condition now shows from day 1).
- **Pack Studio, the edit loop (pack writers and developers; ST-WIRE).** The Studio now edits format 4 packs. Every record card (and a section that is not a list) ends in a JSON row: "Edit as JSON" › Show gives the record as 2-space JSON in a box read as you type (a mistake names its line and keeps Apply off); Apply sets it as one undo step. New `studio/session.js` keeps one editing session per open pack: `commit` runs the pure edits into a new frozen snapshot and that pack's own undo history, the page is drawn again (keeping its scroll and the box being typed in), and about 300 ms later the snapshot is checked with the game's checker (no Worker: Ersveil checks in 15–21 ms in Chromium) and saved as the pack's IndexedDB draft; an edit that changes nothing is no step. Undo and Redo are in the header (↶ ↷) and on Ctrl/Cmd+Z, Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z and Ctrl+Y (left to a text box while typing). Opening a pack whose saved draft differs from the file asks "Restore your draft?" (Restore the draft, one undo step on top of the file; or Use the file, which deletes it); undoing back to the file deletes the draft. Export is off while the pack (or the game a world's zip carries) has errors, with a note and "Go to the first ›"; warnings do not stop it, and a check still waiting runs first. An unedited pack exports byte for byte, an edited one as 2-space JSON. Go to and F8 open the record's JSON row with the caret on the problem's field (`studio/check.js` now gives each problem its `field`). The editing context (`studio/views/route.js`) is wired: `commit`, `setPortrait` and `openDoc` work, `refs` is the reference index, and `keep(id)` holds an editor's state across redraws. On a phone the header's buttons take their own row under the title, as in the mock (MK-1). Format 3 packs stay read-only, and Overview's own fields have no JSON row yet (ST-OVER). The restore prompt shows when the draft was saved but not how many changes it holds. Tests: `tests/packs/studio/wire.test.js`, `tests/ui/studio/edit-loop.test.js`; `tests/ui/studio/studio-page.test.js` now expects Ctrl/Cmd+S to be refused for a pack with errors and the mock's phone header.
- **Pack Studio, "what NPC State seeds" (developer; ST-SEED).** New `studio/model/seed-preview.js`: `previewSeed(entry, { cast, placeName, gated, packName })` runs the game's own cast seeding (`normalizeCast` and `applyCastEntry`, imported from the people module, not copied) on one raw world cast entry and returns `{ ok, record, fields, overflow, pendingLinks, stages }`: the person record the first meeting would make, and the listed fields it fills (`SEEDED_FIELDS`; empty ones left out). The whole cast is passed so ties and key relationships name their people; the starting place is shown by name through `placeName`; gated secrets seed only with `gated` on. Page-load check: the import graph (`apply-entry.js` and `legacy/calendar.js`) loads in a real browser page with no settings and no host globals, so no people-lane change is needed. Nothing calls it yet: the Cast editor (ST-CAST2) will. Tests: `tests/packs/studio/seed-preview.test.js`, which also runs the same entry through a whole host and compares the fields.
- **One zip writer (developer; ST-ZIP).** `scripts/core/build-example-zip.mjs` no longer carries its own `writeStoredZip`; it imports the Studio's `studio/zip-write.js` (unchanged). The two example zips are byte for byte what they were (`--check` passes). Tests: `tests/scripts/example-zip.test.js` (the script's bytes equal the Studio writer's, and the script holds no writer of its own).
- **Pack Studio, the reference index and rename (developer; ST-REFS).** New `studio/model/refs.js`: `buildRefs(doc, other?)` reads a pack's raw text and lists the ids it defines (places, regions, realms, routes, cast, conditions, events, templates, powers, scores, lore entries and facts) and every use of one, with its path, role, form (a whole field, a one-line string, or an object key), who it sits in, and whether it is written as the id or as a display name or alias; one-line strings (conditions, anchors, thresholds, outcome weights, gates, `learned:` ids, `arc-ended:`) are read with the pack checker's own parsers. `usedBy` and `definedIds` give the "Used by" list and the picker ids; the other open pack's name cultures count as uses of this world's regions. New `studio/model/rename.js`: `renameId` gives `set` patches that rewrite the definition and every use written as the old id (only that span of a string, object keys rebuilt in their own order), leaves names and aliases as written, renames the key of an unnamed route when a place it joins is renamed, lists the uses in the other pack it cannot change, and refuses in words (unknown, same, not an id, reserved, taken, an unnamed route). Format 3 and format 4 start blocks are both read. Nothing calls them yet: the forms (ST-FORM2, ST-CAST, ST-PLACES and the rest) and the graph will. Tests: `tests/packs/studio/model-refs.test.js`, `model-rename.test.js`, on a small pack and on every id of every kind in Ersveil.
- **Pack Studio, list form pieces (developer; ST-FORM2).** Four new modules under `studio/form/`, built from the mock's record page. `chipList(spec, onChange)` (`list.js`) is the editable chip list: the values as removable pills and a short box that adds one on Enter or when it loses focus (trimmed; a blank or repeated value is ignored); `onChange` gets the whole new list. `subRecords(spec, on)` (`records.js`) is a titled group of sub-record rows with a count, move up, move down and remove (the first row cannot move up, the last cannot move down) and an add button; the caller changes the document and draws the group again. `openPicker(ctx, spec, onPick)` (`pick.js`) is the id picker sheet over the shared `openSheet`: a search box over a list; a disabled row (already used) stays listed with its tag and cannot be picked; a pick closes the sheet. `gateField(spec, onChange)` (`gate.js`) is the gate text box: each keystroke checks the text with the game's own gate rules through `studio/model/gate.js` and shows the first problem under the box; the text is kept as typed and committed (trimmed) on change. `studio/css/edit.css` styles them. Nothing calls them yet: the editors (ST-LORE, ST-CAST and the others) will. The problem line's look was not checked in a real browser. Tests: `tests/ui/studio/form-lists.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, form fields (developer; ST-FORM1).** New `studio/form/fields.js`: `fieldRow(spec, onChange)` draws one form row for a text, long text, number, select, toggle or segmented value, built on the settings rows of the main UI (`.srow` with `.txt2`, `.sel2`, `.num`, `.sw`, `.sseg2`; no new mock pieces), and `setProblem(row, message)` shows, replaces or clears one inline problem line under the control and marks it invalid. A number gives a number, `null` when blank, and a bad entry is ignored; long text keeps its line breaks. `studio/css/edit.css` styles the long-text box and the problem line. Nothing calls it yet: the record forms (ST-OVER and the other editors) will. Tests: `tests/ui/studio/form-fields.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, format 4 kinds and the slots (developer, ST-K).** The Studio holds a world pack and a game pack at once, one of them active: the header says its kind (`World · Ersveil · 4.0.0`), the nav lists the world's parts under World and the game's under Game (Calendar, Money, Weather, Names, Rules, Trackers, Start), and picking a part of the other pack, or Overview's "Also open" row, makes that one active. Which content keys a part shows comes from the game's `CONTENT_KEYS` for the pack's kind; anything else shows under Other. Open takes a world zip's `game.json`, or a `pack.json` and a `game.json` picked together; Export with the world active writes the open game into the zip as `game.json` (after `pack.json`, before the portraits), and with the game active gives the game's `pack.json`; each comes back byte for byte. A format 3 pack opens read-only in the world slot, with a card at the top of Overview saying so (Convert comes later). New `studio/views/route.js` names the file that draws each content key (20 editor stubs under `studio/edit/`, each drawing today's read-only cards) and each page (`studio/views/{map,graph,timeline,pairing,convert,start}.js`); the map, graph, dry-run and Pairing stubs draw nothing, so their segment or Check part stays hidden until their jobs fill them. Editing still refuses (`commit`, `setPortrait` and `openDoc` say so). `studio/css/{edit,map,graph,timeline}.css` (empty) and `ui/css/world.css` are linked. Tests: `tests/packs/studio/parts-check.test.js`, `zip-export.test.js` (new fixtures `world4-mini.json`, `game4-mini.json`), `tests/ui/studio/studio-page.test.js` (its server moved to `tests/ui/studio/serve.js`).
- **Pack Studio, pairing (developer; ST-PAIR).** New `studio/pairing.js`: `pairPacks(worldText, gameText)` checks the open world with the game's own pack checker (`studio/check.js`) and, when a game pack is open, that too; only when every open pack passes does it run the game's `checkPairing` (against the Built-in game when `gameText` is empty). A pack with problems is not paired: the answer lists which pack (`blocked`: `world` or `game`) and says how many problems it has; a pack of the wrong `kind` is refused the same way. Paired entries (`errors`, `warnings`, `info`, as `checkPairing` gives them) each carry `pack`, the one to open for Go to: `game` for a names culture, else `world`. Nothing calls it yet: the Pairing page (ST-PAIRV) will. Tests: `tests/packs/studio/pairing.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, the document model (developer; ST-MODEL).** New `studio/model/doc.js`, `history.js` and `edits.js`.
