A document is the author's parsed pack (never normalized, key order kept) with its text and portraits, frozen all the way down: `openDoc` keeps the author's text as given until the first edit, and an edited pack is written as 2-space JSON (`derive`); undoing every edit gives the author's text again. `createHistory`, `record`, `undo` and `redo` keep frozen snapshots (the same objects come back; a new edit drops the redo steps; 200 steps). `apply(doc, patches)` runs `set`, `insert`, `remove` and `move` by path on copies, all or nothing, and gives `{ ok: true, doc }` or `{ ok: false, refusal }` (no path, nothing there, not a list, out of range, not plain JSON, `__proto__`/`constructor`/`prototype` keys); `setPortrait(doc, name, bytes | null)` adds, replaces or removes an image and never changes pack.json's text. A key that is a whole number is read back first by JavaScript, so such a key moves to the front; no pack uses one. Nothing calls it yet: the edit loop (ST-WIRE) will. Tests: `tests/packs/studio/model-doc.test.js`, `model-history.test.js`, `model-edits.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, dry run (developer).** New `studio/dryrun/run.js` and `studio/dryrun/worker.js`: `simulate` runs the world engine's daily step (`runDays`) day by day on a pack's content for a seed, a tempo and a number of days (up to 3650) and returns one row per day (what fired, the engine's log and warnings, moves, deaths, the conditions held, power and score standings); the same seed gives the same days. A port of `scratch/lorecheck/sim.mjs` without its strip step: the pack is run as written. `startDryRun` runs it in a Worker with progress and a cancel. Nothing calls it yet: the dry-run form and timeline (ST-DRYV) come next, and the run is re-checked when format 4's W1 changes how the engine reads the world. Tests: `tests/packs/studio/dryrun.test.js`.
- **Pack Studio, the gate model (developer; ST-GATE).** New `studio/model/gate.js`: `parseGate(text)` reads a hidden gate (a lore entry's or a secret's) into "or" groups of "and" terms (an anchor, `place:`, `quote:`, a stage test or a meter test; a term the grammar does not know is kept as `raw` and the gate is not ok), `composeGate(groups)` writes it back (canonical spacing; `meter trust >= 40 of wren` is written `meter trust of wren >= 40`), and `checkGateText(text, content, ownerId)` checks it with the game's own `checkGate` against a pack's `content` (event, condition, place, cast, secret and lore ids; a secret's stage and meter tests are about its owner). The grammar is not copied: `packs/validate/world-pack-parts/gate.js` now exports its `JOIN` and `ANCHORS` constants and a `TESTS` table of the stage and meter patterns, which its own check uses, so the game's check behaves as before. Nothing calls it yet: the gate field (ST-FORM2) will. Tests: `tests/packs/studio/model-gate.test.js`, on every gate of Ersveil and against the game's own pack checker.
- **Pack Studio, draft storage (developer).** New `studio/draft.js`: one IndexedDB slot per `<kind>:<id>` (`world:ersveil`, `game:ersveil`) holding the pack text, the parsed document, the portraits as bytes and the time; `saveDraft`, `loadDraft` and `clearDraft` never throw and answer `{ ok: false, message }` when there is no IndexedDB, the open fails or is blocked, the disk is full or the saved record is damaged. Nothing calls it yet: the edit loop (ST-WIRE) will wire the autosave and the restore prompt. Tests: `tests/packs/studio/draft.test.js`, on a small fake IndexedDB.
- **Pack format 4, weather by climate (developer).** The Director's weather by rule now comes from the climate of where the story stands: a place's own `climate`, `underground` for an underground place, else its region's, else temperate, by season, from new built-in tables for nine climates (temperate is the table it always had). A selected game's `weather` can replace the lines for a climate, read its season-only tables (style `tables`) or leave the weather to the narrator (style `story`). The seasons come from the chat's frame's calendar. A chat whose frame began with another game keeps built-in climate weather. A chat with no pack or with format 3 weather tables is unchanged (format 3 region names still match until format 3 is removed). Not yet: the Weather comes from row does not list Climate.
- **Pack format 4, the RPG pull: a joining person's sheet comes from their NPC State record (R1).** When a person joins (a `+ party` line, the Ask card's Add or Edit first, or People › Add to party), their NPC State record builds the sheet: the level is their Level (held to the level cap: Settings, then the game pack, then 99), else the join line's class as before; the best stat is the line's `best:`, else a fixed keyword table over their role, background and abilities (the same record always gives the same sheet); each ability, in order, becomes a signature skill (up to the game pack's `rpg.pull.abilities`, default 4, at most 6), its type, stat and resource from a fixed keyword table over its name and description, its rank one under the highest the level allows; one stance skill (Guard, Evade or Rally, by the best stat) is added unless an ability already is a stance or `rpg.pull.stance` is false. Their keepsakes that name pack equipment are given as gear (gear on); the rest stay prose. The card's level row and "Edit first" show the same level, best stat and skills as Add makes. A person with no abilities keeps the format 3 cast skills and gear the packs still give, and a person with no record is built exactly as before. The fight board reads a foe's stated level from the built-in Level first (a custom "Level" field still reads when there is none). Read from the reply's own prefix, so a swipe, a swipe back, an edited Re-read, a middle delete, a rewind and a reload agree. **Not built yet:** the sheet is not made when an ally first appears on the fight board, the narrator's `Gear:` cast line still reads format 3 cast gear (`packs.castGear`), and the sheet does not write story facts back (R2).
- **Pack format 4, world events through the chat's calendar (developer).** The daily world step now takes the season of a day from the calendar the chat runs on (the game's `calendar.seasons` from the chat frame, else the selected game's, else the four built-in quarters), so Ersveil's winter is December to February and another game's winter is its own. On a chat's first world step an event whose `date:` anchor is not on that calendar, or whose season the calendar lacks, is Lapsed with one Problems line, and a template with such an anchor is left out. A game's own season names beyond the four quarters are not yet readable as anchors. A chat with no format 4 world or game behaves as before, except that an impossible date such as `date:612-02-31` now lapses instead of waiting forever.
- **Pack format 4, the calendar from the chat frame (developer).** The story clock's calendar is now the game's: a chat with a frame reads the calendar the frame recorded at its start (months, era, leap years, week), so a game swapped in Settings later does not move its dates, and a frame of the built-in game stays Gregorian; a chat with no frame reads the selected pack's calendar as before. The Calendar choice `pack` is renamed `game` (a stored `pack` is read as `game`; the help says "game pack"), and the game calendar's `leapYears` and `week` are used. The start date is the selected world's `start.date`, and its era comes before the game calendar's (the frame and `packs.calendar` carry it); the reader's own Custom era still wins. Chats with no format 4 world or game selected are byte for byte as they were. The weather, money and world readers still read live (T2, M1, W1).
### Added

- **Story › Director: the Arc card can plan, accept and discard.** **Plan the next arc** (with the note box beside it, which now goes to the planner with that plan) asks the planner; a plan that waits shows **Accept this plan** and **Discard** (its text only while the plan is shown); **Planning…** shows while it is asked and a failure is written under the card. The card says **accepted** with the story date the arc began, instead of always "none accepted". A clock past its due day is marked **overdue**. A hook the story itself told (a `+ hook` line) is a Journal lead and no longer counted as a hidden planned hook on the Director tab.
- **Journal › Quests: Edit, Give up and Delete.** The engine now has `quest.edit` (title, summary, step, due, reward, difficulty), `quest.giveup` (the quest moves to Closed as given up) and `quest.delete`; the Edit button and the editor's ⋯ menu use them. They are replies' own commands, so a swipe, a middle delete, a rewind or a reload gives the same story.
- Settings › People ends with **People in this chat**, NPC State's roster as a list: each person with whether they are in the scene, followed, dormant or deceased, an **Open** button (closes Settings and shows their dossier on People) and a **Re-read** button (NPC State's Refresh for that person, with its answer under the list). Removing a person stays on the dossier's ⋯ menu.
- Settings › Tools has an **NPC State data** group: **Back up** downloads this chat's people with their portraits and portrait settings, **Restore** opens the file review before anything is changed, and **Diagnostics** opens NPC State's diagnostics window. They are the same dialogs that were in NPC State's own settings section, which the view never showed.
- NPC State's own calendar form is not drawn: the story calendar in Settings › World and time (Calendar, Era, Months, Weekdays, Leap years) is the one NPC State uses here.

- Settings › Look › **Panel background**: Off (the new default) removes the sky picture behind the Story Suite panels and leaves a plain flat colour, dark or light; On brings the picture back. It applies at once and is kept per device.
- Settings › People: **Custom meters** (Add a meter; each meter's name, lowest, highest, start, its rule for the story and the words shown instead of its number; Remove), **Meter presets** (pick Companion, Rival, Mentor or a pack's sets), **Per-meter stages** (meter, threshold, stage name) and the **Stage ladder** (stage, what it needs, milestone, track) can now be set in the new view. They store the same NPC State settings the engine reads, apply to the open chat at the next reply, and mark a row NPC State does not use. Renaming a custom meter also renames it in the stages that name it.
- Settings pages for a module (People, Director) now grey out and stop taking input while that module's card is off, with one line saying why; switching the card on makes them live again. (World and time and the other pages have no module card of their own, so they are not greyed.)
- Every Settings page's cards fold: the card title is a button with a chevron; a folded card is remembered on this device.
- **Settings › Tools › Prompts: the prompts can be read and changed.** People: how feelings are judged, People: how big a moment is, People: what is worth remembering, People: feelings into behaviour, and the Arc planner prompt each have **Read and edit** (a box with the text sent now, Save and Cancel) and **Reset to default** (only when the text is your own). They are saved in the same settings the old NPC State and planner panels used. Feelings into behaviour goes into the people notes from the next reply; the arc prompt is used at the next arc plan. The other three People prompts are used when People reads the story itself (Refresh, a catch-up read, or a reply that came without its change lines); an ordinary reply carries its own change lines and does not use them. The planner keeps its default as an empty setting, as its old editor did.
- **Settings › Tools › Chat data.** **Export this chat’s people** saves NPC State’s own file of the open chat’s dossiers and portraits and says how many people it holds (nothing is saved if the chat changes meanwhile); nothing in the new view reads the file back yet. Two lines say what the suite does on its own: deleting a message in the middle takes its changes back and rebuilds the story from the replies left, a swipe brings back that swipe’s changes, and an edited reply keeps its changes until you choose Re-read this reply. There is no Import (an import would only reach NPC State’s copy, not the story the suite keeps, so a reload or swipe would lose it) and no switches for rewind and re-read (the old NPC State switches are not read while the suite holds the chat).
- **For developers: `AI-README.md`.** A generated reference at the repo root, built by `scripts/ai-readme/build.mjs` from the parts in `docs/dev/ai-readme/` and from the code (the module SDK surface, change-line marks and command limits, pack format, fields, kinds and limits, the folder list and the Pack Studio's parts). `npm run codemap` writes it and CI checks it next to the code map. The written parts now say what the suite is, its concepts (state, replies, change lines, commands, prompt), the rules every change keeps, how a module plugs in and how a pack is written (the pack section describes pack format 3 and the Studio as it is built, read only).
- Settings › Look › **Panel background**: Off (the new default) removes the sky picture behind the Story Suite panels and leaves a plain flat colour, dark or light; On brings the picture back. It applies at once and is kept per device.
- **NPC State: people from a world pack arrive with their whole profile.** At the first meeting a format 4 world pack's person brings their role, species, gender, ages, forms, home, status, goal, Level, Abilities, Keepsakes, ties and key relationships. A tie to someone you have not met yet stays hidden and appears once you meet them. The narrator now sees each present person's abilities and keepsakes, and their Level while RPG is on. For now Level, Abilities and Keepsakes are shown only in the dossier editor (Level with RPG on), and with RPG on the dossier no longer lists Level as an automatic custom field.
- Settings › RPG and fights: the Difficulty row says what each choice changes and shows the chosen one's numbers; with Custom chosen, four editors set the numbers a character you make in the New character window starts with (stats start at, points to spend, lowest and highest stat at creation).
- Developer: a click-through test of Settings in a real SillyTavern, `node scripts/ui-walk/settings-walk.mjs --url <replica> --out <folder>`: at 1920, 1280 and 390 wide in dark and light it changes every Settings control with real clicks, checks the visible effect (Look, Panel background, the RPG switch, Calendar, a module switch) or the stored value, that the open page redraws, that a fresh Settings and a reload read it, then puts it back, and writes a pass/fail table with a screenshot of each failure. It starts nothing and refuses model calls. The release walkthrough, `docs/dev/release-walkthrough.md`, lists it with the pages and actions to check by hand. Nothing changes for players.
- Developer: the settings click-through now runs in CI as the **UI walk** check (`.github/workflows/ui-walk.yml`): a pull request that touches the UI, the SDK, a settings file, css, a pack UI file or the walk, every release pull request and a manual run start a pinned SillyTavern with no secrets and no model calls, walk every Settings control at 1920, 1280 and 390 wide in dark and light, and fail on any failed row; the table and failure screenshots are kept as an artifact.

### Changed

- **A long time skip in a world with world events runs each world day once (perf PB9, L1).** The open templates' asking ahead used to dry-run every day of the skip before the reply's step ran them again; it now runs after the reply, from the state the step left, one day on. A one-year skip on Ersveil went from 732 world-day runs to 367, and from 21.5–29.1 s to 10.2–12.7 s on a loaded test machine. A slot rolled on a reply is now asked right after that reply, so its answer can settle one reply sooner. A Re-read no longer starts the asking ahead; the next reply asks. The step state is still written whole on every day-crossing reply: the smaller writes tried with this change (L7) were left out, because a delete or an edited Re-read could change the recorded world.
- **A desktop layout for the suite view (layout A).** From a window 1100 px wide the suite view is two columns: on the left the character card (time · place · weather, bars, effects) and under it **Here now**, the people present as light rows (feelings ring, a star for someone you follow, name, mood, where, their thought cut to two lines; a row or its › opens the dossier, which keeps what they are doing, wearing and want, the feelings numbers and the Scene spoiler switch; a line under the list says so; nobody present, no list). On the right the tab strip and the open tab, up to 1100 px wide, with each tab's two-column parts spread across it. Both columns are centred, so very wide windows get side margins. The left column stays in place while the page scrolls and scrolls on its own when it is taller than the window; the ‹ Chat ⚙ mini bar is not shown on desktop. On desktop the Now tab no longer carries its Here card (Here now has it): Needs you and Today sit on its left, This reply, You and the pinned trackers on its right. Under 1100 px the phone and tablet layout is unchanged. With Settings › Look › Text size at 110 % or 120 % the page column still stops at 1100 px on screen, the left column grows with the text, and the switch comes at a 1210 or 1320 px window. The open animation is unchanged.
- **The suite view opens in place on desktop.** On a window wider than 820 px the suite fades in with a slight scale (about 0.18 s) over the dimmed chat and closes the same way back, with no slide from the edge. Phone and tablet keep the edge slide. With reduced motion on, it appears and goes at once at every width. Resizing while it is open starts no animation.
- Settings › Director: a group whose feature is off (World pulse, Arc planning, and any other tied to a switch) is hidden whole while its switch is off and comes back, with its stored values, when you switch it on; the switch stays on the Director module card at the top of the page. Settings › Chat extras hides its Inner chatter rows the same way (World and time already hid its switch-tied rows; People has none). The page has a working Scene spoilers switch for this chat (the same one as on Now and in the dossier). Cast moves have no setting (the cast reads the last 3 messages, up to 3,500 characters, and asks for 700 tokens, fixed in code), so the mock no longer lists them.
- Settings › RPG and fights has no Combat rules button, no Odds switch and no Rolls row: there is no setting behind any of them (Combat Rules v1 is fixed, the fight board always shows its odds, and the Story checks switch already turns rolled checks on or off). The design mock no longer shows them; a test keeps the page to rows that have a real setting.
- Settings › World and time: Era, Months, Weekdays and Leap years show only while Calendar is Custom. Weather comes from and World events say they are the same switch as the Director card's. Where the Day digest or World events rows are hidden, a line says which switch brings them back.

- **Settings wording.** The NPC State switches say "feelings" everywhere ("Feelings drift each story day", "Feeling presets and rules", the stage and social-check descriptions, the Lean preset line); only the labels changed, not what the switches do or store. The Chat extras page description no longer mentions Trackers (they are on Packs and trackers). Start and the section pages say "These switches are the same in every chat" instead of "apply to every chat", which clashed with the "this chat" header. The People page's own rows (Custom meters, Meter presets, Per-meter stages) keep their words for now: they belong to another job.
- **Settings › Packs and trackers: the tracker buttons each do their own thing.** The three buttons that all just opened Story › Trackers are now: **Edit fields** on a tracker (opens Story › Trackers with that tracker's editor already open) and one **Add from a template** (opens the template list there). New tracker and Build it in the chat are gone: there is no blank-tracker form and no chat builder, a tracker is added from a template and changed with Edit fields. A short line under the buttons says so.
- **Settings: Mounts is now Transport; three bag switches moved to RPG and fights.** The Story Block switch and the Home card that said Mounts say Transport (the feature and what it stores are unchanged). Pick what you take, Encumbered and Gear in fights are no longer on the Story Block card: they are switches under Settings › RPG and fights (group Bag and gear), writing the same features, so the card's "N of M on" count has three fewer. The story's own line to the model still says "Mounts:".
- **Settings › Start: real module cards and the three presets.** The NPC State, Story Block and Story Director cards have an icon, a description and an off line (what still runs with the card off), and their count says how many tokens the card's switches add to the fixed prompt, counted by the engine without switching anything (no figure when there is none or it cannot be counted, never "≈ 0 tokens"; the shared instructions every module uses are not on any card, so the cards do not add up to Tools › what is sent). The presets Lean, Standard and Full are drawn with what each turns on and the fixed prompt each would send; picking one sets the switches it speaks for and the page redraws at once. Chat extras keeps its own card text and figure (180).
- Settings: the answer to a change is now read. A refused value (for example a Months list that does not read, or a coin line that does not) is shown under its row; a switch, choice or chip row goes back to its old value, and a number or text box keeps what was typed beside the note. A number the store held to its range (Level cap 100 becomes 99, XP rate 101 becomes 100) is redrawn at once instead of after the next open.
- The README and this changelog are rewritten to be readable: short sections, tables, and the long notes folded away.
- Developer: the harness Settings pages now use the real Settings rows (generated by `node scripts/ui/gen-fixture-rows.mjs`), and a test fails when a fixture row has no real row or the generated file is stale. Rows that are not built live yet (the Tools page's Diagnostics group, one sample add-on view) stay as mock-only rows, listed with their job. Nothing changes for players.
- Settings › Add-ons no longer shows an "Allow custom views" row (the feature does not exist yet); the page lists the installed modules only.
- **NPC State's calendar and relationship code leave `modules/people/legacy/` (B4-7).** Moved with `git mv`, unchanged, every import re-pointed: the calendar (`calendar.js` with its `calendar-clock`, `-config`, `-date`, `-derived` files and part folders) to `modules/people/calendar/`; the focused relationship pass and the relationship pacing (`relationship-pass*`, `relationship-pacing*`) to `modules/people/relationship/`. **Removed (never reached since B4-2):** the reader's own-calendar switch, `calendarOwn` (it was always off): its adapter option and hooks, the cast-birthday skip, the meters' clock check and the Expert note "You keep the calendar here (Source of truth)"; and `setExternalCalendarSource` with its cache (nothing set it since the v1 handshake went). The story's date still comes from Story Director's clock while hosted, the Expert note still says whether Story Director's calendar is the one in use, and nothing a reader sees or a replay records changes. **Not removed, open for a decision:** the focused relationship pass still runs on a manual Scan and on scans that have no one-pass block, and NPC State's own calendar settings (Expert › Calendar & birthdays, with its manual date) still give the dates when no Story Director calendar is linked. Backup: `scratch/backups/b4-7/`.
- **The old NPC State dossier panel is gone (B4-5).** NPC State's own dossier window (Extensions › Story Suite › NPC State › **Open dossiers**, the `.delta-panel.ss-cards` panel with its library, board, sections, Map view and the "Dossier style" look switch) is removed with its launcher chrome (the backdrop, the full-screen phone and tablet rules, the always-hidden floating button) and its two settings slots. A person's dossier is the view's own: an in-chat present card, the world's People list and NPC State's public `openDossier` now open the Story Suite view on People with that person's dossier (`pageHandles.openPerson`, set by the view; a person the view has no dossier for opens nothing and gets a short notice, and the suite stays as it was). **Kept, moved out of `modules/people/legacy/`:** the portrait manager (view, upload, generate, remove) now opens from the view's dossier, ⋯ › **Portrait** (the port answers `npc.portrait` itself, a page dialog, nothing recorded; if it cannot open, for example NPC State is off or has no dossier for that person, the reason shows in the line under the dossier's top bar, as Refresh's answer does); NPC State's settings section keeps Backup / Export, Restore / Import and Diagnostics (`modules/people/tools/`), the calendar (drawn into Expert › Calendar & birthdays) and Maximum output tokens (drawn after Scan context in Advanced) without placeholders; loading the calendar file still activates the saved calendar before NPC State starts, and the meter presets block fills its fold as before. **That section is not visible at present:** while Story Suite hosts NPC State its drawer copy is hidden, and the view's Settings does not mount module sections (so since B4-4 neither the section nor its Open dossiers button could be reached; open for a decision). The NPC editor keeps its life-state control, grouped sections and appearance section (`tools/editor-refine.js`, `tools/continuity-ui.js`); the view's dossier has no Edit yet (`npc.edit` is unanswered). The stored `relationshipMap` and `dossierStyle` settings keep their keys, defaults and normalizers (`settings/runtime-settings-parts/`), so stored settings read the same, but nothing reads them now: `dossierStyle` has no control. Settings › People › **Map** (Relationship map, Default distance) is removed: it wrote `relationshipMap`, which only the old panel's Map view read; the stored value is kept and read by nothing now. **Differences:** the Dossiers tab's list (a module screen nothing draws since B4-4) has plain rows (name and status, search by name or alias) instead of the panel's rows; Diagnostics opened from the settings section shows no per-person relationship audit (it showed the panel's selected person). Code and tests removed are backed up in `scratch/backups/b4-5/`.
- **No more present cards under the reply, and no Megumin tab (B4-6).** NPC State no longer draws the present-cast cards in the chat under the latest reply, in the compact strip, or as a "Present NPCs" tab inside a Megumin block, and the setting **Present NPCs in chat** (Full cards / Compact strip / Only in Story Block / Off, stored as `presentCastDisplay` and `presentCastDisplayChosen`) is gone with its row, its watchdog and its observer of the chat. The present people are shown where they always were besides: the cast cards in the Cast and World panes, with their Mood, Condition and Look lines (same markup, same text). What went with it: the Story Block scene reader (`scenePresentAt`, the World State reader) and the NPC Inner Chatter bubbles beside the cards (Story Block's v1 registry that fed them is already gone, so nothing was shown; the scan no longer passes a scene to the merge and the result is byte-identical), the per-message `inlineCards` history is no longer written (the field stays in a saved chat and in branch copies, unread, until B4-10), and the public `renderInline` call and the `uiStatus` fields `presentCastDisplay`, `inlineEntries`, `inlineAnchors`, `mountedInlineAnchors`, `integratedMeguminBlocks`, `inlineObserver` and `inlineNeedsRepair`. The Cast and World panes still refresh after a reply, a message edit or a chat change (a small new part, `legacy/runtime-cast-refresh.js`, sends the same `npc-state-delta:cast-rendered` event, once, after a short wait). The `megumin_state` chat key is untouched (it is read nowhere in the code before or after), and reading a Megumin block for a dossier scan or import is unchanged. Backup: `scratch/backups/b4-6/`.
- **The old view is gone (B4-4).** Story Suite has one view. Boot no longer mounts the old host (`ui/legacy/`, about 17,000 lines with its CSS), so the bar above the message box, the story panel, the game view (Alt+G), the HUD strip, the cards, notes and Choices under replies, the old settings window and the "Send pending" note are no longer built, even hidden. **One behaviour went with them:** the hidden "Send pending" note still re-sent, once, a Send that SillyTavern ignored while the last reply was being finished; that re-send is gone, and Settings › Look › **Send pending note** has no effect now (open for a decision: rebuild it in the view or drop the row). **Removed with it:** Settings › Tools › Developer tools › **Use the old view** and the way back under Extensions (a device that chose the old view gets the view; the stored choice is ignored), the body class `ss-ui-next` and its hide sheet, the RPG's old screens (Party, Fight screen, the old New character window and first-send dialog, Export, Skills, the "Changed this reply" lines, the RPG settings page), the fight offer line under a reply, the Customize layout and blocks editor with the old Data modules card (dropped by D2), the layout renderer, the canGenerate notice (the hold is still told by a toast, D2) and the old view's Home card "Your character". `sdk.dialog.open` now answers null: there is no dialog mount. **Kept, now started at boot:** the RPG's fight pause that holds a Send, the board, the fight offer and the battle log in the replies run while the RPG is on (`modules/rpg/live/`, loaded only then) and the view reads them as before; the Inspector, the Diagnostics report and the console errors it keeps are built at boot (`core/boot/tools-parts.js`; the Inspector no longer adds a reply's refused lines from the old notes when the turn has none for it). The Story Suite drawer in Extensions is drawn by the view (title, status line, Settings, Open Story Suite; NPC State's settings stay in it). The bundled Nunito font moved to `ui/css/fonts.css`, and the page-level colour tokens the battle log, the Check card and NPC State's sheets read (`--ss-text`, `--ss-border`, … from SillyTavern's theme) moved to `ui/css/tokens.css`, unchanged. Two settings hints that said "set in the old view" now name Extensions › Story Suite. Code and tests removed are backed up in `scratch/backups/2026-11-18-retire-B4-4-view*`. Look's Panel style, Glass strength, Font and Text size now apply in the view (see Fixed); Send pending note is still listed and has no effect. The `ui.next` setting is left in place until B4-13; the leftover `storySuite.ui.oldView` browser key is removed at start.
- **The old view is gone (B4-4).** Story Suite has one view. Boot no longer mounts the old host (`ui/legacy/`, about 17,000 lines with its CSS), so the bar above the message box, the story panel, the game view (Alt+G), the HUD strip, the cards, notes and Choices under replies, the old settings window and the "Send pending" note are no longer built, even hidden. **One behaviour went with them:** the hidden "Send pending" note still re-sent, once, a Send that SillyTavern ignored while the last reply was being finished; that re-send is gone, and Settings › Look › **Send pending note** has no effect now (open for a decision: rebuild it in the view or drop the row). **Removed with it:** Settings › Tools › Developer tools › **Use the old view** and the way back under Extensions (a device that chose the old view gets the view; the stored choice is ignored), the body class `ss-ui-next` and its hide sheet, the RPG's old screens (Party, Fight screen, the old New character window and first-send dialog, Export, Skills, the "Changed this reply" lines, the RPG settings page), the fight offer line under a reply, the Customize layout and blocks editor with the old Data modules card (dropped by D2), the layout renderer, the canGenerate notice (the hold is still told by a toast, D2) and the old view's Home card "Your character". `sdk.dialog.open` now answers null: there is no dialog mount. **Kept, now started at boot:** the RPG's fight pause that holds a Send, the board, the fight offer and the battle log in the replies run while the RPG is on (`modules/rpg/live/`, loaded only then) and the view reads them as before; the Inspector, the Diagnostics report and the console errors it keeps are built at boot (`core/boot/tools-parts.js`; the Inspector no longer adds a reply's refused lines from the old notes when the turn has none for it). The Story Suite drawer in Extensions is drawn by the view (title, status line, Settings, Open Story Suite; NPC State's settings stay in it). The bundled Nunito font moved to `ui/css/fonts.css`, and the page-level colour tokens the battle log, the Check card and NPC State's sheets read (`--ss-text`, `--ss-border`, … from SillyTavern's theme) moved to `ui/css/tokens.css`, unchanged. Two settings hints that said "set in the old view" now name Extensions › Story Suite. Code and tests removed are backed up in `scratch/backups/2026-11-18-retire-B4-4-view*`. The old dossier panel (NPC State's Open dossiers in Extensions, B4-5) no longer gets the old view's glass tokens. Look's Panel style, Glass strength, Font and Text size now apply in the view (see Fixed); Send pending note is still listed and has no effect. The `ui.next` setting is left in place until B4-13; the leftover `storySuite.ui.oldView` browser key is removed at start.
- **Key people is gone (B4-9); "away" now works on the Follow star.** The Follow star (People) is the only list of people you follow; the older Key people list (its Pin / Unpin, its two settings: how many, narrator budget) is removed. **Kept, rebuilt on the star (AWAYFOLLOW):** the narrator still writes `+ away Name — what they are doing | back: <date>`, `~ away`, `! away Name — news` and `- away Name`, now for anyone it sends away, followed or not (no pin needed); the lines and their paragraph are taught only while someone is followed or away, so a chat with neither sends the same prompt as before. Each away person gets a line in the narrator's `[Elsewhere]` tail (was `[Key people]`). NPC State still holds an away person still (no meter drift, no count toward losing a stage, stable profile held through an automatic scan), gives Belonging's "left behind" pull, and tells the narrator once, with the dossier, what they did while away when they come back ("While away"). People › Elsewhere tiles show "back <date>", the latest news age and what an away person is doing; the World screen's Elsewhere section lists the people away and the followed people not in the scene (read-only). Someone who dies while away is left out of the `[Elsewhere]` tail, the away teaching and the Elsewhere rows (read from the people store each reply, nothing stored, so undoing the death brings the line back); `director.away` still names them, marked removed, so NPC State gives them no hold. An old reply's `- key Name | died` is read and dropped quietly (no Problem, no change). **Kept as before:** a followed person is never moved by the cast planner, never deleted by the stale pass (nor is anyone away), and keeps the ★ on present cards and the World's People cards. **Gone:** the status card's key-person lines (someone back, someone no longer a key person) and the pulse counting away people as off-screen. Under the hood: the Director part `away` (`modules/people/away/`, domain `away`, queries `director.away` and `director.away.view`) replaces `modules/cast/keypeople`; NPC State reads `director.away` and its memory query is `npc.awayMemory` (was `npc.keyAway`; the slice field `keyAway` keeps its name). Nothing is converted: a chat saved with Key people data keeps the old `keypeople` domain unread. Code and tests removed are backed up in `scratch/backups/b4-9/`.
- Settings › RPG and fights updates at once after a Difficulty or Custom change (its hint and editors), and says why a Custom number was refused.
- Settings › Look: the Panel style and Glass strength hints no longer mention Solid (the skin is gone); the Glass strength hint now says Follow SillyTavern ignores it.

### Removed

- **The v1 capture (B4-11a).** Story Block v1's envelope (`<!-- STORY_BLOCKS_V1 … -->`, `STORY_BLOCKS_V1_CUT`, `INVENTORY_BLOCK_V05`) is no longer read, hidden or kept out of the prompt: `core/capture/legacy.js` and its parts are gone, with the `LEGACY_FORMAT` Problem and the `legacy` mark on the hidden comment (fresh start: no chat from before 0.1.0 is read).
- **The v1 copy warning (B4-11b).** The warning that an old Story Block v1 is enabled beside the suite (`core/persist/v1-copy.js`, the `V1_COPY` Problem) is gone. An old Story Block already running when the suite starts is still told once, and the suite stays out of its way.
- **The RPG roll planner's last replay paths (B4-11c).** Nothing reads the planner's or the pre-port board's fight records (`rpg.resolve`, `rpg.noroll`) any more: their names, the planner's foe check (`vetOpen`, `open-parts/vet.js`), the cue wrappers that rebuilt such a record's text, their prelude branches and replay-guard route, and the quiet note on swiping or regenerating a reply whose fight was made before the port are gone. A fresh start reads no chat from before 0.1.0, and no later chat holds such a record; the foe slots a fight opens (`openFoes`) and the pre-call's name rule stay.
- **Narrated fights: the last file (B4-11e).** `modules/rpg/board/narrated-grammar.js` and its public `narratedFoeGrammar` are gone (combat is the fight board only). The two foe-kind lines it still made for the reply tail (while foes stand the story may only clear a group that left; after an ended fight it may also set a foe slot) now come from `board/prompt.js`, word for word as before.
- **For developers: the Story Director v1 arc planner is gone (B4-11f).** `modules/atlas/planner.js` and `modules/atlas/planner/` (the planner copied from Story Director v1: prompt, tracker summary, answer parser, plan checks, change ops, auto-plan rules) had no caller left but one function; the arc part (`modules/arc/plan`) plans, reads and checks plans itself. That function, `resolveRelative` (a plan's "in 2 days" or "tomorrow at dusk" written as a calendar date), moved to `modules/arc/plan/read-parts/relative.js`. Nothing a player sees changes.
- **Story Director checks no longer read a 2.1 chat's retired policy field (B4-11g).** The checks part (`modules/rpg/checks/`) dropped `policy.rbActors`, a link to a retired battle module that Story Director 2.1 and earlier kept in every chat, on its first write; fresh start means no such chat is read, so `domain-parts/shared-legacy.js` and its two uses are gone, and a checks slice holding `rbActors` is now an unknown field like any other. The 2.1.1 chat fixture and the test that replayed it are removed. The checks part itself stays: `? check`, `checks.roll`, `checks.actor`, `checks.policy` and Settings › Director › Checks are live (data modules and Assist call them).
- **The old Days screen id (B4-11h).** The Journal's old Days screen id `story-director/days` (a dialog kept for older layouts) is gone; the World screen's Journal is the one place the days show. The weather by rule stays: Story Director still picks a day's weather from the calendar and the region, and Settings › World and time keeps its "Weather comes from" row.

### Fixed
- **Cards under replies are drawn again.** Since the old view went, no module card showed under a reply: a companion who joins in Ask mode (the default) had no **Add to party**, **Edit first** or **No**, and **The story ends here** never appeared when your character died. The view now draws every module's card under the newest reply, above the row with the time and Suite: the join card, the death card and a block's own cards. Add to party and No are recorded with the reply (a swipe or delete takes them back, a swipe back or reload brings the answer back); Edit first opens the New character window filled in. The **Cards under replies** switch on Story Block's card in Settings › Start still turns them off, and its hint now says what it covers. There is no Later fold on these cards any more.
- **Pick what you take has its chips again.** A reply with a `? loot` line now shows its things under it while it is the latest reply: a **Loot** chip on the reply's row (with the number of things) opens the chips, open at first. Tick the ones you want and press **Take selected**. Once taken, the chip shows **Loot ✓** and folds; opened, it says **Taken.** The Bag tab has a **To pick up** card with every reply's loot not taken yet, newest first, with the same chips and button, so an older reply's loot is taken there. Each reply's loot is still taken once, so what you leave stays behind. A refused take (too heavy, already taken) says why under the button. The take is the existing `inventory.lootTake`, so a swipe, a delete, a rewind, a reload and a Re-read of the reply treat it as before. Since B4-4 removed the old view's reply cards, the loot had no place on screen and could never be taken. With Pick what you take off, or no loot laid out, the row and the Bag are as before.
- **People: someone who comes along keeps up (SFF4 #5).** `~ place Highmark Capital | with: Rika` left Rika's dossier place at where she was met ("Auvoth / Pale Market"), so after the scene moved on without her the People tab and her dossier gave the old place. A move's `with:` companions who have a dossier (not party members, not the dead) now get the new place as their dossier place, unless its stored place already is that place or a place inside it (read from the store: People does not show the old place of someone the scene has here); left behind, their last known place is where they were left. With world events on, the Director then counts it as the story's move for a pack person. It follows the move's own `placeFromMap` mark: a reply written before that mark writes no dossier place on Re-read; a reply written since that has a `with:` move gives the companions the new place when it is Re-read.
- **NPC State: lines about a pack person just met are no longer refused (SFF4 #1).** A pack person's dossier was made from the pack after the reply that met them, so `~ npc Torka | level: 9` in the reply with `+ present Torka` (and learns, keepsake, trust and goal lines in the reply after) were refused with "Torka has no record yet", and their values were lost. In lines mode such a line now makes the dossier from the pack entry inside the reply's own step (the pack's profile, Level, abilities, keepsakes, goal, secrets and first meters), then applies (a higher Level, a new ability or keepsake, a goal, a meter move). A swipe, a Re-read, a delete and a reload make the same dossier. `director.cast` takes `{ present: true }` to list, beside the met, the people not met yet whom the reply's own scene has present. A later line in the same reply that names them by an alias (`~ npc Bloodsong`) lands on the same person. Such a dossier asks no background fill-in (its profile is the pack's). The person is found as a listed person is: the full name or an alias, else the one person a first name, surname or role fits (`~ npc Torka` for Torka Vell); a short form that also fits someone with a dossier is nobody, and the line is refused. In the reply after the meeting, when NPC State's step has already made the meter entry but the dossier has not come yet, the line makes the dossier on that entry (one meter entry, under the same id). A pack person whose dossier the reader renamed is not made a second time, and a line whose name finds only someone else's meter entry goes on to that entry as before. Not made on this path: the first-meeting memory (the dossier made after the reply has it).
- **People: the dossier's Profile shows the Level, goal, abilities and keepsakes NPC State holds (SFF4 #7).** They were stored (a pack person's from the pack, later ones from `~ npc` lines) but no page drew them; only the RPG join card used the Level. Profile now has Level in Who (when one is stored), an **Aims** section with the Goal, and **Abilities** and **Keepsakes** sections (each name with its one line). Sections with nothing in them stay out.
- **NPC State: a line that stores a keepsake, an ability or a Level says so in its receipt (SFF4 #9).** `~ npc Rika | keepsake: + Guild licence — granted in Auvoth` stored the keepsake but the receipt read "Rika: nothing moved.". A line that stores a Level, an ability or a keepsake now names it with its value in place of "nothing moved": "Rika: keepsake Guild licence added.", "learns Snare craft", "keepsake … removed", "Level 12"; other fields the same line stored follow in the receipt words of the entry above ("Rika: keepsake Guild licence added, goal stored."); a look is named "look stored" (it read "appearance stored"); beside meters that moved or started they are added after them. A line that stores only other fields (place, goal, status, look, mood…) keeps that entry's "nothing moved; goal stored", which the Journal still counts as upkeep.
- **Time: a chat begun on a world with the built-in game keeps its era when a game is installed later (SFF4 #2).** Importing a game pack after such a chat began turned "CY 611, October 2" into "October 2, 611" at once (the era dropped, so one chat mixed both forms in the narrator's dates). The chat's built-in frame now keeps the world's built-in calendar while that world is selected, whatever game is selected since; `packs.calendar` takes `{ builtIn: true }` for it.
- **Settings: the sheet is readable over the open suite at 390 (SFF4 #12).** The Settings sheet drew its glass colour straight over the open suite, so on a phone the suite's card and rows read through it and its own text overlapped them (more so with SillyTavern's No Blur Effect on, which unsets every blur). As in the mock, the sheet's glass now lies on the sky backdrop (the panel colour over `--su-back` in one background), so nothing of the suite shows through it, at every width, dark and light, blur on or off; with Settings › Look › Panel background Off (the default) it lies on the suite's flat surface instead of the sky.
- **Scene: a move to a format 4 pack place keeps the region's name (SFF4 #3).** `~ place Bluewatch Village` took the pack place's region id (`bluewatch-reach`) into the scene, so the narrator's Place line and the receipt read "(village, bluewatch-reach, from the map)" and the weather, keyed by the region's words, rolled again on the same day in the same region. A region the map or pack gives by id is now stored by its name ("Bluewatch Reach"), as the pack's start place already was. The atlas's anchor (which reads a place the story made, such as a tavern, as the pack place it stands for) now matches a region by its id, name or alias, so a scene region kept by name still finds its pack places.
- **Story Director: the pulse no longer has a messenger ask the way to the place you stand in (SFF4 #6).** The town interruption "A messenger asks you where {place} is, standing in front of it." (at the start place it read "… where Bluewatch Village / Guild Office is, standing in front of it") is now "A messenger asks you the way to the nearest inn, then hurries off before you finish.", in the same slot, so rolls pick the same line number.
- **Settings › Packs: the suggestion card names a game that is not installed (SFF4 #11).** A world pack's `recommends.game` may now carry the game's `name` (optional, at most 80 characters); the card and the muted line read "This world suggests Ersveil rules" instead of the raw id while the game is not installed, and the id only when the world gives no name. The Ersveil world names its game (and examples/packs/ersveil.zip is rebuilt).
- Story › Director: with the plan hidden the tab printed "null" under the plan bar (an empty plan line was handed to the page as null). The line is gone, and with no plan yet (no arc and no thread) the tab shows "The Director has no plan yet; it plans after the opening." whether the plan is hidden or shown; an arc of its own counts as a plan, so it no longer shows that line. The Threads-off line still shows only with the plan shown.
- **People: a place's name keeps its capital (SFF4 #10).** The People tab's Elsewhere tiles lowered the first letter of every last place ("bluewatch Village", "auvoth / Pale Market"); now only a phrase is lowered to run on in the line (a leading little word followed by a lower-case word: "At the Gull" → "at the Gull", "The toll gate" → "the toll gate"), and a place's own name is shown as written ("The Glass Pits", "North Pass").
- Settings: a refused value says why under its row, and a number the engine held says so. A held number is redrawn with a note such as "Level cap is 1-99; kept 99." or "XP rate is 50-300 in steps of 25; kept 100." (RPG rows, the RPG Custom editors, the People page's number rows that hold to a set range (Dormant after, Refresh every and the like) and the Tools budgets name their range; Director, World, Money and Models numbers say "<row> was held to the range it allows; kept N."; an emptied box that comes back with a number says "<row> cannot be empty; kept N."). These refusals now give the reason instead of "That value cannot be saved." or "That value is not one this setting takes.": a Months part that does not read ("“nonsense” does not read as a month. Write each month as “Name: days” …"), Tempo outside 0.25-4, a word a choice row does not have ("Choose one of: …", on the RPG, World, Director and Look pages), an empty or non-number RPG number ("It takes a whole number, 1-99."). A store that refuses without a reason still says "That setting was not saved."
- Settings › People › Per-feeling stages: the Feeling choice, the At number and the Stage name sit on one line at one height (SillyTavern's own select margin had lifted the choice and its label), and the row says "feeling" throughout: the Feeling label, the row hint ("Named thresholds on one feeling") and the not-used note.

- A beat the story fires with `!` now counts as played in that same reply: Director shows it as played and the arc counts it ("1 of 2 beats") at once, instead of only after the next reply. The `!` line is kept in the quest history when the reply is read, so a swipe, a middle delete, a rewind or a reload takes it back or brings it back the same way.

- **One "new person" line in the prompt (SFW6).** The kinds list no longer teaches `+ person Name | look: … | flaw: …`; a new person is written as NPC State's `+ npc Name | role | look` and nowhere else (the prompt had both, and a `+ person` line made no NPC State record, so a later `~ npc` about them was refused). An old `+ person` line is still read and its name still checked; it makes no record.
- The off-screen cast helper's prompts are sent with the player's and the character's names filled in (they went out as `{{user}}` / `{{char}}`).
- The world-events prompt shows a template's summary with its place, region and realm filled in (it showed `{region}`).
- The narrator note's weather is cut at a whole word (it stopped mid-word: `thin and drif…`), and NPC State's own `meters started` / `nothing moved` lines no longer appear as a day's events in the digest.
- Now: a tracker with **Show on Now** switched on appears as a card on Now (it never did); a finished trip no longer stays under Today for weeks (the place row says where you are); a quest whose date has passed says "overdue" instead of "due now"; the coin change under This reply names its unit ("−3 copper"); a fresh chat shows one line instead of a blank page.
- Story › Trackers: the footer names one limit (28 blocks in a chat) instead of two numbers that disagreed with Settings › Packs. Story › Director: no "Rolled this reply ?" before the first roll, and no **End this arc** while there is no arc.

- **A portrait you upload from your device now stays.** ⋯ › Portrait › Upload from device kept the picture only in NPC State's memory, so the dossier, the list rings and Here kept the letter and a reload lost the picture. The upload is now saved once as its own file (named by its content, like a cast portrait) and the person's record names it; if the file cannot be written the picture is kept in the record as before.
- The portrait dialog (⋯ › Portrait) is now glass in the Story Suite look, dark or light, instead of the flat grey tools panel.
- Portrait messages say "NPC State" instead of "NPC State Delta".
- **Dossier (soak findings F21, F25, F27, F16, F17, F14, F8, F24).** Your own feelings (Respect, Loyalty, a preset's) now have their bar on the With page with their own range, and a built-in feeling that has reached a stage shows its stage word under its name; the stage you are at falls back to NPC State's cast row when the ladder query has no answer. The ⋯ menu's **Delete**, the top bar's **Edit** and the Death page's **Mark alive** now work through NPC State's own editor and dossier tools (the dossier closes once someone is deleted; a refusal is told under the top bar). **Merge** and **Inspector** stay hidden: NPC State has nothing to call for them. A person's goal now shows as **Wants** (behind the scene spoiler) on Now › Here and in the dossier, and the Hiding row is left out when nothing feeds it. Now › Here's **Doing** is the same stored status the dossier's Right now shows (it was always a dash). The dossier has no empty **Right now** card for someone with no place or status. An unstated gender is flagged once under the name (it read "gender not stated · gender not stated"). A quoted line in a person's speech, habits or other long text is kept whole when NPC State tidies it (`"Back room. Pay me tomorrow."` no longer becomes `"Back room.; Pay me tomorrow."`). Not fixed here: a custom feeling's moves and per-feeling reasons in the dossier's "Changes with you" list (NPC State records only the four built-in feelings' moves, one joined reason per reply).

- **Trip stop, save points and the Journal day (soak SFW7).** `~ trip stop` on the road now asks the narrator to write the `~ place` for where you are (the trip's own cue went with it, so the header, the routes line and the map kept the place you left). A save point's branch carries the list into the branch (Return here opened a branch whose card said "No save points in this chat yet"): it shows the point as "You are here", the others, and a row back to the chat you left. Save points are labelled with the message number SillyTavern shows (it counts from 0; Problems too) and the story date (a point made before this keeps its real date). The Calendar's day record: a quest written with `+ quest` is listed on the day it was taken; the day's line writes money in coins like the bag and the Journal's money row ("1 silver 5 copper in"); NPC State's own receipts ("People stored.", "…: meters started") are no longer events of the day; News rows read "Farms nailed shut (Bluewatch Reach) is now ongoing." without "Heard of World:". Director and Factions rows say things once: a pulse line without its own kind repeated, an agenda named for its owner without the name twice, a faction without "Seen in: …" beside the event chip that says the same. Not changed: the Coming up list still leaves out the trip's arrival and shown clocks.
- **Deleting a time skip moves later time back when a later reply set an earlier clock face on the day a jump landed on (soak W4).** `! time +2 days` followed by `~ time 6:30 am` (earlier than the landing time) was stored as a set minute, so deleting, swiping or editing an earlier reply that moved the clock (a `! time +3 weeks`) left the clock where it was. Such a face is now an anchored set with a new roll, `day` (Story Block replay rule h, `ops/anchored.js`): it keeps its place in the day and moves with the day, earlier than the clock before it too, and the chronicle's day walk applies the same rule. The same holds for a `~` face back over the world's fallback hour on the clock's own day. Replies told before this change keep their stored sets. Not fixed: a face back over the fallback hour across midnight (on the day before the clock's) stays a plain set, so deleting an earlier multi-day skip still leaves it; a jump in one reply, the early face in the next, then the jump deleted: the face keeps its day offset (the clock goes back to 6:30 am of the earlier day) where a fresh telling refuses it; and the day digests already written before a deleted multi-day jump (as before, for any jump).
- **A time line cut to the longest time jump, and a refused time line, give a Problems row (soak W11, W12).** `! time +6 weeks` with Longest time jump 30 moves 30 days and the Problems list now says so ("…moves the story time more than 30 days at once; it moved 30 days…"); `! time -3h`, a date before the story time and `~ time 25:00` give a row with the reason, not only a "(not used)" receipt. Each part of a joined line (`~ time … · ! time …`) gives its own reason. No prompt text changed.
- **Problems rows follow their reply after a middle delete (soak W12).** A row is kept with its reply's record ID, so after a delete earlier in the chat (and after a reload) it names the reply's new number instead of the old one. A row whose reply's record is not shown (a swipe not shown) keeps the number it was made with.
- **NPC State: names.** In lines mode, a line may use a person's first name, surname, a title with a surname, or their role (`~ npc Hesk`, `~ npc Captain Dalla`, `~ npc the Watch-Captain`). When only one person fits, the line is about them. When two people fit, the line is refused. `+ npc Hesk` is refused while Hesk Dalla is the only Hesk, so no second "Hesk" is made; `+ npc Hesk Varn` still makes someone new. In lines mode, `~ npc Name | aka: another name` keeps that name as an alias, and the one-pass rules teach it in one sentence.
- **NPC State: death is final.** In lines mode, `~ npc`, `~ memory`, milestone and note lines about a person who has died are refused. A `~ npc` line of theirs keeps any dossier labels it has, and the frozen fields stay frozen as before. `+ present` cannot bring a dead person back into the scene, whether the death came from the story or from a scan.
- **NPC State: ties.** In lines mode, a `~ tie` whose `with:` names nobody with a record is refused. A tie line written with a first name is stored under the full name. Ties from the story's tie lines are now edges of `people.ties` (People › Map) and tie lines on the dossier's Ties page. A dossier line that a key relationship already covers is kept as it was.
- **NPC State: who is here.** The People list and the dossier count anyone the scene lists as here, and show no old "away" place while they are. With one-pass on and hosted, after each live reply the scene's own list also marks who is present and when they were last seen. A person who arrives has their old "away" place cleared, unless you locked it. Before this, every record stayed "not here, never seen", so the catch-up Refresh never ran and "Not seen lately" listed the wrong people. `people.list` also follows your Dormant after setting (10–999) instead of a fixed 30.
- **NPC State: hidden notes.** A note that opens at a feeling's value ("Opens at Trust 2") keeps that threshold when it is stored. Before this, the note vanished from the dossier once it opened. A `+ npc` stub's reply stamp also reaches NPC State's memory now, so the person's fill-in, and with it their first hidden notes, runs as designed.
- **NPC State: Manual only.** While Who gets a dossier is Manual only, a `+ npc` line makes no dossier.
- **NPC State: model input.** The scan and Refresh evidence windows leave out Story Suite's hidden comment with the reply's change lines, so the lines are no longer sent as story text. The Relationships rule now names, after trust, affection, desire and tension, those of your own meters that have a rule for the story (the ones the Meter rules line already names). A meter with no rule still adds no text, so with none the rule is unchanged.
- Settings › People: a refused Portraits Prompt or Leave out text box keeps what was typed beside the note (it was drawn again from the stored value).
- Settings › People: the rows and the off-hint say "feelings" like the module switch: Custom feelings, Feeling presets (up to 12), Per-feeling stages, "Feeling presets and rules is off".
- Developer: the UI walk gives the Coins row a line it reads and accepts a number the engine holds to its range (Most output per People call 1 becomes 128) when the page, the store and a fresh Settings agree; it also runs for a change inside a `modules/**/settings*` folder.

- **Story › Director: Show the plan now shows the plan.** The button saved your choice but the Story tab never read it, so nothing opened. It now opens the beats, clocks, hooks, news, off-screen agendas and secrets at once. With nothing to show it says one line: "The Director has no plan yet; it plans after the opening."; with Story Director's Threads switch off it says that switch hides beats, clocks, agendas and secrets.

- Settings › Models › Calls: **Most output per People call** is now a number box (0 keeps the built-in limits; otherwise 128 to 15000, cut as the engine cuts it) that stores NPC State's `scannerMaxOutputTokens`; it was a read-only line that sent you to the old view. **Repair** and **Fallback** stay as two switches (they differ: Repair runs only when a reply has no change block, on the Quick connection, and may write coin and items; Fallback runs after each reply on the Background connection, adds time, place, comings and goings and quest progress the lines missed, never coin or items), and each hint now says when it runs; the Fallback hint wrongly said it ran only when the whole block was missing.
- **Device preferences now survive a reload.** Panel background, Developer tools and the folds were only held in memory in SillyTavern, so a reload reset them; they are now kept in this browser's local storage (one key, `storySuite.ui.fold`). With no usable storage they are kept for the page only, as before.
- **Settings › Tools shows current values.** Flipping Developer tools redrew the page with the budget numbers (Notes per reply, Share of the context, Change lines) from when Settings opened; it now reads them again on every draw.
- **The World tab's map has north at the top.** A pack's y grows north, but the tab drew y straight down the screen, so a pack with a frozen north (such as the Ersveil sample) showed it at the bottom. The map now flips y (and honours a map that says north is down or east is left, as the old atlas widget did); places, roads, region names, the trip line, pan and zoom follow. The land and its grid grow to hold every place.
- Settings › Look works in the new view: Panel style (Glass or Follow SillyTavern), Glass strength, Font and Text size now change the Story Suite panel at once and stay after a reload; Reset Look puts the Glass look back. The panel now opens in Glass by default (light or dark from your SillyTavern theme) instead of always taking SillyTavern's colours.
- The Send pending note shows above the message box in the new view again (sends when the story is ready, not sent yet, sent); its fight-held state stays hidden there, as the Fight tab shows the fight. The Send pending note switch turns it off.
- A companion who joins under a Custom "Stats start at" above 5 no longer loses stats (its best stat was held to 10).
- **Desktop layout (owner's report, 18 Nov).** On a window wider than 820 px the suite was a narrow 820 px panel over the dimmed chat. It now fills the window as mock v7's Desktop does: the backdrop reaches both edges and the card, tabs, page and dossier stay in one 820 px column in the middle. At 820 px and under nothing moves. Layouts that switch at a width now read the window, as the mock's do (from 900 px World › Map shows the trip beside the map).
- **The Suite button no longer covers SillyTavern's swipe counter.** On the latest reply the row under the text now ends above the band where SillyTavern draws ‹ 1/1 › (also in themes such as Moonlit Echoes that move it), at every width and count.
- **Selected buttons are readable.** Text on the accent colour (a selected Settings choice such as "Glass", "Nunito" or "Normal", the primary button, the Journal's today, the World trip's current leg, the fight's chosen mark) no longer gets SillyTavern's glow, and its colour is picked for the accent in use, dark or white, at least 4.5:1. In Glass light it is white (the mock's dark text is 3.4:1 on the violet).
- **Settings follow the RPG switch.** With the RPG off, the RPG and fights page (and its Bag and gear switches) is not listed; switching the RPG on in Start lists it again at once with its stored values, and off takes it away. A module card's "Settings ›" shows only when the module has a page: Story Block has none.
- **Portrait settings can be edited.** People › Portraits: Prompt and Leave out are boxes of several lines, Composition a line, Style preset a list of the built-in styles and your saved presets, Prompt format a list, and the mood, place and gallery rows switches, all showing what portraits use now and saved as the old portrait panel saved them (editing Prompt or Leave out moves the style to your custom preset). Saved portrait presets has Save as a new preset and Delete this preset.

### Removed
- **NPC State's branch journal (B4-10a).** NPC State no longer keeps its own copy of the dossiers for each reply (checkpoints, the rollback journal, a branch's root snapshot, the history of the old in-chat cards), no longer rolls the dossiers back itself after a swipe, an edit or a deleted message, and no longer copies a parent chat's dossiers into a new branch. The store's timeline already does this: on a swipe, swipe back, middle delete, rewind, reload or Re-read the people come from the story's store, as before. Gone with it: the two Advanced switches in NPC State's own settings, **Rewind dossiers on middle deletion** and **Rescan changed branches** (a saved choice is dropped), and the Branch reconciliation section of NPC State's diagnostics. A saved chat state that still holds checkpoints or the rollback journal loads with them dropped. Only if the Story Suite host refuses NPC State or never gets ready (NPC State then works on its own) does this change what a reader sees: the dossiers stay as they are after a swipe or a delete instead of being rolled back.
- **NPC State: the Short one-pass instruction switch** (Settings › People) and the JSON-block path that served a chat without Story Block. The suite asks the narrator for change lines only; a saved choice is dropped. Without Story Block nothing is asked of the narrator and replies are scanned in the ordinary way.

- The fixed "The four feelings" row on Settings › People (nothing could be set there); the README's NPC State section explains the four feelings.

- **The format 3 sample world (pack format 4: format 3 is obsolete).** `examples/packs/ersveil-world` and `examples/packs/ersveil-world.zip` are gone; the shipped Ersveil is the format 4 pair alone (`examples/packs/ersveil` and `ersveil-game`, in `examples/packs/ersveil.zip`), edited directly. With them went the converter's `--ersveil [--check]` rebuild and its Ersveil preset; `node scripts/packs/convert-3to4.mjs <pack.json> <out dir>` still converts any format 3 pack.

### Pack format 4: worlds and games

- **Pack format 3 is no longer read (pack format 4, X1).** A pack with no `format`, or with a whole number below 4, is refused with `FORMAT_OLD` ("convert it with the Studio or `scripts/packs/convert-3to4.mjs`"); above 4 is still `FORMAT_NEWER`, any other value `BAD_FIELD`, and the check runs first, so an old pack gets that one error. `requires.storyBlock` is refused. Removed with it: the format 3 cast checker (`packs/cast.js`), `castSkillsOf` (`packs/cast-skills.js`), `castGearOf` and the `packs.castGear` query (a person's skills now come only from their NPC State record, their gear only from their keepsakes), the top-level `ripples` table (`packs.ripples` still answers, always null; `packs.rippleChoice` gives the authored fallback), and the format 3 warnings. The cast planner request and the rumour tail now get a format 4 world cast person's `role` and the opening of their `history` (new model-facing data: the gate that read format 3 fields was empty for every format 4 world). `partyAdd` lost its `cast` argument. The Packs list tag now comes from the pack's kind ("World pack" or "Game pack"). A format 4 pack validates to the same bytes as before (checked over every shipped format 4 pack and fixture). Tests of format 3 behaviour were removed (backups in `scratch/backups/pf4x1-tests`), the rest converted to format 4. Merged with S1: a format 3 entry stored before X1 stays listed with the `FORMAT_OLD` message as its problem (on Settings › Packs it reads "Old format: remove it or convert it" with Remove and no switch, under Packs, or under Older packs (format 3) beside a world or game) and the reader's own rank label, money and items do not count for it; S1's format 3 tests now check that, on format 4 packs and a stored format 3 entry.
- **Settings › Packs: pick a world and a game (pack format 4).** With a format 4 pack installed, the Packs page now has a World picker (None or one world) and a Game picker (Built-in or one game pack; add-on packs are always on and never a choice) instead of a switch per pack. A world that suggests a game you are not using gets a card, "This world suggests <game>", with Use it and Keep mine; nothing is applied without a tap. Under the pickers one line per kind of problem in the pair (a start date the calendar lacks, world events that never fire, birthdays dropped, name lists unused) says what is switched off, and play goes on. Worlds and games, add-on packs, and each data module's pack are listed by name. Settings › Start has a new "You and your start" group: Rank label, Starting money (the smallest coin) and Starting items (one a line), which come before the game pack's and say what blank means. They apply to new chats (a chat keeps the start it began with) and only while a format 4 pack is installed. With no format 4 pack installed the Packs page is unchanged. Developer: `packs.pairing` and `packs.own` queries, `packs/own.js`, a `lines` row type.
- **The repo's own format 3 material in pack format 4 (C2).** The two Ersveil add-ons, `examples/packs/ersveil-factions` and `ersveil-survival`, are now format 4 game packs (3.0.0, data modules only, same ids and same `pack.<id>/<slug>` module ids, so a chat that has them keeps its state); they are never selected and never displace the selected game, and their trackers are offered in every chat. The authoring kit's tiny world (`authoring-kit/examples/tiny-world`) is a format 4 world pack with a start place. For the tests: the Riverside fixture is a format 4 pair (`tests/packs/fixtures/riverside-starter.json` and `.game.json`), its format 3 copy moved, byte for byte, to `tests/fixtures/packs/riverside-starter-3.json`, which tests read (through `sample` and `sampleText`), while the format 4 pair is read by `riverside-format4.test.js`; the Bluewatch starter is re-pinned once, as a format 4 pair (`tests/fixtures/director/ersveil-bluewatch.pack.json` and `.game.json`, installed with `installBluewatch`; owner question 6), with the old file kept as `tests/fixtures/packs/ersveil-bluewatch-1/pack.json`. The format 4 pair has the old places, routes, items, calendar, money and start; legacy events, quests, creatures, shops, recipes, races, talents, skills and presets have no format 4 home and are not carried over. `scripts/packs/convert-3to4.mjs` now also gives places an `id`, maps place and route kinds to engine classes, names routes that join the same two places, takes a `{ item }` or text start item, and adds a `start.scene.place` record as a place of the world. The QA replay oracle knows the `frame` domain.
- **Ersveil in pack format 4 (C1).** The shipped Ersveil is now two format 4 packs made from the new Ersveil (the format 3 sample world, `examples/packs/ersveil-world`) by a new converter, `scripts/packs/convert-3to4.mjs`: the world pack `examples/packs/ersveil` (4.0.0: lore, the map of 320 places and 352 routes, 30 regions, realms, markets, conditions, powers, scores, 103 world events, templates, the 31 people and the world's start: Bluewatch Village / Guild Office on 1 October CY 611, 07:00), which recommends the game pack `examples/packs/ersveil-game` ("Ersveil rules" 1.0.0: the Gregorian months with Ersveil's seasons, copper/silver/gold, climate weather, the 14 name cultures, the Guild rank, the RPG rules with `formula: "v1"`, the three data modules and 40 starting copper). `examples/packs/ersveil.zip` now holds the world pack, its portraits, the game pack as `game.json` and `CONVERSION-NOTES.md`; importing it installs both (as for any zip, a world or game is selected only when none of its kind was chosen before). Each region gets the lead's proposed `climate` (25 of 30; the other 5 read as temperate), listed with the reasons in `CONVERSION-NOTES.md` for the author to confirm. Ersveil's four general weather tables are the game's `temperate` tables; its 80 region-keyed weather tables cannot be kept (a game pack has no regions) and are listed in the notes instead. The data modules name themselves `pack.ersveil-game/…` now. The converter also converts any format 3 pack (`node scripts/packs/convert-3to4.mjs <pack.json> <out dir>`) and lists what has no format 4 home. The old format 3 Ersveil (2.x, with its lorebook and zip) is a frozen test fixture, `tests/fixtures/packs/ersveil-3`, read by tests only. The format 3 sample world it was made from has since been removed (below); the pair is edited directly.
- **Pack format 4, a fight shares the Level with NPC State (R2).** When a fight settles (the one call that pays XP and practice, RPG on), the Level on a hero's sheet and the Level in their NPC State record are kept in step, in that reply's change set: a Level NPC State holds that is higher than the sheet's raises the sheet first (held to the sheet's level cap, one point to spend for each level gained, as a rise by XP gives; it never lowers the sheet), then the fight's XP is paid, and a level the XP gained is written to the person's record (never lowered, so the record's Level only goes up; a record with no Level is given the sheet's). A hero is matched to their record by name or alias, and one with no single matching record is left alone. A swipe, a swipe back, an edited Re-read (grown or shrunk), a middle delete, a rewind and a reload agree. **Not built:** a skill learned or a keepsake gained or lost on the sheet is not written back as an NPC State ability or keepsake (nothing in the award or practice step sees one), a level the narrator's own lines give a sheet outside a fight settle is not raised until the next fight pays that person; a level the join made or the reader's own edit of the sheet is not written to NPC State either; and the sheet is not made when an ally or a named foe first appears on the fight board.
- **Pack format 4, the RPG pull: a joining person's sheet comes from their NPC State record (R1).** When a person joins (a `+ party` line, the Ask card's Add or Edit first, or People › Add to party), their NPC State record builds the sheet: the level is their Level (held to the level cap: Settings, then the game pack, then 99), else the join line's class as before; the best stat is the line's `best:`, else a fixed keyword table over their role, background and abilities (the same record always gives the same sheet); each ability, in order, becomes a signature skill (up to the game pack's `rpg.pull.abilities`, default 4, at most 6), its type, stat and resource from a fixed keyword table over its name and description, its rank one under the highest the level allows; one stance skill (Guard, Evade or Rally, by the best stat) is added unless an ability already is a stance or `rpg.pull.stance` is false. Their keepsakes that name pack equipment are given as gear (gear on); the rest stay prose. The card's level row and "Edit first" show the same level, best stat and skills as Add makes. A person with no abilities keeps the format 3 cast skills and gear the packs still give, and a person with no record is built exactly as before. The fight board reads a foe's stated level from the built-in Level first (a custom "Level" field still reads when there is none). Read from the reply's own prefix, so a swipe, a swipe back, an edited Re-read, a middle delete, a rewind and a reload agree. Each ability takes a free slot of its kind (the level's combat slots, three utility ones), the stance only a free combat slot; an ability left out is named in Problems. **Not built:** the sheet is not made when an ally or a named foe first appears on the fight board, a new NPC State ability is not offered as a sheet skill later, and the sheet writes only its Level back (R2, previous entry).
- **Pack format 4, weather by climate (developer).** The Director's weather by rule now comes from the climate of where the story stands: a place's own `climate`, `underground` for an underground place, else its region's, else temperate, by season, from new built-in tables for nine climates (temperate is the table it always had). A selected game's `weather` can replace the lines for a climate, read its season-only tables (style `tables`) or leave the weather to the narrator (style `story`). The seasons come from the chat's frame's calendar. A chat whose frame began with another game keeps built-in climate weather. A chat with no pack or with format 3 weather tables is unchanged. Not yet: the Weather comes from row does not list Climate.
- **Pack format 4, world events through the chat's calendar (developer).** The daily world step now takes the season of a day from the calendar the chat runs on (the game's `calendar.seasons` from the chat frame, else the selected game's, else the four built-in quarters), so Ersveil's winter is December to February and another game's winter is its own. On a chat's first world step an event whose `date:` anchor is not on that calendar, or whose season the calendar lacks, is Lapsed with one Problems line, and a template with such an anchor is left out. A game's own season names are read as anchors, as the pack checker reads them. A chat with no format 4 world or game behaves as before, except that an impossible date such as `date:612-02-31` now lapses instead of waiting forever.
- **Pack format 4, the calendar from the chat frame (developer).** The story clock's calendar is now the game's: a chat with a frame reads the calendar the frame recorded at its start (months, era, leap years, week), so a game swapped in Settings later does not move its dates, and a frame of the built-in game stays Gregorian; a chat with no frame reads the selected pack's calendar as before. The Calendar choice `pack` is renamed `game` (a stored `pack` is read as `game`; the help says "game pack"), and the game calendar's `leapYears` and `week` are used. The start date is the selected world's `start.date`, and its era comes before the game calendar's (the frame and `packs.calendar` carry it); the reader's own Custom era still wins.
