Chats with no format 4 world or game selected are byte for byte as they were. A world played with the built-in game starts on its own date and era on the Gregorian calendar.
- **Pack format 4, money from the chat frame (developer).** A chat's currency is now the reader's own Currency setting, else the currency its frame recorded when the chat began, else (a chat with no frame) the selected game pack's, else gold, so a game pack swapped in Settings no longer changes the money of a running chat that has a frame. A frame of the built-in game holds no currency, so such a chat stays on gold. The Price scale reads the setting, else the selected game's `economy.scale`, else 1, as before (it is not in the frame). Chats with no format 4 world or game selected are unchanged.
- **A card's world and game, and a zip's game.json (pack format 4).** A character card may now carry one world pack and one game pack. The Story panel shows one notice for the pair, and one Install installs both and selects both as the chat's world and game (a trackers-only game pack is installed but never selected); "Not now" is remembered for the pair; a second world or game on the card is not offered and is noted in Diagnostics. A world pack's `.zip` may also hold a `game.json`: importing the zip installs both, and a game the checker refuses leaves the world installed with a line saying which file was not imported. Format 3 cards and zips behave exactly as before.
- **Pack format 4, the chat frame (developer).** A chat with a format 4 world or game selected now records, in a deferred `frame` domain of its own state, the world and game it began with (id, version and a hash of each) and the game's calendar and currency, written by a `beforeGenerate` step on the first reply that has none; the query `packs.frame` reads it. The reader's command `packs.frameUse` (the act "Use the current game in this chat") makes the next reply write it again from the world and game selected then. When the selected pack has the id of the frame's but a different hash, one quiet Problem says it changed since the chat began (adding "start a new chat to be safe" for a different major version). Swipes, a swipe back, a middle delete, a rewind to after it, a reload and an edited Re-read (grown or shrunk) keep it as it is; a rewind to before it removes it, and the next reply writes a new one (from the game selected then while the reader has written one message, else the built-in game). A chat that had begun before a world or game was selected records the built-in game at its next reply, so a pack installed later moves nothing in it; with nothing selected no frame is written and the chat is byte for byte as before. The calendar, weather, money and world events read the frame (entries above). No button sends `packs.frameUse` yet.
- **Pack format 4, the RPG level cap and game ranks (developer).** The level cap is now read in the order Settings, then the selected game pack, then 99: a Level cap set below 99 wins over the game pack's `rpg.levelCap` (before, the pack's cap won). 99 is the setting's default, so it counts as not set and the game pack's cap applies. Ranks and the player rank come from the selected game pack through `packs.content` (nothing changed in how they are read). `packRulesOf` lost its dead branches: a pack cannot give numbers or a formula word other than v1, so it returns only `{ pack }`; `rulesFromSettings` and `packInForce` no longer look at a pack's numbers (always false). Combat numbers and the rolls are unchanged. A member the store already holds at the same or a newer version is kept as it is (never downgraded), still selected, and the notice says so; a zip's `game.json` must be a game pack with an id of its own.
- **Settings › Tools: Export settings as a game pack (pack format 4).** A new Game pack card on the Tools page saves the settings now in force as a format 4 game pack file (`my-settings.json`), checked by the game validator before it is offered. Today it holds your money (Currency name, plural, short form and coins, and the Money scale) and your RPG level cap, each only when you have changed it from the built-in game; with nothing changed it says there is nothing to export. Other settings (calendar, weather, names, tempo, and the rank label and starting money and items of Settings › Start) are not exported. Developer: `sdk.registerCardSettings` takes an optional `sections` (`{ <game content key>: { read(), apply(value) } }`, one owner per section; an owner with sections only may leave `keys`, `read` and `apply` out) or `loadSections` (an async function giving the same object, awaited once by the registry's `loadSections()`; Story Block's own money and RPG sections come this way, so the page start reaches neither their code nor the pack checker: the export fetches them when you press the button), and `packs/game-export.js` builds a pack from them (`buildGamePack`) and hands a game pack's sections back (`applyGamePack`). A card never reaches a section. **Nothing in Settings imports a game pack file yet** (a game pack is installed on the Packs page); `applyGamePack` is tested export, validate, re-import without loss and is not wired to a button.
- **Pack format 4, the store and the selected world and game (developer).** An installed format 4 pack keeps its `kind` in the store, and `storySuite.selected` (`{ world, game }`) says which world and which game a chat reads: `packs.packs()` now gives the enabled format 3 packs, the selected world, the selected game, and the data modules and meter presets of every other game pack (a game pack that holds only those is an add-on and is never selected). The first world or game installed is selected when nothing was chosen before; `packs.select(kind, id)` and the query `packs.selected` read and change it, and `setEnabled` on a format 4 pack selects or clears it. The start reads the world's `start.place` and `start.world` and the game's `start.money` and `start.items` (an item is matched to the selected world's items by name or alias, else made as a plain `story/` item); the calendar's start comes from the world's `start.date` (a month or day the calendar lacks gives day 1 of month 1). Every reader of `packs.packs()` (the calendar, weather, money scale, rank, data modules, meter presets and the world's start state) answers from the selection; a chat that has begun takes its calendar, money and weather from its frame (F1). With a selected game the weather reader is given only the game's season-only tables of style "tables" (a table with no region matches every region); climate tables are kept apart for the climate reader (T2).
- **Pack format 4, the pairing check (developer).** `checkPairing(world, game)` in `packs/validate.js` compares a world pack's start date, `date:` and season anchors, cast birthdays and `names` culture regions with a game pack's calendar, seasons and names (or the built-in game when `game` is null). It returns `{ errors, warnings, info }` and never blocks: each entry names the part to switch off (an event that never fires, a start date that falls back, a dropped birthday, an unused culture), and an info line says when the world recommends a different game. Settings › Packs (S1) shows its lines, and a chat's first reply lists the start date, birthday and culture lines in Problems.
- **Pack format 4, the game pack's checks (developer).** A format 4 game pack's `calendar` (months, era, holidays as before, plus `leapYears`, `week` and `seasons`, with no `start`), `weather` (`style` and tables keyed by climate; `region` is refused), `start` (`money` and `items`), `pacing` (`tempo` 0.25 to 4) and `rpg.pull` (`abilities` 1 to 6, `stance`) are now checked and kept in the normalized pack, and `rpg.playerRank.module` must name a data module of the same pack. Format 3 packs are read exactly as before (same output for every example and fixture). The calendar, weather, start, money and RPG sections are read as the entries above say; `pacing.tempo` is checked and kept, but nothing reads it yet.
- **Pack format 4, the world pack's checks (developer).** A format 4 world pack may now give `climate` on a region or a place (one of ten words), `start.date` (era, year, month number, day, hour, minute) and `start.place` (the id of one of its places), and a cast person may give `birthday` ("10 27" or "Icewane 27"). `content.start` refuses any other key, with a pointer for the format 3 ones (`scene`, `inventory`). Because a world pack has no calendar of its own, its `date:` and season anchors are no longer compared to the built-in calendar; the pairing check reads them. A retired format 3 cast field says what replaced it. Format 3 packs are read exactly as before.
- **Pack format 4, the envelope (developer).** The pack checker also reads format 4: `kind` ("world" or "game"), `recommends`, the format 4 top-level fields and the `content` keys each kind may hold, with a "belongs in a … pack" pointer for a key of the other kind. `requires.storyBlock` is refused in format 4. Format 3 and older packs are read as before.
- **NPC State: Level, Abilities and Keepsakes fields (developer, pack format 4).** A person record can now hold a Level (a whole number from 1 to 99), Abilities and Keepsakes (each a list of a name and a one-line description). Abilities hold at most 16 and Keepsakes at most 12; names are cut to 60 characters, descriptions to 200, and a name that repeats (any case) keeps its first entry. A person without them is stored exactly as before. The dossier editor has an Abilities row and a Keepsakes row (one "Name — description" per line), and a Level row only while the RPG feature is on; with RPG off a Save keeps the stored Level. The `npc.dossier` query returns them. The world cast seeds them, the narrator writes them, and the RPG pull reads them (entries above).
- **NPC State: the world cast seeds the person record (developer, pack format 4).** At a person's first meeting NPC State now reads the format 4 world cast names: `role`, `species`, `gender`, `age` (a number or text), `apparentAge`, `forms`, `homeBase`, `status`, top-level `goal` (it wins over `start.goal`), `level`, `abilities`, `keepsakes`, `keyRelationships` and `ties` (cast ids are written as the people's names; an id the cast does not have is skipped). Ties become the person's tie tags in the notes (only with the Notes feature on, like tastes and drives). A taste `{ item, like, why }` now seeds as a like or a dislike with `why` as its source (before, the sign was lost). A figure without `level` seeds no Level. Abilities and Keepsakes past NPC State's limits (16 and 12) are not seeded; the meeting reply's Problems list names the left-out ones. The person's place is where they were met (`start.place` when that is empty). A Level, Ability, Keepsake, status or key-relationship edit made before the meeting reply is kept, like the other seeded fields. An entry that carries `kind` (a format 4 entry) no longer has the format 3 names read: `about`, `fields` (and the "People" field) and `homeBase` from `start.place`. The narrator's lines, the teaching text and the RPG are not changed yet.
- **NPC State: Level goes up only, and the narrator can write abilities and keepsakes (developer, pack format 4).** A stated `~ npc Name | level: N` line now sets the person's built-in Level (it is taught and read only while RPG is on, and never lowers it, so the old "locked custom field" and "rank text in the field" notes are gone). New lines `~ npc Name | learns: <ability> — <one line>`, `~ npc Name | keepsake: + <name> — <one line>` and `~ npc Name | keepsake: - <name>` write the built-in Abilities and Keepsakes (always taught and read, RPG or not; names unique without regard to case, at most 16 abilities and 12 keepsakes; a refused line is a note on the changes card). They are written in the reply's change set, so a swipe, a Re-read, a middle delete, a rewind and a reload undo and replay them. The rules sentence for them is sent on every reply, so the cached rules text changes once. The Level custom field is no longer added while RPG is on; a Level custom field you configure yourself still works as before. The fight board reads the built-in Level first (R1).
- **NPC State: the party sheet no longer overwrites a person's Level on a scan (developer, pack format 4).** The scan-merge step that set an active party member's stored Level to the sheet's (`applySheetLevels`), the matching strip of the scanner's own Level (`withoutSheetLevels`), the "from the party sheet" mark on the dossier's Level, and the party levels the scan and the targeted passes passed to the merge are removed (`sheet-levels.js` is gone). The built-in Level only goes up (previous entry). A module's own `people.fields` command already takes the story facts the sheet will write back (`level`, `learns`, `keepsake`); the RPG writes the Level back at a fight's settle (R2).
- **NPC State: the world cast's people review fixes (developer, pack format 4, PEOPLEFIX).** (1) A world cast entry's key relationships and ties to someone not met yet are no longer lost at the first meeting: NPC State keeps them by cast id only (`castFrom.links`, never shown or sent to the narrator) and names them at read once that person has a dossier (`cast-links.js`; worked out from the stored people, so a swipe, a rewind, a middle delete or a reload shows what the head holds; nothing is written back). They reach the dossier (`people.dossier`, the old view's ties) and the narrator's bonds; a line the person already holds for that name is not repeated. (2) The narrator's dossier block names each present person's abilities and keepsakes (whole names) and, while RPG is on, their Level (`level: N`); a mentioned, absent person's line carries Level while RPG is on. A person with none of the three is sent exactly as before. (3) A chat from before the built-in Level kept it as the custom field "Level": while `level` is absent, a whole number there (any case) is the Level for "only up" (the record, the narrator's line and the store's writer read it alike; the custom field is left as it is). A format 3 pack whose `fields.Level` is a whole number now also seeds the built-in Level. (4) A pack's apparent age in words ("looks older") is kept as written while the field still is those words. (5) Without a meeting place, a format 4 person's location is the starting place's name (from the pack's places), never its id; with no name, none. (6) The static prompt golden carries N3's abilities and keepsakes sentence (its only change), and a test pins the runtime's Level switch to the RPG probe.

### Pack format 4: review fixes (PF4FIX)

- **A pack installed later no longer re-dates a running chat (REVPF4 3).** A chat that began before any world or game was selected records the built-in game at its next reply, so installing or picking a world or game afterwards leaves its calendar, clock and money as they were; a new chat takes the selection at its start. A chat with no world or game selected writes no frame and is byte for byte as before. To be exact: while nothing is selected no frame is written (the chat reads the built-in game live); the built-in game is recorded at the next reply once the reader has written more than one message by the time a world or game becomes selected, and until that reply the chat's calendar reads the new game live.
- **A world with the built-in game starts on its own date (REVPF4 4).** The built-in game is Gregorian: the world's `start.date` and era seed the clock on the Gregorian calendar (February 29 only in a leap year, else January 1), and only while the world is the one the chat began with. The pairing check names "the built-in Gregorian calendar" in its lines.
- **"Each is also listed in Problems" is true (REVPF4 5).** The reply that writes a chat's frame lists, as quiet Problems, a start date that falls back, a dropped birthday and an unused names culture, in the pairing check's words, once.
- **A card never downgrades a pack (REVPF4 6).** A card's world and game install only the members that are new or newer; one the store holds at the same or a newer version is kept, still selected, and the notice says "Kept Ersveil 2.0.0 (the card has the older 1.0.0)."
- **A zip's game.json is checked (REVPF4 10).** It must be a game pack with an id of its own; a world there, or a game with the world's id, is not installed and the world stays as it was.
- **World events on a game's own seasons fire (REVPF4 11).** The daily step reads an anchor on any season name, as the pack checker does; a season the chat's calendar lacks still lapses with its line.
- **The changed-pack note comes once for each chat (REVPF4 12).** "Ersveil changed since this chat began" is not given again with every swipe in the same session, and every chat that began on the changed pack gets its own note.
- **Settings › Packs as the mock draws it (REVPF4 15).** The World picker lists None last; no "by <author>"; an add-on names its data modules ("Reputation data module; offered in every chat, beside any game"); a game's own calendar says its months ("an 8-month calendar", "a calendar" for the twelve real months); a data module's line is "from <pack>". A new game's Start hints show after a pick (the settings overlay refreshes a page's rows in place).
- **The RPG pull fits the slots (REVPF4 16).** Each ability takes a free slot of its kind (the level's combat slots, three utility ones), the stance only a free combat slot, and a join names an ability left out in Problems ("Ann's Old lore has no free utility skill slot.").
- **An old-format entry says so (REVPF4 17).** A format 3 pack stored before format 4 shows "Old format: remove it or convert it" with the converter's words and Remove, and no switch.
- **Checker details (REVPF4 18).** `format` 0 or below is `FORMAT_OLD`; an inherited key name such as `toString` reads as an unknown field; the reader's own start items take the game pack's limits (24 items, 80 characters, 9999 each); the authoring kit no longer lists the retired `story-director` and `npc-state-delta` capabilities.
- **Settings › Packs checked in a real replica (PF4FIX2).** Rendered at 390 and 1280, dark and light, against the mock: the suggestion card's small text is 12px as the mock renders it (it was 12.5px), and the World and Game pickers show their dropdown arrow (SillyTavern's own style had removed it).
- **The calendar rule line fits a calendar of real month names.** A game whose months are January to December is no longer told never to use January to December (the format 4 Ersveil game); only the weekdays are banned for it. A calendar of its own names reads as before.
- Developer: the settings defaults golden pins the `game` calendar preset; `tools.gamepack.export` is listed as a tools-handle call in the live-commands test; the suite tests install the shipped Ersveil world and game (the static prompt golden differs from main only by the Ersveil game's twelve real month names in its calendar line); the AI-README pack table describes format 4.

### For developers
- **B4-10a: the branch journal leaves `modules/people/legacy/`.** Deleted: `legacy/branch*.js` with their part folders, `checkpoint-pack.js` with its parts, `runtime-branch.js` and `runtime-reconcile.js` (about 2,900 lines), and the tests that drove only them (`ancestry-recovery-safety`, `branch-budget`, `branch-restore`, `checkpoint-pack`, `storage-v2`, `v107-deep-rollback-journal`, `v1078-mid-chat-delete-revert`, `v1079-hidden-message-lineage`, `v108-`/`v110-recovery-persistence`, `v111-linear-branch-recovery`, `v113-exact-boundary-recovery`, `branch.helpers.js`, `storage.helpers.js`); the rollback, inheritance and restore cases of about thirty mixed test files went, their other cases stay (about 4,700 test lines in all). Moved with `git mv` (the scan-operation registry and user-dismissed groups unchanged, to `calls/scan-operations.js` and `core/dismissed-groups.js`; the lineage files to `modules/people/lineage/`, trimmed to what is still called, `shared-message-fingerprint.js` renamed `message-hash.js`; fingerprints and keys are the same strings, pinned in `tests/people/lineage.test.js`). New, holding what the hosted path kept as it was: `runtime/runtime-chat-branch.js` (the lineage a change stands on, the host's swipe state, the wait for a new chat's state file, the stale-chat mark) and `runtime/runtime-settle.js` (the swipe settle wait, the merged settle requests and the replies held while a chat opens or waits for its state file; the public `reconcile` is this settle pass and returns `{ state, operation }`). The runtime call `inheritBranch` is gone. A change of NPC State's own keeps the pictures of people it no longer holds while the store holds the chat (the store can bring them back; a person the reader deleted still loses them), and without the store only those of people it holds. NPC State's chat file is written as plain format 1 (a format 2 file still loads, its packed checkpoints dropped). The removed names are on `docs/merge/removed-exports.txt`. Backup: `scratch/backups/b4-10a/`. The sidecar writer itself goes with B4-10b.

- **A Settings gate test (`tests/ui/settings/no-unavailable.test.js`).** It draws every Settings page and module card from the real selectors on a full host (the RPG on, the engine's token count wired in) and fails on a row whose words say it is unavailable or set elsewhere, a shown-only row that is not on its reasoned allowlist (`no-unavailable-allow.js`), a stale allowlist entry, a settable row with no control, a module card with no icon, description or token figure, and a "Settings ›" link to a page that does not exist. The Tools page's quoted prompt text is not scanned.
- **The fight board's stated level reads NPC State's built-in Level (developer, F4).** A foe whose name matches a dossier takes that person's built-in Level (`npc.dossier`'s `level`) as its stated level; a person without one is read from the "Level" custom field as before.

### Removed

- **For developers: the file-moving tool and the old package leftovers.** `scripts/codemod/` (the layout planner, apply, verify and port-branch commands) and its tests, `npm run layout:plan` and its CI job, `npm run verify-import`, the per-package code maps in `docs/codemap/`, the root `workspaces` entry and the `packages/` ignore line are gone; the code map now builds one map. Kept because they have live users: the layout rule files with their reader (`scripts/codemod/rules.mjs`, `layout-map.mjs`, `load-layout-rules.mjs`, `layout/`), which the old-path test helpers and the size ratchet read, and `docs/merge/layout-old-files.txt` and `layout-nonliteral.txt`.

## 0.1.2 — 18 Nov 2026

**Highlights**

- Short story arcs in the world: the Director can write a small arc of 2 to 4 happenings that play out on their own days (behind **World events**, off by default).
- Making your character now works in the new view (the New character window and the "make your character first" hold).
- Six empty fields in the new view now have data (purse this reply, Journal days, planned trips, quest Focus, agendas, news on the road).
- Editing a reply's story text is much faster (about 0.93 s down to 0.13 s at 201 messages).

### Added

- Generated arcs: an opening, up to two more steps and an ending, written and checked when the arc starts and kept with the chat (World events on).
- The New character window and the first-send hold are drawn by the new view.
- Bag › Purse › This turn, Journal day lines, Journal › Planned, a kept quest Focus, Story › Agendas names, Story › News on the road.

### Changed

- A world event that waits for an arc to end happens a day after the ending at the earliest.
- With World events on, a failed call for small happenings is asked once more.

### Fixed

- Editing a reply's prose no longer plans the whole story's steps again.
- Wizard sheets keep the keyboard focus, keep Tab inside, and show normal skill sentences.

### Removed

- The unused Fight tab composer (`ui/tabs/fight/fight.js`).

<details><summary>Full notes</summary>

**Short arcs in the world.** Behind the same switch as 0.1.1, **Settings › World and time › World events** (off by default). With it off the arcs do nothing; the changes outside the switch are listed below.

- **Generated arcs.** A world pack's open templates may now allow a short arc. When such a happening starts, the Director may write it as the first of 2 to 4 happenings in the same place (an opening, at most two more steps, and an ending), each from the template's own menu. The whole arc is written and checked when it starts and kept with the chat; the later happenings then come on their own days with no further call, and the same arc comes back after a swipe, a Re-read, a delete, a reload or a rewind. A step that no longer fits the world when its day comes is skipped. A big arc takes one of its realm's slots for big happenings while it runs. A pack's own events can wait for an arc to end.
- **Known limits.** An event of the pack that waits for an arc to end happens a day after the ending at the earliest. With the switch on, when the Director's call for small happenings fails (it times out, or its answer is cut short), it is now asked once more, or only for the happenings that were cut; when the call succeeds nothing changes.

**Also in this release (not behind the switch).**

- **Six data gaps in the new view closed (UIGAPSFIX).** Nothing moves on screen; fields that were empty now have data. (1) Bag › Purse › "This turn" lists the head reply's coin line in the chat's currency (one net line per reply, "+3 silver 5 pennies"). (2) Journal › a day shows where the scene went, the quests taken, finished, failed and given up, the fights ended and the money in and out, as the day digest kept them (a finished day keeps what its digest was written with, as its own line does). (3) Journal › Planned shows a trip that has not started (`atlas.trip` phase `planned`). (4) The quest you pick as Focus (Journal; Now › Today) is kept with the chat's view prefs, so it stays through a redraw, a reload, a swipe and a chat change, and falls back to the newest quest when that quest is gone. (5) Story › Agendas names the stored person whose name or alias is the agenda's key. (6) Story › News on the road lists the world pulse's open rumours the story has not told yet. Not built: Bag seats (mounts keep no seat count, by design) and structured stage moves on the Journal (they wait for the people store). The unused Fight tab composer (`ui/tabs/fight/fight.js`) is removed; its cards are still the fight scene's.
- **Editing a reply's story text no longer plans the story's steps again (perf PB8).** When an edit leaves the reply's change block (shown or hidden), its stray change lines and its swipe as they were, and every block of the reply still applies, the story is rebuilt as before but the reconcile is skipped. The reconcile planned every guarded step again from the first one in the chat, and each came out as it was stored, because an edit takes nothing back by itself: Re-read is how an edit reaches the story. Every other edit takes the full path as before: a change to the block, a new or changed change line in the story, an edit that adds or removes a day or month name, a `#N`, or a word of a cast person's name or alias, a user message, an edit with no message number, or a reply whose blocks do not all apply after the edit. The same goes for deletes, swipes and Re-reads. The state and the Problems are the same as before, unless settings, packs or module versions changed since the story's steps were last planned: then the steps meet them at the next swipe, delete or Re-read, as after a reload. The stored records differ only in bookkeeping hashes: the boundary and checkpoint hashes after the edited reply are stamped again by the next swipe, delete or Re-read, not by the edit itself. Until then a rebuild replays from the checkpoint before the edit, and the next swipe, delete or other full-path edit does the skipped walk once. Measured in the perf rig at 201 messages: an edit of message 6 went from 0.93 s to 0.13 s, and an edit of the last reply from 0.17–0.22 s to under 0.05 s; the next full-path change after them took 1.0 s instead of 0.16 s. How it is told, and why it is safe: `docs/dev/edit-prose-only.md`.
- **Making your character works in the new view (NEWWIZARD; needed before the old view is taken away, B4-4).** The New character window and the first-send hold were the old view's screens, drawn by its hidden host. The new view now draws both itself, as bottom sheets in the mock's parts (the Party stat sheet's rows and buttons, the Journal editor's fields, the kit buttons; `ui/wizard/`, `ui/css/wizard.css`), with the old window's words, steps (Who · Stats · Skills · Review, with the summary row and Back / Next), rules and templates. Create sends the same commands in the same order as the old window (`modules/rpg/view/wizard-data-parts/create-run.js`, the old Review step's sequence without a DOM, each with `{ cause: 'ui' }`), so a swipe, a Re-read, a delete, a rewind and a reload treat them alike. Three ways in: (1) with the RPG on and no You, the first Send (button or Enter) is held by the sheet "Make your character first" (Send without the RPG for now, or Create your character, then send; closing puts the text back), which opens the suite for it and closes it after; it writes the same chat notes as the old dialog and listens before the old host's own hold, so only one window ever opens; (2) the Party tab shows, with "Create your character" (nobody yet) or "+ New character" (companions but no You), while the story has no You: the tab used to be hidden until the roster listed someone; once You exist it is as before (with Gear in fights off the roster lists nobody, so the tab goes again); (3) People › Add to party › Edit first opens it filled in for that person: the port's `open('story-block/new-character')` is answered by the new view and the old host's page event is no longer sent for it (a second open while one is up is refused, never passed on). Differences from the old window: the Stats step uses the stat sheet's − / + rows instead of the steppers and the radar (the mock has no radar); a skill is a card of stacked fields instead of a table row, so it fits the 460 px sheet; the old Home card "Your character" has no new-view counterpart (the Party tab's button stands in). A chat change closes the sheets and nothing more is sent (a Create already under way stops between commands, so the first chat can keep a Character without its Skills); a sheet closed by hand during Create lets it finish, as the old window did. After a Create that left a block or a thing out only Done stays (no Back). The old host's own wizard and hold stay on the page, unused under the new view, until B4-4 removes them. **Review fixes (WIZFIX):** the keyboard stays where it was when a step is redrawn (Next / Back, an arrow on the step tabs, a stepper, a skill's type, Add and × keep the focus on the equivalent control inside the sheet; before, every step change dropped it to the page); every sheet now keeps Tab inside itself (the sheet is aria-modal), and closing a sheet gives the focus back to its opener only when it was still in that sheet, so the join card closing after Edit first no longer takes the focus from the wizard opened over it; a skill's "What it does" is a two-row box, so a normal sentence shows; the sheet title's theme-bar reset is in the shared sheet style, not only the wizard's; the docs say the real skill limits (five combat skills to start, up to eight with level, three utility).

</details>

<details><summary>For developers</summary>

- **Generated arcs (WEGENARCS).** Behind `story-director.world-events`; with it off nothing here runs, and a world with no open template that carries `arc` steps exactly as before (checked on a flag-on scenario with open templates against 0.1.1: the same bytes). An open template with `"arc": { "steps": [2, 4] }` may start an arc: the Director's answer for the slot carries `arc: { steps, ending }` (later steps with `after` and `within` days, optional `severity: "major"`), the whole plan is checked once against the menu and guards, stored with the slot's answer in the ledger and copied into `chronicle.daily.arcs`; code fires each step on its window (a seeded day, no Director call) and records `arc-ended:<template>@<ref>` when the ending fires. A major step holds one realm slot while the arc runs; a minor step counts against density but is not blocked. Steps are checked again when they fire (a step that no longer holds is skipped, never rewritten). New: `daily/arc-plan.js`, `daily/arc-day.js`, `arc-ended` in the anchor grammar, the arc wording in the Director's system text (only when a slot may start an arc), and stored answers with a plan may be up to 7000 characters (others stay 3000).
- **Generated arcs, review fixes (ARCSFIX; ruling 52).** Behind `story-director.world-events`; with it off nothing here runs, and a template with no `arc` stores the same answer as before (name, summary, people, effects only). An arc template's range now counts every happening, the opening and the ending included (`[2, 4]`: `arc.steps` lists 0 to 2); the opening's `severity: "major"` counts for the caps. Only checked fields are stored and a plan's texts are cut before it is checked, so a stored plan is always whole JSON (before, a plan with extra fields could be cut mid-JSON and lose its arc); a plan still over 7000 characters is refused like any bad plan. The call asks for 1200 tokens plus 1200 per arc slot, keeps the whole rows of a cut answer (only the cut slots are asked again) and asks a timed-out call once more. The prompt now shows the arc shape, the counting rule and the 1-90 / 1-60 day ranges. Hidden lore titles match ignoring extra spaces. The ledger's answers stay within about 120000 characters (oldest first). Known: an authored event anchored on `arc-ended:` reacts the day after the ending (authored events run before arcs each day); a rewind past an answer that was dropped from the ledger uses the fallback.
- **Release 0.1.2 (REL012).** `world/arcs` (two commits on `world/engine` before 0.1.1, which reached main as one squashed commit) was replayed onto main (0.1.1 and the commits after it) with its own two commits, not merged: a merge gave 45 add/add conflicts with the squashed copy. The code change is line for line the reviewed one; `docs/v2/PACKS.md` keeps both 0.1.1's LAB2ENG and sub-place text and the arcs paragraph; `docs/codemap/jobs.json` gains only the arcs' entries; the maps are regenerated. Version 0.1.2 everywhere (`node scripts/release.mjs 0.1.2`; `docs/INSTALL.md` by hand). The UIGAPSFIX, PB8 and NEWWIZARD entries, which landed inside the 0.1.0 section beside stale copies of 0.1.1 lines, move here and the copies are dropped (0.1.1 and older read as released).

</details>

## 0.1.1 — 18 Nov 2026

**Highlights**

- The world moves on its own: events, conditions and a daily world step (behind **World events**, off by default).
- News travels along the routes and arrives as rumour beyond its reach; the reply tail carries a short [World now] note.
- The Director fills small happenings from the pack's menu and judges arc plans against the world.
- NPC State keeps a world pack's secrets until they are earned.
- Sample world pack Ersveil (31 people, 103 world events), and a first read-only Pack Studio.
- Faster and smoother replies (several perf fixes) and a switch to keep the last two replies' change blocks in the prompt.

### Added

- World events and conditions, the daily world step, Tempo, news by courier, the [World now] note, the World tab's Conditions here card, and the Settings rows (World events, Tempo, Director calls this game year).
- Open templates, the Director judge and the arc world note.
- Pack secrets in NPC State; the sample pack `examples/packs/ersveil-world`.
- Follow star puts a person on the narrator's key list; People › Elsewhere shows what followed people do.
- Settings › Tools: Save points. Pack Studio (read only, `studio/index.html`).
- Settings › Models › Calls: keep the last two replies' change blocks (on by default).

### Changed

- A pack may carry world-pack sections and installs (before, it failed).
- The World tab shows a region's name, not its id.
- The day digest no longer lists the names check as an event.
- A first meeting's [Cast] note and the world pulse no longer give the narrator the stranger's name.

### Fixed

- A repaired travel stretch no longer overshoots; a doubled reply draft is cut; a repaired day jump keeps the time of day.
- No 5-second wait after an edit or delete (NPC State); the World map no longer lays out every frame; chat scrolls smoothly under the new view; less work per reply in the new view.
- A trip stop keeps the same id after a Re-read.

### Removed

- Nothing for players.

<details><summary>Full notes</summary>

**The world moves on its own, and the Director directs.** Everything in this list is behind one switch, **Settings › World and time › World events** (off by default). With it off the world features below do nothing and the story runs as in 0.1.0; the few changes that are not behind the switch are listed under "Also changed with the switch off".

- **World events and conditions.** A world pack can describe what may happen in its world: one-off and recurring events, with chances, seasons, what they need and what ends them. Named conditions of the world ("grain is dear", "the roads are closed") hold at a place, a route, a region or a realm and fade after their time. The story can set and lift them too.
- **The daily world step.** When the story crosses into a new day, the world takes its step: events may happen, conditions fade, people of the pack move about. A jump of several days steps each day. **Tempo** sets how fast the world moves.
- **News travels.** News of a happening reaches each place after the time a courier needs along the routes. It is fact as far as its reach goes, and one step beyond that it arrives as rumour, naming only the region. The reply's tail carries a short **[World now]** note: the conditions where the scene is and the newest news that has reached it.
- **Open templates: the Director fills small happenings.** A pack's small templates can leave the details open; the Director fills them in from the pack's own menu, and the pack's fallback text is used when that call fails.
- **The Director judge and the arc world note.** When the arc planner makes a plan, one more short call checks its beats against the world (the events under way, the protected people, the hidden lore) and accepts, reshapes or refuses each one. The same call writes a short note on how the arc and the world meet, shown under the arc line. The planner reacts to world events but never decides them.
- **NPC State keeps a world pack's secrets until they are earned.** A pack person's secrets wait in their dossier and come out only when their condition is met.
- **The World tab and Settings rows.** The World tab shows the conditions where you are and how the news came. Settings › World and time has **World events**, **Tempo** and **Director calls this game year** (read only).
- **Sample world pack: Ersveil.** `examples/packs/ersveil-world` (and `examples/packs/ersveil-world.zip`) is a full world pack: 31 people (12 companions with portraits), lore, realms, powers, conditions and 103 world events. Its world events run only with World events on.
- **Known limit:** a world pack's cast fields beyond the basic ones do not reach NPC State yet. Pack format 4 fixes it.

**Also changed with the switch off.**

- A pack may carry the world-pack sections (conditions, lore, regions, realms, world events, templates and the rest) and installs; before, they made the install fail.
- The World tab shows a region's name instead of its id when the pack names its regions (display only).
- The day digest no longer lists the names check's "Name: … is new" lines as the day's events (LAB2-5); the meeting stays in the Journal's Met.
- Settings › World and time shows the World events row.
- The fixes and speed-ups merged since 0.1.0 (below) are not behind the switch.

**Fixes and speed-ups since 0.1.0.**

- **Follow star reaches the narrator; People › Elsewhere shows what followed people are doing (G3).** The star (stored per chat by `people.follow`, shown on the People tile, the dossier and the Now Here card, set only in the dossier as in the mock) now also puts the person on the narrator's key list: Follow sends `keypeople.pin { followed: true }`, Unfollow `keypeople.unpin`, so the away lines, the tail and the death/archive sync work as for Key people with no change to the model's wording. A follow-made pin does not count against the Key people limit (a pin from the World tab still does). If the key list refuses or has no such person, the star stands. People followed before this build are not pinned until followed again. An away person's Elsewhere tile adds `back <date>` to the place line and shows what they are doing in the small italic line under it (the mock has no such line there, so it is the mock's `said` style). Key people itself is still there (removed in B4-9).
- **Two fixes from the second live A/B (LAB2-4, LAB2-6).** (1) A repaired day jump keeps the time of day the reply gives: the repair prompt now says that after a stretch of a day or more, when the reply gives a time of day ("after dusk"), the clock time goes on the next line (`! time +10d` then `~ time 7:00 pm`); the safety net already accepts both lines. (2) The `[Cast]` note for a first meeting no longer gives the narrator the stranger's name: it names them by a descriptive alias ("the ferrywoman", else "a stranger"), leaves name forms of the name out, says the player does not know the name until it is said in the story, and the name, or any word of it (first or family name, 3+ letters), is also replaced in the note's own text, whatever the aliases are. Someone the story already named (met in the story) keeps the name as before. The world pulse's chance-meeting line ("World this reply: … crosses your path in <place>, by chance.") does the same: it calls the unmet person by their first descriptive alias ("The ferrywoman crosses your path…"), else "A stranger crosses your path…", never by name (the line only ever picks people not yet met, so it never names anyone).
- **Keep the last two replies' change blocks in the prompt (on by default).** A switch on Settings › Models › Calls, "Keep the last two replies’ change blocks in the prompt" (host setting `keepRecentBlocks`; Reset Models turns it back on). The two newest replies reach the model with the `<Changes>` block the narrator wrote for them, so it sees its own recent blocks; older replies lose theirs as before. Only the narrator's own lines come back (never repair or Assist lines, nor a block the reply was cut off in); a reply without a block counts as one of the two. In one live round the narrator kept its own block on 9 of 10 replies (4 of 10 and 5 of 10 before), at about 390 tokens more per request and no extra call. Two guards go with it: a `+ item` or `+ coin` line a reply copies from those two blocks (identical in every part) is not applied again unless its story, after its first paragraph, shows it happening again; a line that differs at all always stays (a Re-read judges it the same way), and a reply that ends in a `<Changes>` block left open with no line in it now counts as having no block, so the repair is asked (it was skipped before). Off, every prompt and every capture is exactly as before.
- **Less work per reply in the new view (perf batch PB2).** Nothing drawn changes; the view reads less. (1) The tab strip's flags (the RPG is on, a fight is open or waiting, an interaction is open and its title) come from a light `flags` view (`ui/select/build.js`), not from building the whole Now card and the fight and scene views on every engine event; a port without a `flags` view is read the old way. (2) The flags, the card, the open tab and the chat surfaces (the line under the reply, the drawer, the reply-menu button) are each read at most once a frame, however many events a reply sends: the flags are a redraw section drawn first in its frame, so a fight's tab is in the strip before the tab draws, and the chat surfaces are drawn by one per-frame callback (`frameCoalesced` in `ui/runtime/redraw.js`) on the same port and SillyTavern events as before. (3) While the suite is closed its card and tabs are not read; opening it draws what changed meanwhile (`isOpen` and `reveal()` on the redraw scheduler). The card's sky is painted from the same card read. Measured in the perf rig on the 18 Nov A/B chat (11 people), per reply: with the World tab open, card builds 14 → 2, chat builds 12 → 2, fight and scene reads 30 → 0, `npc.cast` 26 → 4; with the suite closed, card builds 14 → 0 and chat builds 12 → 2.
- **No 5-second wait after an edit or a delete (perf PB5).** NPC State no longer polls for 5 s after each edit or delete of a message, and the console warning "edit event did not settle … within 5000ms" (or "delete event …") is gone. SillyTavern sends the edit and delete events after it has changed the chat, so the wait never saw a change and timed out every time, while each of its polls (every 50 ms) built two full copies of NPC State's branch history. Now the chat key and the swipe state are read directly, and the wait takes one look on the next tick and ends. The edit or delete is still read again twice, as before: by NPC State's immediate reconcile and by its queued branch reconcile. A second edit or delete in the same tick still settles against the first one's baseline. One that comes later is now read on its own: it no longer adds a third, "settled" reconcile, and its immediate reconcile no longer takes the first event's earlier message number or delete. While the store holds the people (once Story Suite has accepted NPC State) every one of these reconciles ends at the store-owned exit, so nothing changes there; the old sidecar path (NPC State refused by the host) was not measured.
- **Three fixes from the live A/B (LAB-2, LAB-3, LAB-4).** (1) The repair no longer overshoots a travel stretch: its prompt tells the model to count only the time the reply tells, and a repaired `! time` stretch over a day is cut to `! time +24h` (with a Problems note) unless the reply names a stretch longer than a day ("the next morning", "two days later", "after three days of hard riding", "for a week", "after a fortnight", "on the third day"; a distance still to cover or "the second day" does not count). The cut line is what is stored, so Re-read and reload read the same. (2) When a model drafts the reply in the message itself (its first paragraphs, a review, then the whole reply again), the draft in front of the second copy is cut and a Problems note says so. Only a real doubled reply is cut: the whole first paragraph must come back ending a paragraph, and what follows must repeat most of the text before it, so a refrain or a flashback that reopens on the opening words keeps its text; a first paragraph under 60 characters, and a Continue, are never touched. (3) The day digest no longer carries "NPC meters: the reply was counted" as the day's event.
- **The World map no longer lays out every frame (PB4).** The "you are here" ring used to animate the SVG `r` attribute, which made the browser lay out and restyle the World tab on every frame, even idle. The ring is now one round HTML overlay on the map, placed and sized from the map's view, that scales up from about half size to full size while fading from 1 to 0 over 2 s, the same sweep as before (transform and opacity only, so the compositor does the work). Reduced-motion still shows the plain ring. Nothing else changes.
- **Faster replies: NPC State reads the story clock once per task, not once per state read (PB1).** Each NPC State read asked Story Director for the clock again (two host queries that copied the settings), about 2,500 times per reply. `storyClock()` (`modules/people/adapter/timeline.js`) now keeps its answer until a host event (`committed`, `head`, `turn`, `feature`, `modules`) or the end of the current task, so a settings edit shows by the next task. Without host events nothing is kept. The ages and dates are the same; only the number of host queries drops.
- **Pack Studio, first skeleton (read only; for pack writers and developers).** `studio/index.html` is a page of its own: it opens a pack `.zip` (through the same reader as the game's import) or a `pack.json` with its portrait images, checks it with the game's own pack checker (`packs/validate.js`) and shows each part read only: Overview, Lore, Cast (with portraits), Places & routes, Conditions, Events, Templates, Powers & scores, Items & foes, and Other (fields as written). A Problems page lists the checker's problems by part, with Go to the record; search finds records by title, id, name, keys or aliases; Export gives the zip or `pack.json` back as opened (read as UTF-8 the way the game reads it, so a leading BOM is not kept; a plain UTF-8 file comes back byte for byte). Keys: Ctrl/Cmd+O, Ctrl/Cmd+S, `/`, Alt+1…9, F8, Esc. It looks like the Settings overlay (Glass dark or light) at phone and desktop widths. It edits nothing yet, it is not in the release bundles and nothing in the extension loads it; open it from a checkout through any static server (`docs/dev/studio.md`). A world pack's new parts (lore, conditions, world events…) are listed as problems until the world-pack checks reach main.
- **Trip records: the three recorded limits checked (TRIPLIMITS2).** Nothing in the trip's behaviour changed; one test now pins what holds. (1) "An edited earlier reply that starts a trip a later reply's trip had replaced can still show as the trip" and (2) "its records count toward the record limit" no longer hold: a later trip stands, and the records stay a go and its delays (a new test: grown and shrunk Re-read, swipe, middle delete, reload, compared with the chat told that way). (3) The same-minute edge stays, by design: a delay whose go was edited away, told at the standing go's own story minute, is ordered by revision, and a Re-read of the go's reply can drop it. Stored records carry no chat position, so that case cannot be told from a delay that belonged to an earlier trip; fixing it needs a new field on delays. A `<sid>.<n>`-keyed delay that names an existing go is never affected.
- **A trip stop has the same id after a Re-read as in the story told that way (TRIPID).** After an edit that added a `~ place` line and a Re-read, the trip's first stop read as the map's pack node (`pack:<name>`) until the next send, while the chat told that way read the minted atlas place: a Re-read runs no beforeGenerate, so the atlas place was not made yet. `atlas.trip` (legs, the You point and the movers) now names a pack place by its canonical id (the pack's definition id, else the atlas's own id for a place no pack has), whether or not the atlas place exists, so both agree and the id does not change on the next send. The map widget finds its map node by the stop's name (`widget-view-parts/anchor.js`), so the legs, the You marker and Fit trip draw as before. The `atlas.trip` `fromId`/`toId` values are therefore canonical ids, no longer map node ids (nothing else reads them). The Story Director Re-read fuzz no longer counts this difference (seeds 19, 27, 36, 57, 64, 67, 73, 94 show none). Not changed: an edit that removes a `~ place` line leaves the atlas place the story already made in the store (nothing reads it).
- **Save points on Settings › Tools, and the card offer no longer needs the old view (B4 prerequisites).** (1) Settings › Tools has a Save points card (only while the feature `story-block.savepoints` is on, which it is by default): this chat's save points with name, message number and date, a Save point button that asks for a name, Return here (opens the branch) and Forget (takes it off the list; the branch stays in SillyTavern). The save points are built once at boot (`core/boot/host-parts.js`) and the old view is handed the same instance. The "Removed" list on Settings now says only "Return in time" is gone. (2) The card-offer controller is built at boot too (`packs/card-offer-wiring.js`, moved out of `ui/legacy/ui-host/`); boot reads the card again on a chat change and clears the "Installed …" line after a reply, so the Now card's pack offer works without the old view. The old view takes boot's controller when given one and builds its own otherwise (nothing changes for it).
- **Chat scrolls smoothly under the new view (PB3).** The old view's reply cards (`ui/legacy/view/mounts/card`) are hidden under the new view but were still built for every message that scrolled into view, each replaying the story from a checkpoint. While the body has the class `ss-ui-next` the card mount now draws, observes and looks up nothing, and a card strip left from before is taken away. "Use the old view" removes the class, and the mount then watches the messages again and draws the cards on screen. Nothing else changes: with the old view the cards are drawn exactly as before.

</details>

<details><summary>For developers</summary>

- **Release 0.1.1 (REL011).** `node scripts/release.mjs` now also writes the pack checker's suite version (`packs/validate/core-parts/host-version.js`, which `requires.suite` is checked against; before, a bump left it behind and only `tests/packs/format3.test.js` noticed), and `tests/scripts/release-versions.test.js` pins it. The release gate's version is 0.1.1 (`scripts/release/check.mjs`). A pack that names exactly `"0.1.0"` (not a range like `"^0.1"`) is now refused, as a pinned version should be; the two test fixtures that did (`tests/packs/helpers/world-pack-sample.js`, `tests/packs/world-pack-spec-examples.test.js`) now say `"^0.1"`, as every shipped pack does. The Studio's tests follow the engine's pack checker: `tests/packs/studio/parts-check.test.js`, `zip-export.test.js` and `tests/ui/studio/studio-page.test.js` expect the world-pack kinds to be known, the sample world pack to pass, the fixture's remaining problems (`content.start`, an event's `cue`, a missing `ends_when`) under their parts, and a zip's top-level `.md`/`.txt` notes to be left out while any other stray file is still refused. Not built: a narrator's `+ quest` counting as "took on" in the day line (it needs a new stored day-facts field; the `Director: + quest` receipt is not kept in the day's entry).
- **The v1 Story Suite handshake is removed (B4-2).** NPC State no longer writes `globalThis.StorySuite`, announces itself to a v1 registry, offers the Source of truth settings or the "Manage all in Story Suite" button, or reads the `sourceOfTruth` setting (a saved one is dropped). The scene follows Story Block's `scene.present` and the calendar follows Story Director, as they did by default. `legacy/runtime-suite.js`, `suite.js`, `suite-handshake*` and `story-block-tabs.js` are gone; the live parts (`npcs()`, `npcProfiles()`, the reply preview, the dossier target) are in `modules/people/npc-api/` and `runtime/runtime-npc-api.js`. Backup: `scratch/backups/retire-b4h-handshake`.
- **B4 removal tools (B4-0).** `scripts/b4/` has a backup script (copies the files of a removal to `scratch/backups/<unit>/` with a sha256 manifest, refuses an existing folder), a dead-export check (fails when a kept file imports, re-exports, dynamically imports or `new URL`s a removed path or export, and lists files with computed specifiers to grep by hand) and `unit.mjs`, the one command that runs the per-unit recipe: backup, git rm, dead exports, git grep empty, shrink-only lists, code map, touched tests (`docs/dev/b4.md`). Tools only: they delete nothing by themselves and are not part of CI.
- **NPC State cannot stop the rest (F1b).** NPC State is now the last built-in module (`modules/people/module.js`, id `people`, listed in `core/modules-list.js`); its register hook is the start (`startPeople`) that `index.js` used to give as a `late` part, so it now starts just before the host is marked ready, not after. A failure is a MODULE_START Problem in Diagnostics, the module is switched off, and the new view's switch still appears. The dossier list's rows moved to `modules/people/dossier-rows.js` (the old path re-exports them). The people module carries `hidden: true` (`sdk.modules()` adds the key only to a module that sets it), so Settings › Add-ons' Installed modules list leaves it out; every other module's entry is unchanged. Nothing was removed: the v1 handshake and every legacy file stay until B4-2 and later.

World events engine, first pieces. Everything here is behind one new feature switch, `story-director.world-events` (**World events**, off by default, declared once in Story Director's chronicle part). With it off every output is as before, with one exception: a pack may now carry the optional `content.conditions` section (job A) and the world-pack sections (see the install check below) and installs whatever the switch says (before, those made the install fail). A second exception, from LABFIXENG: the World tab shows a region's name (`content.regions`) instead of its id with the switch off too (display only; a pack with no `content.regions` reads as before).

- **Sample world pack (PACKSAMPLE).** `examples/packs/ersveil-world/` is the finished new Ersveil (pack 3.0.0, the author's checked build: 31 people, lore, realms, powers, scores, conditions, markets, 103 world events, templates) with its README, and `examples/packs/ersveil-world.zip` installs it. The twelve companion portraits are 512 px WebP copies (about 30-40 KB each, down from 3 MB PNGs; the cast's `portrait` fields name the `.webp` files). `scripts/core/build-example-zip.mjs` now builds both zips (`--check` covers both). The older `examples/packs/ersveil` is unchanged. Examples are not part of the runtime bundle. The pack's world events run only with `story-director.world-events` on; with it off the pack installs and the rest of it works as any pack. Tests: `tests/packs/ersveil-world-sample.test.js`.
- **Fixes from the live A/B (LABFIXENG: LAB-1, LAB-5).** (LAB-1) is behind `story-director.world-events` (the fallback hour is written only with it on; with it off the state is the same as on main and on the engine base, checked with the same flag-off scenario on all three trees). (LAB-5): the World tab's region names are display only and are not behind the switch, so a pack with `content.regions` now shows names there with it off too and a pack with none reads exactly as before; the news, open-template and arc lines are behind the switch as they were. (LAB-1) The fallback hour for a reply with no time line is "untold" minutes (`director.time.soft`); a stretch time line that comes later for that same reply (the repair's `! time +5m`, a Continue's, a late line) now counts from before that reply's own fallback minutes (kept in the soft slot as `sid` and `own`; the untold hour of an earlier reply stays, as before), as a `~` clock face already did, so the repair no longer stacks on the fallback (before: +1 h then +5 m; now: +5 m). A reply that is repaired with no time line keeps its fallback hour; a time line that is a date (`~ time`) is unchanged. It is the same on swipe, swipe back, a middle delete, a rewind, a reload and a Re-read (edited, grown or shrunk: a reply with its own time line gets no fallback). (LAB-5) The World tab (the map's region filter and labels, the place sheet) shows a region's name from `content.regions` (by id, name or alias) instead of its id: a new query `chronicle.regionNames` (null with the switch off) is read by the World view-model. The `{region}` of an open template's fallback line, the region in the arc brief's event line and the region a hidden place's news is named by use the name too; the id stays only for a region the pack gives no name (the last resort). Conditions here and the world note already used names for region conditions (realm conditions are named by the realm already). A faction's area text still finds its places by the region's id or its name. **Known limit (E14, pinned by its test):** when the fallback hour itself crosses midnight the new day is stepped at once; a repair that then takes the clock back leaves that stepped day standing (nothing is undone), and a Re-read of that reply drops the step (it reads the repair's line inline, so no fallback hour). Only a reply read at the very end of a day is affected. Stored open-template slots may now carry `regionName`.
- **News beyond its reach is rumour (NEWSREACH; ruling 53).** Behind `story-director.world-events`; with it off nothing changes. Confirmed news still stops at `reach`; one step further it arrives as rumour at courier time ("(rumour)", naming only the region, never fact there): a local happening is fact in its town and rumour in its region; a regional one is fact in its region and rumour in the rest of its realm and in regions a route joins to it; a realm one is fact in the realm and rumour in realms a route joins to it; world is fact everywhere. A minor happening is fact only in its own region. Further than that it is not heard. Derived at read time, nothing stored, so replay matches live. Spec v0.14.12 and acceptance test 30 updated.
- **Fixes from the live A/B v2 (LAB2ENG: LAB2-1, LAB2-2, LAB2-3, LAB2-5).** LAB2-1, LAB2-2 and LAB2-3 are behind `story-director.world-events` (with it off the prompt is the same as before; the new query `atlas.anchor` exists but nothing reads it); LAB2-5 is not (the day digest is not behind the switch). (LAB2-1, traced) The run's two code-fired happenings (the harvest failing in Shackles City, the Ashen Veil taking a hold at Highmeadow in Valegard) are `reach: regional` in the pack and happened in other regions than the scene's (Bluewatch Reach, then Auvoth Compact), so news stopping at its reach (spec §4, acceptance test 30) kept them out of every note: that part is as designed and is unchanged. What was wrong is fixed: a scene at a place no pack names heard as an unknown place (before the atlas had the tavern, every happening in the world came at "tier, 10 days", which is how the run's driver saw the Shackles harvest; once the atlas had it, a local happening of the town it is in was never heard there), and a happening placed at a region itself (a world line with `place: Auvoth Compact`) was an unknown place for news (rumour everywhere for 10 days); it is now a place of that region (its reach applies) and is heard in that region at once, as fact. (LAB2-3) A place no pack names stands for a pack place: the new atlas query `atlas.anchor { name? }` → `{ name, region, via }`: the pack place itself (`pack`), its town part (`town`: "Auvoth / Pale Market Inn", "Pale Market Inn in Auvoth"), the place the atlas saved it in (`parent`), the pack place last written on the map in its region (the atlas's region, else the scene's: `last`), else the region alone (`region`, `name` ''). Known limit (pinned by its test): `last` is the pack place last written on the map in that region, not always the last one visited (back in Auvoth after a trip to the Auvoth Depths, which the map does not write again, a new tavern reads as the Depths). With the switch on, the news (`world.heardHere`, `world.newsAt`, `world.cueToday`), the conditions here (the world note and the World tab) and the atlas's routes line (only for a place with no routes of its own) read the place it stands for, so the same news comes the same way in the tavern as in the town (before: "via route, 0.96 days" at Auvoth and "via tier, 10 days" at the Sled & Winch) and the tavern gets the town's conditions and "Routes from Auvoth". It reads only the stored atlas and the packs, so a swipe, Re-read, delete, rewind or reload gives the same. (LAB2-2) An open slot rolled inside a step over several days (a skip) waits from the step's last day (a `wait` day on the pending slot) instead of running out three days after its own day inside the skip (before, every slot of a long skip fell back with no call, so a skip never asked the Director). The look-ahead after the skip's reply then asks for them in one batched call (at most 4 slots; the 5th and later of the step use their fallback, as before), in the background; the next reply stores the answers and the one after applies them; stored once, and replay reuses them. A slot that was waiting before the skip still falls back three days after its own day. The look-ahead for the day after the skip may still be its own call, as before. A happening placed at a sub-place ("Bluewatch Village / Guild Office") names its town in its text (the region when the pack has no town), in an open template's fallback and in a code-written template's line; its place, where its news starts, is unchanged. (LAB2-5) Not fixed by main's #141, which took out only "NPC meters: the reply was counted": the names check of a `person` line ("Name: Torger Venn is new; look: …") was still picked as a day's event in [Earlier days]. That receipt (`capture.person` with a "Name: " summary) is now bookkeeping like the other ticks, so for days captured from now on it is not a line of the day anywhere the day's lines go: not in [Earlier days], not among the Journal day's Events, and someone in the scene only for their own names check is not a witness of the day (`who`); the meeting is still in the Journal's "Met". The e2e playthrough's check that pinned those lines (`tests/suite/break/e2e-playthrough.test.js`, "earlier days") is changed to match. Not changed here: the run's "CY 611, October 1: 32 copper out." is that day's money fact, and it was the day's only fact because the day had no place move and no event line, and a narrator's `+ quest` line does not make a "took on" fact (a quest's `taken` stamp is written only by an accepted offer, `threads.accept`), so the convoy job taken that day is not in the line.
- **A pack's spec secrets in NPC State (NSSPECSEC; owner decision 17 Nov: withhold).** Behind `story-director.world-events`; with it off nothing changes (a spec secret is dropped as before, the prompt and every screen are as before). With it on, a pack person's `{ id, gate, text }` secrets are seeded into their dossier (kept as `packId` + `gate`, `from: 'pack'`). While a gate is closed the secret is absent from the narrator's prompt; when it opens (`stage`, `meter`, anchors incl. `fact:` / `learned:`, `place:`, `quote:`, joined by and/or) the narrator gets `Name (may come out): text` and the reveal watch lists it, and a `~ reveal` line with a quote found in the reply opens it (a swipe, delete, Re-read or rewind takes it back). Opening is what `chronicle.world.learned` records as `learned:<cast id>/<secret id>` and `writes_fact` (matched by the pack id). The Director's brief lists unlearned spec secrets under a never-hint rule. Gates are read from the head state at the prompt (so a gate that opens in a reply is revealable from the next prompt) and from the reply's own state at the `~ reveal` line (anchors and place by `ctx.query`, so a Re-read of an earlier reply reads the world as it was then); `quote:` terms are checked at the reveal; `stage` uses the person's pack stages by NPC State's own ladder test (a stage's `milestone` is its cue, never a need); `place:` is the scene's place by name or id, whole (a district under it is not read). Learnable in lines mode (the shipped mode). A new query `chronicle.world.anchors` gives NPC State the recorded anchors. A gate term that names another person (`meter x of <id>`, `stage of <id>`) is false, never that person's value on the owner's secret (WEFIXSEAM). Not built: hidden lore still has no source for `learned:lore/...`. Old-shape secrets keep `(hidden)`. At most one `(may come out)` line a person (the first open one in the pack's order; the next shows once it has come out) and two in all, people in key order, and the reveal watch lists only those; a gate's `of <id>` naming the owner reads the owner's own value, another person's id stays shut (WEFINALFIX).
- **World events: the engine reads the spec rulings (WEFIX1).** Behind `story-director.world-events`; with it off nothing here runs (no step, no `start.world`), and with it on only a pack that uses these fields steps differently (before, season anchors never held and major templates were never rolled). The daily step is given the chat's calendar: season anchors hold in their calendar season; `date:` anchors and `on` (a one-day, tempo-fixed window at set-in-motion 100%) are read; a "N% per season" chance on an event that requires a season is spread over that season's days (tempo no longer changes its yearly odds) and "100% per season" fires once per stretch on a seeded day; windows opened by an anchor made later the same day open that day; thresholds in `likelier_if` / `less_if` are applied instead of dropping the event; outcome weights are sums and differences of numbers and `power:` / `score:` refs; templates honour `requires_any`, `likelier_if` and `less_if` and major templates are rolled under the major caps; a protected person is killed only by an authored event naming them, and a skipped death says "(<name> survived)" with a Problems note; `start.world` is applied once on the first step (no news, no event, no cap use) and `content.regions` names appear in `{region}` and in the world note. Not built: `arc`, and `standing:` weights (read as neutral). Details in `docs/v2/PACKS.md`. An anchor in `ends_when.any_of` ends the event when it happens after the firing, a recurring one included: a `fired:` / `set:` anchor already true on the firing day ends it on its next occurrence (offset passed), a season the event fired in ends it when that season comes round again (WEFINALFIX).
- **Open templates (WEOPEN).** Behind `story-director.world-events`; with it off, and for any template with no `open` block, every output is as before (checked against the world/engine tip). A minor template with `open: { menu, writes_facts }`, an optional `fallback` and `per` is filled in by the Director from the pack's menu: new `chronicle.world.open` command (stores the Director's answers and the call count in the chat's ledger in the message before the reply), a Quick-profile batched call (up to 4 slots, one retry with the errors, then the fallback), a pure check of each answer (menu bounds, protected people and places, authored reads unless `open: true`, `writes_facts`), `chronicle.daily.slots` / `pending` (the day record and the slots waiting), asked ahead and never blocking a reply. Swipes, deletes, Re-reads, reloads and rewinds in the session reuse the stored answer. Only minor templates are open; generated arcs are not built. Prompt wording recorded in `NSD-WORDING.md`. Tests: `tests/modules/world/open`. The asker's in-memory answers are kept per chat and seed (the last 3), handed over only for the slots that chat has pending, with the calls that chat made; if the retry cannot be made (busy, aborted, no model) the first call's accepted answers and its call are kept and only the refused slots are asked again later (WEFIXSEAM). The look-ahead (the dry run that asks the Director ahead of time) is given what the daily step is given: the reader's tempo, the calendar and the cast tie, so at a tempo other than 1 it no longer asks for slots the step never makes (at tempo 2 over 200 days: 2 calls for 2 slots, before 92); beforeGenerate stores only answers for the slots the look-ahead wants (WEFINALFIX).
- **The Director judge and the arc world note (WEJUDGE; "the director directs").** Behind `story-director.world-events`; with it off nothing here runs (no second model call, no new draft or record field, no new prompt line; checked against the world/engine tip: the planner's request, the draft, the accept receipt and the arc record are the same in full, and so are the arc and clock lines of the tail; the rest of the tail was not compared, since its random pulse line differs from run to run on the tip too). With it on, after the planner answers and the react-not-resolve code guard has run, one Quick-profile call (module "story-director arc judge", 20 s limit, 500 tokens out) judges the plan's beats, clocks and agendas (hooks and secrets are not judged) against the world: the Active world events, Still to come, the conditions in the party's realm, the protected cast (names), the hidden lore (titles only; the text of a hidden entry is never sent, from the digest or the key-matched entries) and the lore digest (titles, then the entries whose keys match the scene's place, within a size cap). The answer is one line per item: `accept`; `reshape` with the corrected line (read again by the strict plan reader and the guard; a line that does not read, is another kind, repeats another item's name or decides an Active event is a refusal); or `refuse`, either `event "<title>"` (the adopted note, the guard's own text) or a short reason (note: "Refused: this <beat|clock|agenda> was left out: <reason>."). An item the judge does not answer stays. The notes go on the arc card and, once accepted, in the accept receipt, as the guard's do; if every item is refused the plan cannot be used. A call that fails, times out or answers in no readable way leaves the guard's result (the plan is never blocked), and nothing is asked again: the tally (`judge: { calls, accept, reshape, refuse, fell }`) and the world note travel in the accept command's payload and are stored on the arc, so a swipe and swipe back of the plan's own reply (a swipe is a reply with no plan, so the arc goes with it and comes back), an edited Re-read of it or of a later reply, a middle delete, a rewind, a reload or a chat change reuses them; the note was written for the plan as the planner answered, not as the reader may edit it before accepting (the draft is kept per chat in the browser; accept never calls a model). The same call writes the arc's world note (at most about 120 tokens, cut by code): the tail adds a line "Arc and the world: <note>" under the arc line while the arc runs and the switch is on; a re-plan writes a new note with its arc. The note is an ordinary line under the Director's existing tail total (no budget was raised, so the off output cannot change); on a busy reply it is cut before the arc line. `chronicle.world.brief` gains `protectedCast` and `hidden` (lore titles) when there are any. Settings › Director calls this game year now counts the judge's calls (by the year the arc began in; a plan nobody accepted is not counted) and the open templates' calls (the ledger keeps `open.year` and `open.yearCalls`): the counter used to read a field the ledger never wrote and always said 0. The judge is also told the secrets not yet learned, under a never-reveal line (the planner's brief list; WEFIXSEAM). Not built: the judge never sees hooks or a `! world` line; a daily pass; the Director confirm screen. The judge is told the unlearned secrets, so code keeps them out of its free text: a NOTE that shares a run of words with a secret's text (three words, two of them four letters or longer; four words whatever their length; or a secret under four words whole) is dropped (no `Arc and the world:` line), such a refusal reason, or the judge's own `<n>: refuse: secret`, becomes `Refused: this <beat|clock|agenda> was left out: it touches a secret not yet learned.`, and such a reshaped line is not used (the existing "could not be used" refusal). It catches a secret quoted or re-punctuated, not one paraphrased. The system text adds the `refuse: secret` form and "The NOTE, the reasons and the corrected lines never name, quote or hint at hidden lore or a secret not yet learned." (WEFINALFIX).
- **Arc planner: react, never resolve (WEARC).** Behind `story-director.world-events`; with it off the planner's brief, its answer and the plan are as before (the rule is added only with the switch on). The brief opens with the rule: "World events resolve by the world's own rolls. Plan how the party and the world react to an Active world event (rumours, preparations, refugees, a letter, a choice), never how it ends: no beat, clock or agenda may decide it, end it or show its outcome (no coronation while a succession is undecided)." A code guard backs it, on the planner's answer only: a planned beat, clock or agenda whose text names an Active event (its title or id) together with one of that event's outcomes, or names an outcome by an id-like name (with a dash, underscore or digit), is left out, with the note "Refused: this <beat|clock|agenda> decides how the world event \"<title>\" ends; the world resolves it by itself. Plan a reaction instead." on the arc card (the review's list) and, once the plan is accepted, in the accept receipt's summary (kept in the command's payload, so a replay says the same; the host's Problems list takes a command's problem only from a capture, so it is not used). If nothing is left the plan cannot be used. `chronicle.world.brief` gains `guard` (id, title and outcome names of each Active event). The free-text judgement (a beat that decides an event without naming an outcome id) is the Director judge's (WEJUDGE, above).
- **Pack install check up to spec v0.14.10 (WEPACK2).** The check accepts and keeps a condition's `open`, `content.regions`, `start.world`, `@@place`, `date:` and `arc-ended:` anchors, `"on"`, weight expressions, thresholds in `likelier_if` / `less_if`, open templates (`per`, `open` menu with ladder direction, `arc`, `fallback`, `requires`, `requires_any`, `likelier_if`, `less_if`), `set:` / `cleared:` gate terms naming a place, a number `age`, and refuses `place`, `region`, `route`, `realm` as ids. Check only, except open templates (WEOPEN, above): the engine side of the rest is not built. Structural, not behind the switch. A `major` world event whose `where` is `{ kinds }` with no `region` or `realm` gets a warning (the pack loads): it counts against the major-event caps of every realm with such a place (WEFINALFIX).
- **World events: the v0.10-v0.14.9 seams (WEV10).** All behind `story-director.world-events`; with it off every output is as before. **Event moves and the living cast** (rulings 8, 37, seam audit E1): the daily step's moves are written into the cast store (`where`, `doing`, a new optional `base` slot `{ at, since, routine, by }`, `since` = now; a journey under way is cleared), a release clears the base it set, an unmet person's death sets `alive` false (and the must-happen death the world's cue applies to someone with You, WEFIXSEAM); the day planner is told a based person's base and routine ("staying at <place> because <event title>" when the event gives none), its moves away from the base are dropped by code and no goal pull starts a journey or meeting for them; a withdrawn meeting does not put back someone moved after it. The story's own bases (`cast.story`, a new system step after the reply): a narrator `where:` on a pack person's dossier, a party leave (a stays record, "waits for You at <place>"), a rejoin (cleared); what was last seen is kept in a new optional `told` slot. **With You** (rulings 33-34): someone in the party (not left) or in the scene is not moved by an event or an evacuation; the Journal says "<name> stays with You — <title>" and a deferred move waits for the first tick after they part (dropped if the event no longer holds it).
