A death of someone with You is refused unless the event says `even_in_party`; then it is a must-happen line in the world note ("<name> dies here: <summary>"), applied when NPC State records the death or by code after 3 replies (`chronicle.world.cue`, a new system step; Problems note); the daily step now knows who has been met (it used to count everyone as unmet). **from** (rulings 35, 38, 40): a move or death with `from` applies only in that place's group; a move without it only while the person is still at their start; `"anywhere"` moves them wherever they are; a death without it applies anywhere. Named moves apply before the same event's evacuation, which leaves the people they placed (ruling 39). **Learned secrets** (ruling 32): `learned:` anchors and `writes_fact` in the install check and the step; `chronicle.world.learned` (a new system step after the reply's daily step) records NPC State's opened pack secrets in the ledger. **Arc planner** (ruling 9): the query `chronicle.world.brief` and the planner's brief carry each Active event as a world-event thread (now, then "outcome decided by the world"), "Still to come" and the conditions in the party's realm. The chronicle's after-reply hook now also runs for a reply with no story lines, only to count a pending death cue. Not built: the react-not-resolve teaching and judge (owner wording), the open-template cast move, a source for `learned:lore/…`.
- **World events aligned to the author spec v0.14.4 (A, D, E, F; WEALIGN).** All behind `story-director.world-events`; with it off every output is as before (checked byte for byte against the world/engine tip and its base). **Conditions:** a ref is read as the condition's declared scope through the packs' map, the same reading the daily step uses: ids, names and aliases; a realm is a `realms` entry (no longer a region or a pack name) and a region in no realm is in the Wilds; a route by its `name` (default `From-To`; the corridor is no scope of its own). A place ref on a region condition is refused, and a clear acts at the scope the condition is held (a region clear clears it for every place in it). Timers are whole days, at least one ("6 hours" and "0 days" are 1 day). `chronicle.conditions` takes `at` (a place, region, realm or route): the conditions that hold there through the scope hierarchy (the city a district sits in, its region, its realm; the daily step's local-clear masks apply). Clear beats set for the whole day across the world line and the daily step: the conditions cleared today are kept in `chronicle.ledger` (a new optional field, a slot) and nothing sets them there again that day. The `set:`/`cleared:` anchors a world line or the Director makes go to the same ledger, and the next daily step takes them into its anchors, which keep the first occurrence for good (seam audit E24). A `placeStatus` condition is refused for the narrator and the Director where a protected cast member lives (or in a district under it); a minor template that would set one is not placed there; a city's `placeStatus` covers its districts (no templates there; the whole group evacuated to the nearest place outside it). **World line:** a realm condition takes the line's place's realm; a route condition (or any other ref) is written `+name@ref`; the one-line cap counts only lines with `conditions:` (seam audit E38). **News:** what the daily step fires and ends becomes one chronicle entry per occurrence (`by: event:world.<id>`, a template's `event:world.template.<id>`; a fixed id from the event and the day, so a Re-read or a later reply's record never makes a second; `reach`; the place it happened, or the seat of its region or realm; dated on its own day, so news of a long skip has already travelled); the end resolves it and is news of its own (E33, E34). Couriers go at the route class speeds of spec §1 (`routeKinds`; the old single pace only for a route kind with no class), routes blocked or slowed by conditions and doubled at a `placeStatus` end, multiplied together; where no courier route joins two places, reach steps (the Wilds are no realm here). News stops at its reach. A world happening is heard when its courier comes (`tag: 'fact'`); a world line's happening is heard at once, fact at its own place and rumour elsewhere until the courier time. News from a hidden place the party has not reached names its region. The News card shows the place and state as heard here and when and how the news came (E36). The World pulse never makes a rumour of a world happening (E35). **World note:** it takes the [World] lines' place while the switch is on (they overlap, E23); its head (the place status, then the conditions here, narrowest first, each with its `means` and told effects, e.g. "all goods ×1.3, grain ×2") is pinned, then the two newest news items heard here and today's world happening at the scene (new query `world.cueToday`). New model-facing note wording, for the owner's approval: "Status here: …", conditions as "name at ref: means (told effects)". A thread kind `world-event` is reserved for world-event mirrors (E32): valid in a saved chat, no capture kind, outside initiative, offers, lapses and News; nothing writes it yet.
- **World conditions store (job A).** `chronicle.conditions` keeps named states of the world (`grain-dear`, `roads-closed`) as `"<name>@<ref>": { name, scope, ref, since, fadesAt, why, by, rev }`, `scope` being place, route, region or realm. The commands `chronicle.condition.set { name, scope?, ref, fades?, why, by }` and `chronicle.condition.clear { name, ref, why, by }` (never a click) are refused with a note when the name is not in the pack's declared list, the scope is not the declared one, or the ref is not a known place, route, region or realm; at most 64 conditions are active and 8 per ref. `fades` is a duration ("10 days", "1 season" = 90 days, "2 months" = 30 days each, "until cleared"); left out, the pack's own. Before each reply a settle step (`chronicle.conditions.settle`) removes the ones whose end has come, with a receipt. Queries `chronicle.conditions({ ref?, scope?, name? })` (active ones only) and `chronicle.conditionNames()` answer null while the feature is off. A pack may declare them as `content.conditions: [{ name, scope, fades?, means }]` (an optional section of pack format 3, kept only when given; `fades` defaults to until cleared). With the feature off no command or query answers, the chronicle has no `conditions` field and every output is as before. Nothing the model reads changes. The condition calls (job B) and the world line's `conditions:` (job D) read and write it through these queries and commands. Since WEALIGN (above): refs are read as the declared scope through the packs' map (a realm is a `realms` entry, not a region or a pack name), timers are whole days, `at` reads the hierarchy, and a same-day clear is not undone.
- **World events: the condition language and the event fields (job B).** With the switch on, a pack event's `when` may call `condition('name')` (a world condition is active at the event's `where`, anywhere when it has none), `condition('name', 'ref')`, `active('event-id')` (the event happened on this line of the story and no `fired:` or `set:` anchor in its `ends_when.any_of` holds) and `fact('text')` (a Journal record mentions the text, any case); conditions are read only through the `chronicle.conditions` query and facts through `director.journal`, so a swipe, delete, rewind or Re-read changes the answer with the store. The pack event fields of the author spec v0.6 §3 (`kind`, `certainty`, `reach`, `severity`, `anchor_mode`, `where`, `requires`, `window`, `chance`, `likelier_if`, `less_if`, `pre_empted_by`, `outcomes`, `effects`, `ends_when`, `cooldown`, `summary`) are checked in the spec's strict grammar and stored: `chance` as "N% over the window" or "N% per season" (recurring), odds modifiers as `×N` (a percent or bare number is refused), the anchor vocabulary (`set:` `cleared:` `fired:` `ended:` `lapsed:` `died:` `departed:` `fact:`, a season, `start`) in `requires`, window `after` (plus an optional duration), modifiers, `pre_empted_by` and `ends_when.any_of`, effects as `name@ref: duration` or `{ condition, at, for }` (stored as objects), `ends_when` as an object, and outcome weights `power:` or `score:`. Every place, route, region, realm (and the Wilds), power, score, condition, cast member and event named must exist in the pack's `packs.content` sections (a section the pack lacks is not checked); `deadline` is no longer a field. Bad ones make the event invalid, with the reason. Nothing uses the fields yet: the daily trigger is a later job, and an event still needs `id`, `cue` and `when` as today. With the switch off the new calls are unknown names and the fields are not read, so pack events behave exactly as before. A ref is matched in any case, as the store matches it. Spec events now install in `content.worldEvents` (see the pack install check below); a legacy `content.events` entry reads the calls at install whatever the switch says.
- **World line carries conditions (job D).** With the switch on, the narrator's `+ world Name — text | state: | place | factions` line may add `| conditions: +name, -name` (and a `region:` label for region or realm conditions): each name is checked against the pack's declared list and set or lifted through `chronicle.condition.set` / `chronicle.condition.clear` (by `narrator`); what the command would refuse (an unlisted name, a place or region the world does not know, no story date yet, a full cap) is found before the call and is a note on the changes card, and the world line itself stands. A region or realm condition with no `region:` label takes the region of the line's place. A route condition (or any other ref) is written `+name@ref` (WEALIGN). A reply may carry one world line with `conditions:`: a second such line is refused with a note (the reply is marked in the chronicle's scratch field `worldLine`); lines without conditions are not capped (WEALIGN, seam audit E38). The teaching sentence is owner-approved (17 Nov) model-facing text, sent only while the switch is on. With the switch off nothing changes: the plain line runs and `conditions:` is ignored.
- **World events: news arrives over the routes (job E).** With the switch on, news of a chronicle entry reaches each place after a delay worked out from the entry's start (`since`) and its place: the courier's time along the shortest chain of routes (new `atlas.courier` query, `modules/atlas/atlas/courier.js`, still used for places only the atlas knows; between the packs' places WEALIGN goes by the route class speeds above, and **Settings › World: news travels this many km a day by courier**, default 60, is the pace of a route kind with no class), and where no chain of routes joins the two places, tiers: the same place at once, the same region 1 day, the same realm 4 days (a place carries a realm only when the atlas gives one), anywhere else 10. Two new queries, `world.newsAt { entryId, place }` → `{ arrivesAt, via: 'route' | 'tier', km?, days? }` and `world.heardHere { place, now }` → the entries whose news has reached the place (default: the scene's place and the Director's clock), answer null with the switch off. Used by the Journal's news (the World screen's News list); the narrator's [World] lines are replaced by the world note while the switch is on (WEALIGN). Nothing is stored: a swipe, an edited Re-read, a middle delete, a rewind or a reload gives the same answer from the same story and clock. A rumour (the World pulse's) is never held back, and an entry with no place or no start time is heard at once; since WEALIGN a world line's far happening is heard at once as rumour, and every entry carries its reach and tag (above). Not built: the Journal's per-day lines (the day digest) are not filtered. Nothing the model is told changes with the switch off (the model-facing text is unchanged either way).
- **World note (job F).** While the feature is on, code adds a short **[World now]** block in the reply's tail (in place of the [World] lines since WEALIGN), about 160 tokens at most: the conditions active at the scene (its place, then its routes, kept as "From-To", its region and realm), the two newest world entries whose news has reached the scene's place, and the day's authored cue when a pack has one. It reads `chronicle.conditions`, `world.heardHere` and `world.cueToday` only (the first two are the world engine's other jobs; with none of them in the host the note is empty), keeps nothing of its own, and so a swipe, a middle delete, a rewind, a reload and an edited Re-read give the note the state gives. With the feature off no line is added and the tail is the base's, byte for byte. A news item whose tag at that place is still rumour is written with " (rumour)" after its title (e.g. `Border War (rumour) — Raiders cross the river`; the tag is the entry's `tag: 'rumour'`, or a pulse rumour event's `rumour` text); a fact has no tag. The wording is approved by the owner (17 Nov) (`modules/world/chronicle/world-note-parts/build.js`). `world.cueToday` exists since WEALIGN (the world happening that fired today at the scene); the note's lines and order are as the WEALIGN entry above says. A rumour about a happening already heard at the place as fact is left out of the news line, so one happening never fills both news slots (WEFINALFIX).
- **World events: the daily step (job C).** With the switch on, after each reply's lines code checks the story clock (Story Director's chronicle part, afterCapture). A reply that moved no clock (no time line) moves it on by one hour (`director.advance`, cause `world-clock`); a time line always beats it. A reply that crossed into a new story day carries `chronicle.world.daily`, which steps every day from the last stepped one to today, one at a time (the first step of a chat starts at today; nothing before it is simulated). Each day: condition timers fade; displacements whose condition cleared lapse; the pack's spec events run in the fixed order (set-in-motion, chain stages, one-offs, recurring; by id), then the minor templates. Events: Dormant / Eligible / Active / Ended / Lapsed / Replaced; `requires` and `requires_any`; `pre_empted_by`; the chain cascade (a stage whose only path Lapsed or was Replaced goes the same way the same day; a `requires_any` stage only once every path is gone); a lapse is recorded at the first day after the window, terminal for one-offs and chain stages; `anchor_mode` first/each. Today's chance: the window or per-season chance as a daily rate, odds modifiers on the daily chance, the set-in-motion hazard 1 − (1 − 1/d)^m (certain on the last day; a set-in-motion event whose requirements fail on its last day lapses with a warning); a chance of 0 is not rolled ("blocked (×0)"). The draw is seeded by the campaign seed (drawn once, kept in the chat), the day and the event id, so a swipe never rolls again. Caps: 2 active majors per realm (the Wilds is one), 4 in the world, 1 new major per realm per 45 days; a set-in-motion event fires over them, takes its slots and restarts the timer; a success blocked by a cap is discarded and the event rolls again the next day. Firing picks an outcome (a number, or `power:`/`score:` standing + 100) and applies the event's effects, then the outcome's: clears, sets (no duration on an event = held while it is Active; on a template or `on_end` = the condition's `fades`; at least 1 day; a re-set keeps the longer time; a clear at a ref holds for the rest of that day), powers and scores (clamped to −100..100, no per-deed limits), facts (anchors `fact:<id>`), and named cast (a move is a `displaced` record `{ to, by, until, routine? }`, a death sets `died`; both leave a `died:`/`departed:` anchor and a line on the step's card (the rumour at the place left is not kept anywhere yet: there is no Journal or news entry); a person with `canDieUnmet: false` is not killed, with a warning; templates never touch cast); a `placeStatus` condition set on a place moves its residents to the nearest suitable place. `on_end` applies when an event Ends, not when it Lapses or is Replaced; `ends_when: "instant"` ends the same day. Recurring events pick a place where their `requires` hold (size-weighted; `@place`, `@region`, `@realm` bind to it) with their cooldown per place. Minor templates roll daily (their own "N% per season", else 70% per season), are placed size-weighted by class (capital/city > town > village > outpost = site; class `none` never, unless `where.kinds` names the kind), at most 2 per region per 7 days, with per-place cooldowns, and never on a place under a `placeStatus` condition unless they need it there (a clearing path such as reconstruction). The step keeps its state in `chronicle.daily` (an optional field, absent until the first step, kept as a one-entry list so each step writes it whole: a later step still replays when the reply of the first one is deleted) and writes `chronicle.conditions` (a change to an entry an earlier reply made is marked so it is passed over if that entry is gone; a hold whose event is not Active in the step's state is released); everything is recorded on the reply that carries the step: a swipe or delete of it takes it back, a Re-read runs it again on the new lines, and later replies keep what they recorded. Its card lists what fired, ended, lapsed, was replaced or faded; a skip of more than 30 days adds "N world days simulated"; its warnings go to Problems on that reply. A condition an Active event holds carries `holds` (and `open` when an "until cleared" setter shares it): the settle step leaves it and `chronicle.conditions` counts it active. `chronicle.condition.set` (world lines, the director) now keeps the longer remaining time and any event holds, as the spec's holders rule says. `time.lastLine` does not count the fallback hour, so the note's "the clock has not moved for a while" reminder still works. A route with no `name` is named "From-To", as the conditions store keeps it. With the switch off nothing of this runs: no step, no extra hour, no `daily` field (outputs byte-identical to world/engine and to its base). Not built here: writing deaths and moves into NPC State (they stay in `chronicle.daily.cast`, and every pack person counts as unmet); a tempo setting (tempo is 1; the step itself scales by any tempo 0.25-4); season anchors; drift and the per-deed limits; major templates (the Director's); the warning when an edit removes a later firing's cause; `@route` binding. Real packs now carry spec events, realms and templates (see the pack install check below); events with a power or score threshold are left out by the step with a warning until the engine reads thresholds.
- **Pack install check for the world pack (spec v0.13).** A pack may carry `content.lore`, `placeKinds`, `routeKinds`, `realms`, `powers`, `scores`, `worldEvents` and `templates`, conditions with `effects` and `placeStatus`, places with `id` and `weight`, named routes (`name`; two routes between the same places must each be named; `hours` on a sea route) and a `companion` / `figure` cast, and it installs whatever the switch says (the check is structural; with the switch off the parts are kept and unused, outputs as before). Each broken rule is a Problem naming the path: unmapped kinds, `@` or `: ` in names, duplicate ids across realms/regions/places, undefined conditions, powers, scores, events, templates and people, refs of the wrong scope, legacy ids in anchors, placeholders where they do not belong, a missing `ends_when`, thresholds outside `requires`/`requires_any`/`ends_when.any_of`, bad prices, a template with cast effects, hidden lore with no gate. A bad cast entry is refused alone (the rest load; references to it from other people are dropped with a Problem). Warnings: a `fact:` nothing writes, a place status with no way to clear it, a power also in `ripples.powers`, an unknown season. The full rules and the list of what is not built (the step skips events with thresholds; NPC State is not seeded from these cast entries) are in `docs/v2/PACKS.md`, "The world pack". The check runs in full only when a pack carries one of the eight new kinds; other packs keep the old rules, so their output is unchanged.
- **Lore digest for the director (job G).** `director.loreDigest()` answers `{ lines: [{ title, keys, line, protected? }], rev }`, one line per lore entry (first sentence cut at 100 characters, at most 4 keys) from the open character's lorebook (read by the packs' lorebook reader; the Story Suite's own tagged entries are skipped) and from the enabled packs' lore entries (`packs.content({ kind: 'lore' })`); a lorebook entry is protected when its title carries `[protected]`. `director.loreFor({ place, factions, people })` answers the full text of the entries whose title or keys match (whole words), at most about 4000 characters. The digest is kept between replies and rebuilt only when the source text changed; nothing is stored in the chat. With the switch off nothing is read, asked or answered. On Ersveil (227 lore entries, about 38k tokens) the digest is about 9k tokens.
- **World events: the daily step (job C).** With the switch on, after each reply's lines code checks the story clock (Story Director's chronicle part, afterCapture). A reply that moved no clock (no time line) moves it on by one hour (`director.advance`, cause `world-clock`); a time line always beats it. A reply that crossed into a new story day carries `chronicle.world.daily`, which steps every day from the last stepped one to today, one at a time (the first step of a chat starts at today; nothing before it is simulated). Each day: condition timers fade; displacements whose condition cleared lapse; the pack's spec events run in the fixed order (set-in-motion, chain stages, one-offs, recurring; by id), then the minor templates. Events: Dormant / Eligible / Active / Ended / Lapsed / Replaced; `requires` and `requires_any`; `pre_empted_by`; the chain cascade (a stage whose only path Lapsed or was Replaced goes the same way the same day; a `requires_any` stage only once every path is gone); a lapse is recorded at the first day after the window, terminal for one-offs and chain stages; `anchor_mode` first/each. Today's chance: the window or per-season chance as a daily rate, odds modifiers on the daily chance, the set-in-motion hazard 1 − (1 − 1/d)^m (certain on the last day; a set-in-motion event whose requirements fail on its last day lapses with a warning); a chance of 0 is not rolled ("blocked (×0)"). The draw is seeded by the campaign seed (minted once before the first reply, see the seam-audit entry below), the day and the event id, so a swipe never rolls again. Caps: 2 active majors per realm (the Wilds is one), 4 in the world, 1 new major per realm per 45 days; a set-in-motion event fires over them, takes its slots and restarts the timer; a success blocked by a cap is discarded and the event rolls again the next day. Firing picks an outcome (a number, or `power:`/`score:` standing + 100) and applies the event's effects, then the outcome's: clears, sets (no duration on an event = held while it is Active; on a template or `on_end` = the condition's `fades`; at least 1 day; a re-set keeps the longer time; a clear at a ref holds for the rest of that day), powers and scores (clamped to −100..100, no per-deed limits), facts (anchors `fact:<id>`), and named cast (a move is a `displaced` record `{ to, by, until, routine? }`, a death sets `died`; both leave a `died:`/`departed:` anchor and a line on the step's card (the rumour at the place left is not kept anywhere yet: there is no Journal or news entry); a person without `canDieUnmet: true` is not killed while unmet, with a warning; templates never touch cast); a `placeStatus` condition set on a place moves its residents to the nearest suitable place. `on_end` applies when an event Ends, not when it Lapses or is Replaced; `ends_when: "instant"` ends the same day. Recurring events pick a place where their `requires` hold (size-weighted; `@place`, `@region`, `@realm` bind to it) with their cooldown per place. Minor templates roll daily (their own "N% per season", else 70% per season), are placed size-weighted by class (capital/city > town > village > outpost = site; class `none` never, unless `where.kinds` names the kind), at most 2 per region per 7 days, with per-place cooldowns, and never on a place under a `placeStatus` condition unless they need it there (a clearing path such as reconstruction). The step keeps its state in `chronicle.daily` (an optional field, absent until the first step, kept as a one-entry list so each step writes it whole: a later step still replays when the reply of the first one is deleted) and writes `chronicle.conditions` (a change to an entry an earlier reply made is marked so it is passed over if that entry is gone; a hold whose event is not Active in the step's state is released); everything is recorded on the reply that carries the step: a swipe or delete of it takes it back, a Re-read runs it again on the new lines, and later replies keep what they recorded. Its card lists what fired, ended, lapsed, was replaced or faded; a step of more than 14 days (or of more than the longest jump setting when that is lower) adds "N world days simulated"; its warnings go to Problems on that reply. A condition an Active event holds carries `holds` (and `open` when an "until cleared" setter shares it): the settle step leaves it and `chronicle.conditions` counts it active. `chronicle.condition.set` (world lines, the director) now keeps the longer remaining time and any event holds, as the spec's holders rule says. `time.lastLine` does not count the fallback hour, so the note's "the clock has not moved for a while" reminder still works. A route with no `name` is named "From-To", as the conditions store keeps it. With the switch off nothing of this runs: no step, no extra hour, no `daily` field (outputs byte-identical to world/engine and to its base). Not built here: writing deaths and moves into NPC State (they stay in `chronicle.daily.cast`, and every pack person counts as unmet); a tempo setting (tempo is 1; the step itself scales by any tempo 0.25-4); season anchors; drift and the per-deed limits; major templates (the Director's); `@route` binding. Real packs cannot carry spec events, realms or templates yet (the pack check at install, a separate job); the step reads `content.worldEvents` and spec-shaped `content.events` once they install.
- **World events: the daily step, seam-audit fixes (E13-E21) and thresholds (v0.13 ruling 18).** With the switch on: **the campaign seed** is minted once before the first reply (`chronicle.world.seed`, a beforeGenerate step with the claim `world:seed`, stored on the message before that reply as `chronicle.worldSeed`), so a swipe or an edited Re-read of the reply that took the first step keeps the seed and its rolls; the step reads its own recorded seed first, then this one (deleting the seed's message keeps the later steps' seed; a new one is minted but not used by them). One edge: when the switch is turned on mid-chat and the next generation is a swipe of the last reply, the seed rides on that reply, so each such swipe mints a new one (no world day was kept before it). **The fallback hour and the clock face:** the fallback's `director.advance` carries `soft: true` and keeps its untold minutes in `director.time.soft` (a one-entry list `[{ at, minutes }]`, summed over fallbacks in a row, read only while the clock still stands where the last fallback left it, removed by any time line that is read); a `~ time` clock face up to those minutes earlier than the clock is now used (a plain set) instead of refused, and when the fallback crossed midnight the face on the day before is the one used if it is inside those minutes and nearer than the one ahead (the stepped day stays: nothing is undone, and the next midnight steps nothing twice); earlier than that it is refused as before. Deleting the first of two fallbacks in a row before any face loses the window (the later one's minutes no longer stand where the clock is). **The long-skip note** shows above 14 stepped days, or above the longest jump setting (Story time › longest jump, read through the chronicle's `settings.reads`) when that is lower; at the default 30 a single reply's 20-day skip now shows it (before, the 30-day note could not show from one reply at the default). **Edits:** an edited Re-read of a reply that is not the last and whose old step fired events that its new step does not fire puts "World: after this edit … no longer fired on this reply's days; the later replies keep what they recorded" on that reply's card and in Problems (the hook hands the old step's fired ids in the payload; a Re-read with no day crossed now carries a step with no days just to say so; the lost ids stay in the step's `data.lost`, so a second Re-read still warns). The later replies' records are kept as they were (history is not rewritten). This compares what fired on the edited reply; it does not check which later firing needed it. **Unmet deaths:** a pack person counts as `canDieUnmet: true` only when the pack says so (absent counts as false, seam audit E6), in the daily step. **Thresholds (spec v0.13 ruling 18):** `power:<id> >= N`, `power:<id> <= N`, `score:<id> >= N` and `score:<id> <= N` in `requires`, `requires_any` and `ends_when.any_of` (`modules/world/daily/threshold.js`), checked each day on the value the step holds when the event is checked (an effect of an event earlier in that day's order counts), not latched (Dormant again when the value falls back; Lapsed after its window like any requirement, a set-in-motion one with a warning); in `ends_when.any_of` the first day it holds ends the event. Powers and scores are kept by `id` when the pack gives one (else by name, as before), and an effect or outcome weight may also name an entry by a unique display name. The step leaves out, with a warning, an event whose threshold names an unknown id, a display name, an operator other than `>=` / `<=`, a non-whole N or an N outside the entry's range (`range`, else −100..100), or a threshold in `likelier_if`, `less_if` or `pre_empted_by` (the install check's own refusals are the pack-format job's). The values are part of the step's state recorded on the reply that crossed the day, so replay applies them and never recomputes. A Continue steps no day twice and adds no second fallback hour (tested). With the switch off none of this runs: no seed, no `soft`, no note change, no warning (outputs as before). Not built: tempo as a rule setting (tempo stays 1, so nothing can re-roll); story deeds moving powers and scores.
- **Lore digest for the director (job G).** `director.loreDigest()` answers `{ lines: [{ title, keys, line, protected? }], rev }`, one line per lore entry (first sentence cut at 100 characters, at most 4 keys) from the open character's lorebook (read by the packs' lorebook reader; the Story Suite's own tagged entries are skipped) and from the enabled packs' lore entries (`packs.content({ kind: 'lore' })`, none in pack format 3); a lorebook entry is protected when its title carries `[protected]`. `director.loreFor({ place, factions, people })` answers the full text of the entries whose title or keys match (whole words), at most about 4000 characters. The digest is kept between replies and rebuilt only when the source text changed; nothing is stored in the chat. With the switch off nothing is read, asked or answered. On Ersveil (227 lore entries, about 38k tokens) the digest is about 9k tokens.
- **World events in the new view: Settings rows and the World tab (WEUI).** Behind `story-director.world-events`. With it off: the scripted-chat harness output, the World tab's DOM (Map, News, Factions) and the world view-model are identical to the world/engine base (compared against it, not against main: main has the old `packages/` layout and cannot run the harness); the page itself gains its switch row, and `sdk.settings('story-director')` now carries `chronicle.tempo: '1'` (the settings-defaults golden gains it) and the chronicle settings schema one new field. **Settings › World and time** (Time group) gains **World events** (the switch, off by default, one help line), **Tempo** (0.25-4, default 1; "How fast the world moves: 2 = seasons last twice as long"; shown while the switch is on) and **Director calls this game year** (read only, shown while the switch is on; `chronicle.directorCalls`, which reads `calls: { count }` from the world ledger: 0 until the Director part writes it). **Tempo is built in the engine:** the setting `chronicle.tempo` (text like the map's scales, default `'1'`, under Story Director) is read by the daily step (`world-daily/tempo.js`) and by condition fades (`conditions/fade.js`: a condition the story or Director sets with "10 days" or "1 season" fades at that × tempo, as a daily-step one does). A value that is not a number runs at 1; one outside 0.25-4 runs at 1 and is named in Problems ("Tempo 9 is outside 0.25-4; tempo 1 is used"). Days already stepped are recorded and never re-rolled when the setting changes; a swipe or edited Re-read of a day-crossing reply steps its days again at the tempo now in force, and an event's "ends after N days" is checked each day against the tempo in force. Reset World and time switches World events off and sets Tempo back to 1. **World tab:** while the switch is on, a **Conditions here** card (under the Map, News or Factions card) lists the conditions in force at the party's place, narrowest first: the ref each holds on, what the pack says it means, since when, because of what (the happening's own summary when an event set it, else the words the story or pack gave) and what it does to prices and roads; new query `chronicle.conditionsHere` (null while the switch is off), the same reading the world note uses (place, routes from it, region, realm; moved unchanged to `world-note-parts/here.js`). Nothing is drawn when none holds. The News card's heard line starts with **rumour** or **confirmed** for news the world's own courier brought. The dev harness takes `?events=1` to show both.

World events engine, core fixes (block review of the engine). With the switch on: a recurring or minor `where` with `kinds` now honours its optional `region` / `realm` (before they were read and dropped), and its realm slots in the caps come from the places it can match (a one-off with a kinds `where` took none); an anchor in `ends_when.any_of` ends an event only when it becomes true after the event fired; the news of a happening is timed by the roads as they stood when it set out, so a road closed later no longer takes back news that has come; a narrator clear of a condition that is not active leaves the day's tomb as the daily step's clear does; setting a condition whose time has passed (not yet settled) starts it anew; a nameless route with id ends gets its courier factor. A condition on the realm "the Wilds" still holds at wild places (kept, now written down in PACKS.md). Switch off: unchanged.

</details>

## 0.1.0 — 18 Nov 2026

**Highlights**

- First release as one extension: Story Block, Story Director and NPC State Delta are now Story Suite.
- A new view with tabs Now, Story, People, Party, Bag, World and Fight, and one Settings overlay.
- Combat Rules v1: one set of rules and one way to fight, on the fight board.
- Pack format 3 and the Ersveil example world.
- Fresh start: nothing is carried over from the old three.

### Added

- One extension, one drawer, one Models page, Diagnostics for parts that fail to start.
- The new view, per-reply change lists, Story › Trackers, a Foes-are-standing card, the pack offer on Now, a trip card.
- Combat Rules v1: seven stats, typed skills, foe tiers, the New character wizard.
- Activities get a tab while open.

### Changed

- The safety net now runs on the Background connection (it used to use Quick helpers).
- Story checks are success or fail, with a decisive natural 20.
- Packs use format 3; a pack can no longer tune combat numbers.
- Ersveil is converted to Combat Rules v1 as far as needed to play.

### Fixed

- Replies that forget their change block are repaired by the safety net (see the full notes).

### Removed

- The older combat rules, the roll planner, narrated fights, the Rolls and Fight board switches.
- NPC State's hidden JSON block, save points, the layout editor, the top strip.
- The separate NPC State Delta drawer and any migration from the three old extensions; the Riverside starter pack.

<details><summary>Full notes</summary>

First release of Story Suite as one extension. It brings together what was Story Block, Story Director and NPC State Delta (now called NPC State). Their own changelogs, with every earlier version, are kept in `docs/history/packages/`.

This part is for players: what is new, what is different, what is gone. The notes for developers are under "For developers" below. It starts fresh: nothing from the three old extensions is carried over (no settings, chats or sidecar files), so read the install steps in `docs/INSTALL.md` before you update.

### One extension

- Story Block, Story Director and NPC State Delta are now one extension, Story Suite. You install one thing, there is one drawer in Extensions (headed `Story Suite 0.1.0`), and NPC State's settings are a section of it. Updating later is the Update button in Extensions → Manage extensions.
- Delete the old three first (disabling is not enough). The new extension does not start while the old Story Block is still on, and it says so.
- Settings are in one place, with a Models page that lists every model call (the story, quick helpers the reply waits for, background work, the safety net that reads changes a reply forgot) and the connection each one uses. One thing moved: the safety net now runs on the Background connection; it used to run on Quick helpers, so check the two if you set them differently.
- If one part fails to start, the rest still runs; Settings → Diagnostics lists it.

### The new view

- The new view is the one you see. It has the tabs Now, Story, People, Party, Bag, World and Fight, one Settings overlay, and the same look in dark and light. The old view stays only as a hidden fallback (Settings → Tools → Developer tools) until it is removed.
- Each reply's menu and the line under it list what that reply changed (items, coin, a move, who came or left).
- Settings has a page for each part: Start, Models, Director, RPG, World and time, Money, Chat extras, People, Packs and trackers (install, switch off or remove a pack; read one from the character's lorebook), Add-ons and Tools. The switches on the module cards work, and "Reset to Standard" redraws the open page.
- Story → Trackers works: pin a tracker to Now, change its values by hand, remove one this chat made, or add one of three starters.
- Before the first reply the Now tab shows the place and the time the chat already has. The story clock also runs without a pack: a time line in a reply (a clock time, a part of the day, "next morning", "+2h") sets it while it is not set yet.
- Foes left standing (an ambush or a standoff) show a "Foes are standing" card on Now, with the offer to fight.
- A character card that ships a pack offers it on the Now tab (Install or Update, Not now).
- Now shows a trip card before the trip starts ("leaves …"), and the Story tab marks an agenda step that was told in this reply.
- Gone from the old view, on purpose: save points (to go back, restart from a message), the layout editor (Customize) and the strip across the top of the screen.

### Combat Rules v1

- There is one set of combat rules and one way to fight. Every fight is played by code on the fight board: you choose for each party member, the dice are rolled by code, and the story is told afterwards. The older rules, the roll planner and narrated fights are removed, and so are the Rolls and Fight board switches.
- A character has seven stats from 1 to 30 (STR, VIT, AGI, DEX, INT, WIS, CHA), and HP, MP and SP worked out from the stats and the level. Luck is gone: WIS covers luck, will and senses, and a character file or pack that still says LUK is read as WIS. Skills have a type (attack, area, defence, evasion, movement, heal, support, utility) and practice from 0 to 600.
- The New character wizard makes you (the name is your persona's) and has no "Who they are" field. A companion joins only through the join card: Add to party, or Edit first.
- A foe group a lost or fled fight left starts the next fight from the same HP, if the story sets the same group again. Foes are set with a tier from mob to apex; the fight set-up asks for it and follows a size rule (an even fight is at most 4 standard foes).
- Story checks (`? roll`) are success or fail, with a natural 20 decisive when it meets the DC.
- The built-in skill sets (Guild fighter, River scout, Hedge mage, Field medic, Silver tongue) carry typed skills.
- A chat that already holds a character made under the earlier rules is not converted; start a new chat. (Fresh start applies here too.)

### NPC State: lines and Refresh

- NPC State no longer asks the narrator for a hidden block of JSON. Changes to people are short lines in the reply: `+ npc` for a new person (at most four a reply, and one fill-in after the reply writes the rest), `~ npc` for a change, `~ tie` for a relationship (kin, partner, friend, ally, mentor, serves, rival, enemy, owes), `level:` when the story states a level. This makes replies shorter and the changes visible on the reply card.
- A death is now recorded by NPC State, and the narrator is no longer taught `- key Name | died`.
- The people list the narrator sees names active people only; archived, dead and long-unseen people appear only when your message names them.
- Switched on by default: the one-pass way and the rolling Refresh are on. A profile saved before this release is switched on once; after that your own choice stands.
- Refresh (the quiet catch-up that fills in slow fields: personality, speech, background, mannerisms and the rest) is rolling. Each person waits N replies after their last Refresh (setting "One-pass catch-up", default 4, 0 = off), and each reply refreshes up to K people (new setting "NPCs refreshed per reply", 1 to 3, default 1), longest-waiting first, most-seen next. Your own Refresh button counts. It can read messages SillyTavern has hidden (for example by a memory extension), but not swiped-away or deleted replies.
- A person's ⋯ → Refresh in the dossier now runs a catch-up for that person (before, it did nothing).
- Quotes in the dossier show as `#12`, the message number SillyTavern shows.

### Replies that forget their change block

- Every reply is asked to end with a `<Changes>` block, empty when nothing changed, so a missing block means something was forgotten.
- Repair is on by default (Settings → Models → Calls, "Repair a reply's missing changes"). When a reply has no block, one quick call reads the reply and writes the changes it plainly shows: items, coin, a move, a quest or lead, the time. It never writes experience, loot, rewards, heals or rolls.
- The repaired lines are marked "Assist" on the reply's sheet. **Discard repair** on the sheet takes the repair out and the reply is not repaired again. Swipes, going back, deleting a reply in the middle, rewinding, reloading and Re-read all keep what was stored or discarded.
- The repair does not list a lead it already sees as open; it moves the clock when the reply's actions take time, even with no clock time in the text; and a `<Changes>` tag that is only mentioned in the text, inside code marks, is not read as a block.
- If a reply ends up with no recorded changes, the Problems list says so and the new view shows "No changes recorded — Re-read" under the latest reply. The Problems list is kept with the chat and comes back after a reload.

### Known limits

- How often the narrator writes the change block depends on the model; the repair fills the gaps but is a guess from the reply, so check the changes card when it matters.
- Discard repair works only on the latest reply.
- The "No changes recorded" badge is on the latest reply only.

### Activities

- An open interaction (a fight, a mini-game or any module's activity) gets a tab named after it, shown only while it is open. There is no general "Scene" tab any more; the word Scene stays for the Here card and the Scene sheet.

### Packs, format 3

- World packs use format 3. A pack names the Suite version it needs with `requires.suite`; a pack whose format is newer than 3 is refused.
- A pack can list its foes by tier (name, tier from mob to apex, optional level and notes); a foe it does not list takes the tier the fight set-up gives it.
- A pack can no longer tune combat numbers: the numbers are fixed by Combat Rules v1 (a pack that sets them loads, with a warning, and the numbers are ignored).
- The example world Ersveil is converted to Combat Rules v1 only as far as needed to play (skills have explicit types; its two luck items use WIS). Its story is not rewritten. The small Riverside starter is no longer shipped as an example; it lives on as a test file.

### Removed

- The older combat rules, the roll planner, narrated and manual fights, the Rolls and Fight board settings.
- The hidden JSON block of NPC State, and `- key Name | died` for the narrator.
- Save points, the layout editor and the top strip in the new view.
- The separate NPC State Delta drawer, and any migration from the three old extensions.
- The Riverside starter pack as a shipped example.

</details>

<details><summary>For developers</summary>

- **The old view's survivors move out of `ui/legacy` (B4-3).** Files that live code, the packs and the modules still use were moved with `git mv`, every import and path string re-pointed, no behaviour change; the old view imports them from the new places. `ui/legacy/director/` → `modules/director/`; `diagnostics-parts/` → `core/diagnostics-parts/`; `extras.js` and `extras-parts/` → `modules/scene/`; `ui/scene-tab.js` and `inventory-tab.js` → `modules/scene/domains/core/`; the screen data and its validators (D1: pack and module screens stay for the Template tab): `view/template/`, `view/css.js` and `view/css/`, `view/bind*` → `packs/template/`; `view/screens.js` → `packs/screens/registry.js`; the default screens (`screens/` except the old view's own Save points, ready hint and UI sample) and `inspect.js` → `packs/screens/defaults/` and `packs/screens/inspect*`; `layout/` → `packs/layout/`; `view/widgets/`, `view/icons/`, `scroll`, `modal-stack`, `gear-form`, `hero-edit` → `packs/widgets/` and `packs/icons/`; `ui/dom.js` → `packs/dom.js`. The prompt-size and sent-text helpers (`promptSize`, `sentText`, `redactSentOf`) → `core/sent/`, the settings text they read → `core/settings-text/`, and the list-field lines → `core/list-lines/`; `host-ui.js` still re-exports the three helpers, and the new view's Tools page and the Diagnostics report import them from `core/sent/`. One shim stays in the old view, `ui/legacy/screens/home.js`, so `ui-host/setup.js` keeps its listed line; it goes with the host in B4-4. The tests of the template, widgets, icons, layout and Director screens moved with them (`tests/packs/…`, `tests/modules/director/screens/`). Nothing was deleted; the dry-run card text finds the moved files by their new paths. The full table is in `scratch/batch/SMALL/night/B4MOVE.REPORT.md`.
- **The v1 Story Suite handshake is removed (B4-2).** NPC State no longer writes `globalThis.StorySuite`, announces itself to a v1 registry, offers the Source of truth settings or the "Manage all in Story Suite" button, or reads the `sourceOfTruth` setting (a saved one is dropped). The scene follows Story Block's `scene.present` and the calendar follows Story Director, as they did by default. `legacy/runtime-suite.js`, `suite.js`, `suite-handshake*` and `story-block-tabs.js` are gone; the live parts (`npcs()`, `npcProfiles()`, the reply preview, the dossier target) are in `modules/people/npc-api/` and `runtime/runtime-npc-api.js`. Backup: `scratch/backups/retire-b4h-handshake`.
- **Repair fixes from live round 1 (CHGFIX5).** (LR1-1) The repair's state lines now list the open hooks and quests by name (`Open hooks: …`, `Open quests: …`, from the Director's active threads, `core/runtime-parts/open-threads.js`; the newest 20 of each, at most 600 characters a line, placed before Carried; the state limit is 2500 characters and the open lines are never cut by it, the other lines give way, `modules/assist/repair-parts/state-fit.js`) and its foot says a lead already listed is never added again, whatever words the reply uses: write ~ or - for it only when the reply changes or closes it. (LR1-2) A `<Changes>` tag named inside inline code (backticks) or a closed fenced code block is a mention, never a block: the block finder looks for tags in the reply with those spans blanked (`core/capture/blocks-parts/code.js`; bodies are read from the real text), so such a reply with no real block is repaired, and the "cut off" mark (`open`) is written only when the reply ends inside a really opened block. A real block that a model wraps in a code fence is still read (CHGFIX6): a fence is blanked only when its body has no line starting with the `<Changes>` tag (open or close), so a fence-wrapped block, one cut off inside a fence and then continued, and one after a stray odd fence are all one block, as before. The stored T10 text of live round 1 reads as main did (no block) with or without this; what made the suite store its empty "open" block could not be reproduced from the stored text, so only the code-span cases are pinned. (LR1-3) The repair can move the clock by minutes: its time line is "! time +Nm", "+Nh" or "+Nd", and its foot asks for an estimate whenever the reply's actions take time even with no clock time (walking across the village and buying bread is about ! time +20m); never a date, and no time line when no time passed. The wall already accepted `+Nm`. (LR1-5) The Problems list is kept in the chat's metadata (`storySuiteProblems`, last 100 rows, `core/turn/problem-keep.js`) and comes back after a reload; each chat has its own list (CHGFIX6: a chat change clears the list at once and writes nothing to the new chat's metadata until its own rows are read, so the old chat's rows never reach it). The flag-off identity test no longer compares that key, and the native-heal test expects the earlier row to be listed again, not told again.
- **A reply's changes are never lost silently: the block is always asked for, a repair on a miss, and a visible warning (CHGDROP).** (1) The three prompt texts the owner approved on 18 Nov: the static rules say "leave out what did not change; when nothing changed, still write the block, empty: <Changes></Changes>." (the old "and the whole block when nothing did" is gone); the closing cue reads "End every reply with the <Changes> block, after the story and outside your thinking; when nothing changed, write <Changes></Changes>." (the npc_state_delta clause is dropped; `closingCue()` takes no argument); NPC State's people cue (`npc-meters-prompt.js`) reads "Whenever anything changed, write it in the <Changes> block at the very end of the reply; a lie, secret, taste, tie or turning point gets its own line." The static prompt golden and the flag-off fixtures (and the two `off-*.json` prompt hashes) were regenerated for these texts only. (2) **Repair on a miss** (`modules/assist/repair.js`, new setting `assistRepair`, "Repair a reply's missing changes" in Settings › Models › Calls, **on by default** (the owner approved the prompt on 18 Nov)): when a captured reply (live, swipe, or a Re-read of the latest reply when it still has none) has no `<Changes>` block, one call on the Quick connection, with a prompt of its own (`repair-parts/heads.js`: the reply, the state lines Place, Present, Equipped, Wallet, Carried, the story date and time, and the kinds' grammar), writes the block. It runs whatever the safety net's switch says, with the same 20 s time-out and the 600-token answer limit. The lines are stored in the reply's `<Changes-assist>` section, applied as Assist lines (marked as Assist lines on the changes card; a reply's whole repair can be discarded from its sheet, see (4); there is no per-line undo: edit the reply's hidden section and Re-read, rewind, or delete the reply) and replay the same on swipe, swipe back, middle delete, rewind, reload, Re-read and an edited Re-read, with no second call. Unlike the safety net it may write `+ coin`, `- coin`, `+ item` and `- item` (the wall's `repair` option; never XP, loot, rewards, heals or rolls; after send the strict wall stays) and the quest, hook and milestone lines (the people's `~ npc` lines have no grammar line, so NPC State's own scan of a reply with no block still covers people). The three texts use the bare tags (<Changes></Changes>), not code-formatted ones: a model that copies backticks writes a block the reader cannot find. (2b) Review fixes: a repair that failed or timed out is asked again by Re-read (an answer, lines or nothing, is not); the Problems row is one per reply and its text is the latest outcome; the repair prompt has its own short lines for coin (only coin that changes hands in this reply, not prices talked about), items (never + item for what is already under Carried), hook and quest (accept or close only what the reply plainly shows) and time ("~ time 9:30 am" for a clock time today, "! time +Nh" / "! time +Nd" for a stretch it estimates from the story time given, never a date), weather is not one of its kinds, {{user}} in its grammar lines is the persona's name, and the story time shows as "Story time now: 08:00 am, day June 3". (3) **A miss is never silent:** a reply with no block and nothing stored for it (the repair is off, failed, timed out or found nothing) gets a Problems row (`NO_CHANGES`, once per reply) and, in the new view, a "No changes recorded — Re-read" badge on a line of its own under the latest reply's row (the old view's note already showed one; it now also stays away when a repair stored lines). (4) **Discard repair (CHGFIX3):** the new view's reply sheet marks the lines Assist wrote (the repair's and the safety net's) with an "Assist" tag, and a reply that holds a repair has one **Discard repair** button: the repaired `<Changes-assist>` comment (its header says `repaired`) is taken out of the reply's text, a comment headed `discarded` stays so the repair is never asked again for that reply, and the reply is read again (when the Re-read refuses, the text is put back). The mark is in the text, so a swipe back, a reload, a Re-read (also an edited one), a middle delete and a rewind keep it discarded. The safety net's lines are never touched. The Models row now shows ON when nothing is stored (it read OFF while the repair ran), and its hint says repaired lines can be discarded per reply. Also the two prompt tweaks the owner approved on 18 Nov: the coin line reads "+ coin 30 copper | from: who (- coin 8 copper | to: who): only coin that changes hands in this reply, with the amount AND the coin name the reply uses; never a price that is only talked about" and the Wallet state line lists the chat's coins, and the time example is fixed and generic ("e.g. morning to dusk is about ! time +10h"). A repair coin line with a heal, XP or reward label is refused. The reply sheet and the ✦ button now have their own CSS in `ui/css/chat.css` (the mock's `.trsheet`, `.tra`, `.xbt` rules; there was none, so the sheet drew unstyled). A Re-read whose repair stores lines drops the earlier "nothing was recorded" row. Known limits: the new view's badge is on the latest reply only, it shows for the seconds a repair call is still running, and a reply the repair judged to have nothing to record still shows it after a reload; the sheet is the latest reply's, so only the latest reply's repair can be discarded there; a repair that ran after the reader had sent (stored as `<Changes-assist-next>`) is not marked.
- **Settings and Now fixes (new view).** Settings › Start no longer draws an empty "Gone (decided)" heading; "Reset to Standard" redraws the open page's switches and counts at once; Settings › Money's Coins hint no longer names a pack. Before the first reply the Now tab's Today card shows the place and the clock the store already has (a "Where" row, drawn only while the card is otherwise empty, so a populated Today is as before). One presence in the new view: a person is Here when NPC State says so (the reply's flag, or last placed at this place) or the scene's `scene.present` list names them (matched against the name and the aliases, any case); an empty or frozen list removes nobody NPC State has here. The chat's Here drawer, the Now Here card, the People tab and the dossier all use it.
- **The story clock works with no pack (F8W-4).** With no pack (or a pack with no start date) the clock was never set, so the story's `~ time 9:30 am` and `! time next morning` were dropped as "(not used)" and the Director skipped its rolls ("story clock not set"). Now, while the clock is not set, a time line with no date (a clock time, a part of the day, `! time +2h`, `! time next morning`) reads from the calendar's default start (the first day of its first month, year 1, 08:00) and sets the clock there (`modules/time/tracker/time-parts/default-start.js`, used by `applyTimeLine`); a line with a date the calendar can read still sets the clock as before, and while the clock carries the stored marker that it came from that default start (`time.fromDefault`, set by the first date-less line and cleared by the first date) a story's first real date is not held to the longest jump from the arbitrary start; a pack or story that starts in year 1 by a date has no marker and keeps the jump guard. A replay reads the same for an unchanged calendar setting. A line whose date the calendar cannot read (`~ time 2026-11-17 9:30 am`) is still refused with a Problem. Nothing is seeded before the first reply, so a chat with a pack start date, or one whose story names a date first, reads as it did. A story that never writes a time line still has no clock.
- **A reply's boundary hashes are stamped from its final text (F8W-9).** After a swipe and back, one block's `prefixHash` / `payloadHash` had changed while the message, state and people were equal. Cause: a reply's change block moves into the hidden comment after its blocks are stamped, so prose a model left after the block (such as a copy of the hidden marker, defanged) joined the text the hashes cover and left them stale. The hashes are now stamped again once the text is final (`store.restampHashes`, live and Re-read), so none is stale. They are read by the diagnostic `store.boundaryProblems()` and by Re-read, which compares a block's `payloadHash` with the reply's to keep the block's Source when a native checkpoint is published (a stale one lost that). `tests/core/swipe-span-hash.test.js` covers a trailing marker copy live, Re-read, Re-read with a native checkpoint on the reply, swipe, swipe back, rebuild and reload, and that Story Suite leaves every swipe a string with its `swipe_info`; SillyTavern's "swipe that is not a string" warning comes from its own `ensureSwipes` and is not written by Story Suite.
- **The dossier's Refresh now runs (F8W-10).** ⋯ › Refresh ("re-read the recent story for them") sent the engine's stage-only `npc.refresh`, which refused with nothing to do and showed nothing. The new view's port now runs NPC State's own Refresh for that person (the rolling catch-up's call: `refreshFromChat`; one model call, the one-pass default; it counts as that person's last Refresh), and the dossier shows a line under the top bar: "Re-reading the recent story for <name>…", then the result or why it could not run. NPC State's own toast still lists the fields it changed. `ui/port/people-refresh.js`.
- **Foes left standing now have an offer in the new view.** An ambush or a standoff (foes set without the reader's attack) showed only the old chat line, which the new view hides, so such a fight could not start there. The RPG's own offer (`board/offers.js`, the one behind that line) now reaches the new view through the hold bridge: a Needs-you card on Now ("Foes are standing", the foe names), the Fight tab's offer with Fight on the board and Auto-fight (no story button: nothing is held back), and the same choose as the line's buttons, so the mark on the reply and the board are identical and swipe, Re-read, delete, rewind, reload and a chat change follow the reply as before. `tests/ui/fight-offer-live.test.js` runs it on the live mount. The ‹ Chat pill's 44 px now follows the window (900 px or less), not the suite panel.
- **One-pass capture is switched on once for profiles saved before 0.1.0.** An older build saved `narrationCapture: false` into every profile on read, so profiles that ran it kept one-pass off without the owner choosing it. NPC State's settings now carry a version marker (`npcStateSettingsVersion`, 1). A profile with no marker (or a lower one) gets `narrationCapture: true` once and the marker is written; after that a player's own 'off' is kept. New profiles start with one-pass on and the marker. Nothing else about the settings changes; the settings-defaults golden gains only the marker.
- **The new view is the default.** Story Suite opens in the new view for everyone: `index.js` hides the old view's chat furniture from the first paint, then mounts the new view as soon as Story Block is ready (`ui/entry/start.js` `startView`; it no longer waits for the Extensions panel). The "new view (preview)" checkbox and the `ui.next` setting are no longer read (the key stays in the settings defaults until the old view is removed). The wand menu entry is "Story Suite"; the old view's wand entry, its two drawer buttons and its strip switch are hidden while the new view shows, and the Story Suite drawer gets the new view's own **Settings** and **Open Story Suite** buttons. The old view is a hidden fallback until it is removed: **Settings › Tools**, switch on **Developer tools**, then **Use the old view** (this device only, kept in localStorage `storySuite.ui.oldView`); it takes the new view away at once, and a ticked "Story Suite: use the old view" row under Extensions is the way back.
- **Settings › Director in the new view.** The Settings overlay now draws the Director page from the live settings: checks (difficulty when none is written, and how strongly and how far feelings move a social check, which are NPC State's), plan spoilers, the world pulse, the planner (when to plan, accept automatically, genre, messages read, longest plan, stalled after, running low, the note), the cast (new faces, unmet people dying), names and initiative (banned names, suggestions, replies before the same person acts, threads elsewhere). Each row reads the stored value over the default and a change writes the same module setting the old view's page writes (`story-director` or `npc-state-delta`), so a value set in either view is the same value; a row whose feature is off is left out and its value kept. Not built, because no setting backs them: "A miss by this much is a success at a cost", "Cast moves: messages read / longest answer" and "Scene spoilers hidden by default" (the scene spoiler is the per-chat switch on Now and in the dossier). "Plan spoilers hidden by default" is the old `Behind the scenes` setting. Banned names is one line here; the old view's box takes one name per line (a comma works in both).
- **Settings rows in the new view.** The Settings overlay's Start, Look and Tools rows (budgets, panel style, glass strength, font, text size, sending) now have their values: the sdk's `settings` function carries the host's frozen `defaults` and `schema` (`sdk.settings.defaults`, `sdk.settings.schema`; the same constants as `HOST_SETTINGS_DEFAULTS` and `HOST_SETTINGS_SCHEMA`), which the overlay was reading and finding empty. Nothing else about `settings(id)` changes; no setter is added.
- **Settings writes and resets that did nothing now work (new view).** The Look rows (panel style, glass strength, font, text size, sending) and the Tools budgets (notes per reply, share of the context, change lines per reply) had `host.*` keys no handler answered, so a change failed with "There is no command called settings.set"; `ui/port/host-settings.js` now stores them as the host's own settings, the place the old view writes them (a choice row by its label; the Solid skin is not offered). The Reset button of World and time, Director, Chat extras, Models, Look and Tools failed the same way; `ui/port/resets.js` now puts that page's settings and switches back to their registered defaults (World: the calendar, map and time settings and the weather switch; Director: its rows in Story Director and NPC State; Chat extras: the two chatter rows and the three switches; Models: the two connections, one-at-a-time, the fallback kinds and the fallback switch; Look and Tools: their own rows). The old view had no page Reset, so these are the defaults its controls start from. The Add-ons page has nothing to set and draws no Reset. **Director is always listed:** when the host holds none of its settings it shows the defaults, as the mock does (a row whose feature is off is still left out when the feature list is read). **The RPG page reads Story Block's settings through the sdk,** as Money and Chat extras do (it read SillyTavern's extension settings directly); the Models page's Calls group still reads them directly. The duplicate "Add to party on a People page" line is gone.
- **Settings › RPG and fights in the new view.** The page is drawn with live values from Story Block's own settings: Difficulty (`rpgDifficulty`; a first switch to Custom copies the numbers in force into `rpgStandard`, as the old page does), Level cap, XP rate (50 to 300 in steps of 25), Practice per skill use, Companions who join, Set up a fight from my attack (`rpgPrecheck`), Most exchanges Auto plays (`rpgAutoCap`, 9 = until the end) and Story checks use your stats (`rpgChecks`); each change writes the same key as the old view's page, with the same limits, and **Reset RPG and fights to defaults** stores each default. Not built: the mock's "Odds on the fight board", "Combat rules" text button and the separate "Rolls" row (no setting or editor behind them; the old page's Combat rules box, Custom numbers and Gear switch stay in the old view; Custom numbers are not editable in the new view yet).
- **Settings › World and time in the new view.** The page now has its rows on the live host: Calendar (Calendar choice, Era, Months, Weekdays, Leap years), Weather (Weather comes from), Map (North, East, Km per map unit, Roads wind, Water and air speeds) and Time (Longest time jump, While-away recap, Deadlines, Quiet after replies and story days, Due soon, and, while the day digest is on, Days kept whole, Days kept in all and Remind the story of the last day). Each row reads and writes the same Story Director setting the old page does (`tracker.*`, `time.*`, `atlas.map.*`, `chronicle.digest*` under `story-director`; the weather row flips the `story-director.weather` feature), so a value set in either view is the same value; the host's own limits still hold a number. No prompt text changes. **Differs from the mock:** Months and Weekdays are two text rows (the mock has one Months and weekdays Edit button; the new view has no list editor); "Hours on the road per travel day" and "Coming up looks ahead" are not drawn because the engine has no setting for them (the pack's own drive hours and the Journal calendar's span are not settings); the Calendar choice says "From the pack" without the pack's name; the calendar fields are not greyed while the choice is From the pack (they are used only with Custom, as their hints say); the "Reset World and time to defaults" button is the page's shared one.
- **Settings › Money in the new view.** The Money page (Rules) now has its six rows with live values, as the mock draws them: Coins, smallest first (one line such as `copper: 1, silver: 10`, read by the old page's own list reader; a line that does not read is named under the row and nothing is saved), Show money as (Each coin / Smallest coin only), Money word, Plural, Short form and Price scale. Each row stores the same Story Block setting the old view's Money page stores (`coins`, `coinDisplay`, `currency`, `currencyPlural`, `currencyShort`, `economyScale`, through `settings.set('story-block', key, value)`), so a value set in either view is the same value; the page reads them at open (`ui/select/money-rows.js`, `ui/select/money-lines.js`) and writes through `ui/port/money.js`, which also answers the page's Reset button. The old page's check on the coins (a name used twice, a worth out of range) is not drawn in the new page; the store and money.js still read only the usable coins. Nothing the model is sent changes.
- **Module cards' switches and "Reset to Standard" now work (new view).** The module cards on Start and on the Director and People pages (Story Block, Story Director and NPC State, including the RPG switch, which is the only RPG on/off in the new view) sent `modules.chat.<module>[.<feature>]` and `modules.reset`, which nothing answered: the switch moved on screen but nothing was stored. A new port handler (`ui/port/module-cards.js`) writes the feature store through `writes.features.set`, as the old view's switches do. A module switch acts only on that card's own switches (Story Block's Choices, Inner chatter and Your look are on the Chat extras card and are left as they are): off keeps what was on (a small note in the saved settings, `storySuite.notes`, `core/settings/card-notes.js`), and on puts exactly that back; with nothing kept it turns on the ones that start on (all of them when none does). Reset to Standard sets what the Standard preset sets (`presetSwitches`): a switch outside the presets (RPG, Gear, Inspector, Savepoints, HUD, Cards, Extractor, Encumbrance) keeps the player's value. Per-chat module sets are not built and are not part of 0.1.0, so the New chats scope switch is not drawn while no New chats set is stored; the cards say the switches apply to every chat. `tests/ui-gaps/live-commands.test.js` now also sends every Settings key the live port draws (rows, module-card switches, page Resets) and fails on any that falls through to UNKNOWN_COMMAND.
- **Settings › Chat extras in the new view.** The new Settings now has a Chat extras page: its module card (Choices, Inner chatter, Your look, moved out of Story Block's module on Start) and the Inner chatter group's two rows, "Send the last reply’s thoughts back to the story" and "Most chatter lines per reply". They read `sdk.settings('story-block')` and store `chatterInPrompt` / `chatterMax` and the three features through the same stores the old view writes (`ui/port/extras.js`), so a value set in either view is the same value; the number is clamped 1–10 by the schema. The mock's fourth switch, Trackers, is not drawn: Story Block has no such feature (trackers are on the Packs and trackers page). Scope "New chats" writes the same global stores until per-set defaults exist.
- **Settings › Packs and trackers in the new view.** The Settings overlay has the page (Setup, after Models). It lists the installed packs with their on/off switch and Remove (asks first), Import a pack file (a pack.json with its images, or a .zip) and Read from the character's lorebook, which install through the same code the old Packs panel uses (`packs/import-run.js`, now shared by both) and say what was installed, ignored or refused; the pack data modules of this chat (a switch uses the module with `dm.pin` and the hashes read now, as the old Enable button does; a module in use has no way off, so its switch is locked on); and this chat's trackers with their count of the cap. The tracker buttons (Edit fields, New tracker, From a template, Build it in the chat) close Settings and open Story › Trackers, where trackers are made and edited; they are not forms on this page. Packs are switched and removed by the port's new `packs.*` settings keys (`ui/port/packs.js`) on the same installed-packs store as the old view, so a pack changed in either view is the same pack.
- **Settings › Add-ons in the new view.** The new view's Settings has the Add-ons page (Setup, after Packs and trackers and before Tools), read live: **Installed modules** lists every module the sdk's `modules()` reports, each with its version and, when it is not running, why (`ui/select/addons.js`; it reads `sdk.modules()` each time the overlay opens, and says "none yet" with none). There is no custom-views row (the feature does not exist yet), so the page reads and writes no setting, and nothing about prompts changes. The old view has no Add-ons page; its Modules page (settings and versions of each module) stays where it is.
- **Fight tab fixes (block final review, items 1 to 4 and 8).** (1) *Edit first* on a cast member's *Add to party* card keeps the heal, defence, area (and every other) skill type; before, only `utility` survived and the others became attacks in the wizard (*Add as is* was never affected). (2) The fight's end card shows what the fight paid: each member's practice chips, and "+N XP each" when every paid hero got the same XP (v1 pays by each hero's level, so with different levels the figure is left out until the owner decides how to show it); it reads the settle's receipt, so a rebuild, reload or Re-read draws the same card. (3) The Fight tab has the stance switch: the row shows the stance's name, "now: soak N%" for a Defence stance, and the switch. Turning it off writes `args.stance = false` into that member's pick (the engine then pays nothing for the stance in that exchange); a switch left on writes nothing, so earlier records replay unchanged. The switch starts where the last exchange left the stance, and Auto keeps a stance that was switched off, off (read from the steps before it), for each hero by that hero's own latest pick: a step that has no input from a hero (a Flee staged alone, a hero who is Down) leaves that hero as the steps before left them, for Auto and for the next reply's carry. The line that says what switching off costs is not shown yet (it waits for the owner's wording). (4) The *Field medic* set's Mending now says it uses Mana (a heal always draws MP). (8) The ended fight's card has **Back to the story ›**, which goes to the chat tab; the fight still goes to the story with the next message.
- **Fight end card and stance row (owner calls, block fix 2).** The end card's member rows each show that member's own "+N XP" (new `xp` on `FIGHT_SHAPE` `end.members`, null when the fight paid that member nothing; `xpEach` stays for an equal party). The stance row now always says what the stance costs: on, "<name>: 1 Stamina each exchange, paid at its start; off when Stamina runs out" (defence adds its soak); off, "Off: no Stamina from this exchange on." (defence: "Off: soak 12%. No Stamina from this exchange on."); a magic stance (INT or WIS) says Mana. New stance fields `pool` and `cost`. Ersveil and the built-in sets already hold the owner's skill types (Binding Rune support, Mother's Hand utility, Iron Breath and Silver tongue utility); `tests/packs/ersveil-skill-types.test.js` now holds them.
- **NPC State: changes are lines, not a JSON block (in progress, N7).** The reply is asked for `<Changes>` lines only: the hidden JSON block is no longer asked for (a stray one is still removed and nothing from it is applied). The relationships paragraph (`~ npc Name | trust: up | impact: meaningful | why: …`, with `via:` while the anti-lever feature is on) is the only `~ npc` teaching; custom meters, the caps and the per-day limit add no text because code enforces them. The `~ tie` line lists nine tags (kin, partner, friend, ally, mentor, serves, rival, enemy, owes). The people list in the prompt names active people only (archived, dead and long-unseen people only when the player's message names them) under the header "People you know about (match by these names):". The fallback scan runs only when a reply has no `<Changes>` block and names someone. With lines on there is no scan by cadence (Scan every, the safety scan) in a hosted one-pass chat: a reply is scanned only by that fallback (a crowd it finds is capped at 4 new people, as a block was). The capitalised-name test skips the places Story Director's Atlas knows. The catch-up Refresh counts every hosted reply by who is in the scene, scanned or not. A person a `+ npc` line made gets one fill-in after the reply, asked once per reply: the mark is kept in the reply's journal entry, so a reload does not ask again for a fill that live dropped. In a hosted chat the capture journal (what a replay reads instead of asking the model again) is keyed by the reply's own ID and a fingerprint of its text, so a swipe back, a middle delete, a rewind and a reload find a reply's entry and an edit does not; a chat saved before the switch is moved to the new keys once (entries that match no reply are dropped). Every hosted reply has an entry; it keeps who the catch-up took and what its Refresh answered, and a fallback scan's answer, so a replay repeats the same Refreshes and the same scan with no model call. Not hosted, the journal keeps its lineage key. Still to do in N7: the compact-rules setting and the JSON block path stay for a chat with no Story Block (on `docs/merge/b4-removals.md`).
- **NPC State: four new people a reply (N7 fix).** A reply's `+ npc` lines make at most four new people, as the capture block did; the fifth and later are refused on the changes card ("Only four new people a reply; <name> waits for a later reply that names them again."), so the fill-ins they own stay at four. A hosted reply with no JSON block is no longer counted as a missing capture in Diagnostics or the dossier tools line.
- **NPC State: the Level line (N7).** While the custom Level field is on (a configured field, or Story Block's RPG feature) and lines mode is on, the rules carry one more sentence ("level: write it only when the story states a person's level outright, as a number (e.g. ~ npc Mira | level: 5); code keeps it and a line never lowers it.") and `~ npc Name | level: N` stores N (a whole number 1 to 99) on the person's Level custom field. A line never lowers a level, never replaces a rank or other words the field already holds ("C-D"), and never writes a person whose custom fields you locked; each refusal is a note on the changes card and the line's other labels still apply. With the Level field off the rules are unchanged and `level:` is an unknown label as before.
- **NPC State: changes are lines, not a JSON block (in progress, N7).** The reply is asked for `<Changes>` lines only: the hidden JSON block is no longer asked for (a stray one is still removed and nothing from it is applied). The relationships paragraph (`~ npc Name | trust: up | impact: meaningful | why: …`, with `via:` while the anti-lever feature is on) is the only `~ npc` teaching; custom meters, the caps and the per-day limit add no text because code enforces them. The `~ tie` line lists nine tags (kin, partner, friend, ally, mentor, serves, rival, enemy, owes). The people list in the prompt names active people only (archived, dead and long-unseen people only when the player's message names them) under the header "People you know about (match by these names):". The fallback scan runs only when a reply has no `<Changes>` block and names someone.
