With lines on there is no scan by cadence (Scan every, the safety scan) in a hosted one-pass chat: a reply is scanned only by that fallback (a crowd it finds is capped at 4 new people, as a block was). The capitalised-name test skips the places Story Director's Atlas knows. The catch-up Refresh counts every hosted reply by who is in the scene, scanned or not. A person a `+ npc` line made gets one fill-in after the reply, asked once per reply: the mark is kept in the reply's journal entry, so a reload does not ask again for a fill that live dropped. In a hosted chat the capture journal (what a replay reads instead of asking the model again) is keyed by the reply's own ID and a fingerprint of its text, so a swipe back, a middle delete, a rewind and a reload find a reply's entry and an edit does not; a chat saved before the switch is moved to the new keys once (entries that match no reply are dropped). Every hosted reply has an entry; it keeps who the catch-up took and what its Refresh answered, and a fallback scan's answer, so a replay repeats the same Refreshes and the same scan with no model call. Not hosted, the journal keeps its lineage key. Still to do in N7: the Level rule has no lines wording (waits for the owner); the compact-rules setting and the JSON block path stay for a chat with no Story Block (on `docs/merge/b4-removals.md`).
- **NPC State: Refresh writes the slow fields (in progress, N8; stacked on N7).** The quiet catch-up Refresh is now rolling. After each captured reply at most K people are Refreshed (the new setting "NPCs refreshed per reply", 1 to 3, default 1; beside it "One-pass catch-up", N, default 4, 0 = off, now reads "each person waits N replies between Refreshes"). A person is eligible only if they were in the last N replies and N or more replies have passed since their last Refresh (a catch-up or the reader's own Refresh button); no more than K × max(1, N-1) catch-up Refreshes happen in any N consecutive replies (N=4, K=1: 3 per 4 replies); a person not seen in the last N replies is never taken. The slots go first to an eligible person not Refreshed for 3N replies (12 at the default), longest-waiting first, then to the most seen in the last N replies, ties by last Refreshed (oldest first); the people left over keep their place and lead next time. Each reply's journal entry keeps `roll` (who was in it, who its catch-up took, whose manual Refresh came after it); the order is read from the replies the chat stands on now, so a swipe, a swipe back, an edited Re-read, a middle delete, a rewind and a reload give the same order (an edit keeps the reply's marks; a catch-up Refresh that did not finish is not counted). The picker reads back 20 × N replies. A person never Refreshed waits from the first reply they were seen in (that reply counts), so a newcomer is due after N replies and overdue after 3N, like everyone else. The reader's own Refresh counts as the last Refresh whether it changed anything or not. The old per-session counter is gone. A failed one (a model error or a timeout) shows the same note as a failed fill-in, once per person; nothing is written, and a cancelled or discarded one (a chat change, a cancel) stays silent. **Refresh (catch-up and manual) also reads the messages SillyTavern has hidden** (`is_system` set by /hide, for example by a memory extension) inside its own window, as evidence, in [mN] order. Still not read: swiped-away and deleted replies, SillyTavern's own notes (anything it stores with `extra.type`: narrator, comment, help and the rest; the ones with no type are skipped by their own marks: a tool-call record by `extra.tool_invocations`, a help or safety note by its system avatar, an Image Generation message by a generated item in `extra.media`, a bias-only /sendas by having no text but its bias) and our own faded replies. Only the Refresh window changes; the narrator prompt, Fill-in and the reply scans still skip hidden messages. **Background quotes are stored** (`profileEvidence.background`, same caps as the other slow fields; a background with no quote of the window it read is left out of a Refresh, in the person's row and in the profile row alike, as rule 3 says; the quotes are not sent in a scan prompt, so scan prompts are unchanged). Its prompt is the owner-approved NSD11 text: it is the only writer of personality, speech, behaviorProfile, mannerisms, background, home base, key relationships and the relationship summary; the slow fields need quotes (in `profileUpdates.evidence`; the prompt asks for a background quote too, and it is stored; the wording of rule 3 is a draft awaiting the owner's OK) and two or more moments or an outright statement; the dossier JSON it reads gains the stored `tastes` (at most 12) and `ties`. It returns only changed fields: no `keep` states, no mood numbers, no `tastes`, no `keyRelationshipEdges` (a returned `tastes` key is ignored). The forwarding of Refresh tastes to the story is removed (`runtime-hosted-parts/tastes.js`, backed up). The background prompt golden changes for the five Refresh prompts only; the static golden is unchanged.
- **One repository, one number.** Every place that carries the version (`package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `manifest.json` once it exists, `core/version.js`, the title of `README.md`, the head of this file) must say the same thing; `npm run release:check` fails if one disagrees, and `node scripts/release.mjs <version>` writes the number into all of them. A release is a version bump merged to main; releases are not tagged.
- **Bag data (G6).** The Bag's load card now lists its chips from `gear.view` (`carry.parts`: each member's strength, each carrying item, each mount with the party, and mounts left behind dimmed). `gear.view` gains `carriers` (the mounts, with the party or not, the riders a `where` such as "ridden by Wessel and Tam" names, and what each adds; the party's carrying items), and a transport asset in the Bag takes its "with the party" flag, riders and "+N load" from them. With a denominated currency (Settings → Coins) the purse and item worth read "3 silver 5 pennies" (or the smallest unit with base display); the three-coin split stays off, so the money editor is still hidden. Not built: seats (nothing records them, so `seats` stays null), the Bag-slot link, per-turn purse lines (they wait for receipt reading; `thisTurn` stays `[]`). Load already weighed each unit by its definition's weight, so numbers for chats are unchanged.
- **Combat Rules v1 is the only rules (combat v1-only job).** There is no rules chooser: every chat and every new sheet is made from the four v1 templates (`CHOOSER_TEMPLATES`, which the block chooser, the New character wizard, a joining companion and the Combat rules settings read). A new Character has seven stats 1 to 30 (STR VIT AGI DEX INT WIS CHA; LUK is gone), HP, MP and SP as numbers with their largest values worked out from the stats and the level, and one point per level; a Skills block has a type on every skill (attack, area, defence, evasion, movement, heal, support, utility) and practice 0 to 600, and a learn line that leaves the type out still learns an attack skill (the narrator is told `| type: attack|area|defence|evasion|movement|heal|support|utility` as required, without brackets). The four block descriptions the narrator reads are the v1 ones (Character, Skills, Foes, and the last line of Combat rules). The New character wizard builds v1: the stat steppers and radar run to 30, the line under them says HP, MP and SP, the Skills step has a Type column in place of Uses, and Create writes HP, MP and SP full with their largest values, so the first reply moves nothing. A companion the story adds (`+ party`, `best: wis`) is built the same way (every level one point; best stat and DEX first); a chat that already holds a Character made under the earlier rules keeps building the earlier blocks for a join and its Re-read, byte for byte (the party line's `about` is still read there), and keeps reading `best: luk`, until the earlier rules are removed. A character file reads WIS (a file with only LUK reads its LUK as WIS), skill types and ranks 0 to 6; a file written now has WIS and the type. The Party tab draws a v1 Character with its numbers and stats. The narrator's party-line grammar and the dice-bonus table say WIS where they said LUK; a check that names no stat and fits no skill word uses WIS. **The New character wizard makes You only, and "Who they are" is gone:** the Who step has no You or A companion choice (the name is the persona's), the Party tab's New character button shows only while the story has no You, and the v1 Character block has no About field (the wizard, the join card, the party payloads, the card and the Party tab no longer carry it; a file or a line that has `about` is read and the text ignored). A companion is made only through the join card: Add to party as before, or Edit first, which opens this wizard filled in from the join with every field editable. **A v1 fight against foes a lost or fled v1 fight left now starts from their bars:** `board/resume-v1.js` reads the chat's fight receipts, so when the story sets the same group again (same slot, exact name, level, tier and count) and the newest fight that covered the slot was lost or fled, the next fight opens with those HP; a won, unfinished or old-engine fight in between, a different group, a rewind or delete of the fight's reply, or another chat resumes nothing; a foe that fell stays down. The earlier engine is not removed yet (combat C11): a chat whose setup was made under it replays unchanged. **Things that only showed once a v1 chat could be made, fixed here:** a story check (`? roll`) in a v1 chat failed its step (a v1 character has no Soul Class, and the step hashed the missing one); a v1 Foes group counted as "no fight standing" (the Combat rules text was dropped from the prompt during a fight, the "No foes are set" note was told with foes on the sheet, and the pre-call and pre-check did not see the fight), so a filled v1 slot is now a standing group; a second companion in a chat of v1 Characters was built under the earlier rules (the chat's blocks were taken for old ones); the Skills screen explained the earlier roll formula and four ranks for a v1 skill (it now shows the stat, the rank and a seven-rank bar); the Spend points sheet's radar stopped at 20 and its line spoke of blocks and luck (it runs to 30 and says HP, MP and SP); a character file exported from a card of the earlier rules writes its LUK as WIS; a pack skill set or cast skill that names LUK is built with WIS, and a pack may name WIS. **Model-facing changes beyond the four block descriptions:** the party-line grammar says `wis` where it said `luk`; the dice-bonus table has WIS where it had LUK; a check that names no stat and fits no skill word is worked with WIS (it was LUK), and the words for luck, will and senses point at WIS; the v1 Skills learn line shows `type` as required (a line without it still learns an attack skill); the v1 Character sentence and the `+ party` line no longer mention who they are / `about` (a party line that still carries `about:` is accepted and the text ignored). **Not changed (the earlier engine's removal, combat C11):** Settings → RPG still shows the earlier Standard formula's footnote and its "10 to 20" stat maximum; Gear accessories still give LUK and a piece cannot give WIS; in a chat that already holds a Character made under the earlier rules a new fight opens an earlier setup, the wizard builds v1 blocks beside the earlier ones (the engine refuses a mixed sheet), and a join builds the earlier blocks.
- **A fight from an attack runs in the new view (block live fix).** In the live check the waiting choice (the pause before the reader's attack goes out) showed only as the old view's pause card and the fight was played on the old board: the new view had no surface for the pause (`interaction.view` answers only an open fight) and the old view opened its own Fight screen. Now, while the new view is shown, the RPG's pause controller answers it through a page event (`modules/rpg/board/hold-bridge.js`): the old view's Fight screen is claimed by the new view (it opens its Fight tab instead, for the pause, for Send while it waits, and for a fight begun from the offer line), and the Fight tab lists the waiting fight as mock v7's offer (head, the offer card with Fight on the board, Auto-fight and, when the pre-check found the foes, Keep telling the story; then the foes at full health and the party; `ui/select/hold.js`, `ui/tabs/scene/fight-offer.js`). The answers go through the port's `interaction.choose` to the same controller the old pause uses, so the chat metadata is the same either way; on a fight the waiting message goes with, Send with this and the end card's Back to the story send that message (and the view closes; closing alone would leave it waiting) and Discard fight puts the offer back, as the old board does. With the old view chosen nobody claims the screen and the old pause and board are unchanged. The Now card's "A fight is open" row and the Fight tab show for the waiting fight too. While the pre-check is still reading the message the Fight tab shows its wait line and no buttons (the mock has no such state). The bridge says when the pause changes or can first be read (op `changed`), so a view mounted before the RPG screens loaded, or a reload into a waiting fight, lists the Fight tab then. While the new view is shown, the old view's "fight not sent yet" note (`.ss-send-note`) and its chat offer line for standing foes (`.rpg-fight-offer`) are hidden (`ui/css/next-hide.css`); the new view has no offer for foes left standing without an attack yet.
- **A stance switched off stays off across replies and with Auto; an ended fight has one primary button (block live fix, F2 and F5).** The Fight view's stance switch is read from the last exchange of the current reply only, so a fight that went on in the next reply showed it on again and paid 1 Stamina or Mana, and Auto pressed in the same exchange as the switch paid it too. Now the next reply's setup carries the heroes left off (`setup.stance`, from the steps sent: `modules/rpg/board/v1-stance.js`), the view shows the row off, and a pick with no `stance` in the first exchange of a reply, and Auto, keep it off. Turning it on over a carried off writes `stance: true` into the pick. The Auto command takes an optional `stance` (actor id → on or off) for the switch as left in the exchange being played; the step Auto stores holds the stance, so every replay (swipe, Re-read, rewind, reload) is the same. A fight from before this carries nothing and plays as before. On an ended fight the scene view no longer draws "Send with this" beside "Back to the story ›": Back sends first when a send is still owed, then goes to the chat.
- **New view: a person page left open no longer covers the suite.** Opening the suite on a tab (the menu entry, a fight, a chat row) closes a person's page left open over the tabs; before, a page left open when the suite was closed came back over every tab and took the clicks meant for the card's Settings. Opening on a tab the strip does not list yet (a fight that just began) reads the flags first. The person page's top bar no longer shows the text "null" where a control nobody answers (Edit) was left out.
- **No dead buttons in the new view (block fix 5).** Most action buttons of the new view sent command names the engine never registered, so they did nothing and said nothing. Now: **Spend points** (`rpg.stats.spend`) writes the raised stats and the points left as one `dm.set` on that member's Character block, as the old Spend points sheet does (refused when it would take the points below 0 or take points back); **Quiet scene** and **End this arc** reach the Director's `director.pulse.quiet` and `director.arc.end` (`ui/port/aliases.js`). The live port has `can(type)` (true for what it answers itself and for every command a registered module lists; the fake port says yes to everything, so the harness is unchanged), and a control whose command it refuses is not drawn: the dossier's Edit and each ⋯ entry, + Add a memory, Mark alive, People + Add, Party + Invite, skill Edit and Bench / Export / Leave, Bag Use on…, quest star, Edit, Save, give up and delete, Plan the next arc, and the Scene sheet's Save. The dossier's ⋯ › Refresh now sends the person's name (`npc.refresh` takes `{ name }`). The character card's ‹ Chat closes the suite the way the mini bar's does (it sent `suite.close`, which nothing answered). `tests/ui-gaps/live-commands.test.js` scans `ui/` for every command it sends so a new button cannot slip through. Not built: Edit, the ⋯ menu except Refresh, memories, Mark alive, + Add, Invite, Bench / Export / Leave, skill edit, Use on…, quest edit / give up / delete / focus, Plan the next arc and Scene Save have no engine command yet, so they stay hidden.
- **Desktop chrome matches the mock (CSS only).** From tablet width the tab pills are 37 px tall (`.su-tab`; the phone bar's 44 px is unchanged). The card's ‹ Chat is the mock's pill again (padding 6px 12px, radius 999px: 32 px tall above 900 px, 44 px tall when the window is 900 px wide or less), and its ⚙ has the mock's padding 1px 6px at normal weight. The Now tab's small lines (`.rnr small`, `.cr small`) are back to the mock's 18 px per line (the earlier 16 px block made every row 2 px short). `tests/ui/rendered/desktop-chrome.test.js` measures this at 390, 1143, 1280 and 1920, dark and light, including the distance from the first Now subline to the Show all button against the mock's value; the tab-strip and harness-sweep tests carry the 37 px / 32 px exceptions.
- **Settings covers the window at desktop (CSS only).** While Settings is open the suite root drops its 820 px column (`.su-layer:has(.sov:not([hidden])) .su-root{max-width:none}` in `settings.css`), so from 601 px the overlay is 18 px in from the window's edges, as in the mock (decision 17), instead of inside the centred panel; the phone is unchanged. The page rules that read the container width (the 900 px touch sizes, the 600 px phone list) now see the window, so at desktop Settings uses the mock's mouse sizes rather than 44 px targets above 900 px. `tests/ui/rendered/settings-window-cover.test.js` (replaces `settings-1280-targets`) measures the box at 390, 1143, 1280 and 1920, dark and light.
- **Now trip before it starts; Story told this reply.** `atlas.trip` answers phase `planned` (with `departs`, the date the first leg starts) when it is asked at a story minute before the trip's first leg starts (an earlier reply's card, a swipe back, a rewind); the new view's Now trip card shows it at 0 percent with "leaves …" (before this a planned trip showed as on the road, and the card hid it). The map, the road marker and the narrator's lines still read the trip as before. `director.plan` marks an agenda `told: true` when its thread changed on the Director's current turn, and the Story tab's agenda shows it as told this reply (it was always off); an agenda not written in this reply carries no `told`. The step line ("step 2 of 3") was already read from the plan. The focus pick and the per-chat spoiler setting are not built.
- **The earlier rules, the roll planner and narrated fights are removed (combat C11).** Every fight is played by code on Combat Rules v1; there is no other engine. Gone: the Fight board switch (the board is on whenever the RPG is), the Rolls setting and its row in Settings → RPG (what stays in that card is Story checks, Auto-fight's cap and the fight set-up switch), the narrated and manual fight grammar (the narrator may still set a foe slot and clear a group that left; it never hurts one), `rpg.resolve` and `rpg.noroll` (no chat holds a record of either), the earlier fight engine, its Fight view and its note, the exact-odds bar under the Difficulty numbers (it read an earlier Character), the earlier Character, Foes and Combat rules templates, the earlier sheet reader (a Character, Skills or Foes block that is not a v1 one is left out of the sheet with a plain Problem sentence), the earlier wizard and join branches, and the story practice rule's Rolls setting (practice comes from rolled uses while the RPG is on). A character file, or a skill in one, that still says LUK is read as WIS (each is one line in the file reader); a `+ party` line whose `best:` says LUK is refused with the list of the seven stats. The cast and the world pulse now see a filled Combat Rules v1 foe slot as a fight on (their reader stopped at a Foes block with no `hurt` field). The page start loads 12,769 bytes less RPG code (the lazy-load cap is 110,400).
- **Built-in skill sets carry v1 types; the pre-check's raw tier answer is kept.** The New character wizard's five built-in sets now give each skill a Combat Rules v1 type (a set holds at most one stance skill) and the wizard keeps it: before, every skill from a set was an attack (Guild fighter's Iron Breath and Shield work included). Guild fighter: Short blade attack, Iron Breath utility, Shield work defence. River scout: Rope work utility, Swimming movement, Tracking utility. Hedge mage: Spark attack, Ward defence, Lore utility. Field medic: Mending heal, Herbs utility, Calm support. Silver tongue: Persuade, Read people and Haggle utility. A set's utility skills fill the three utility slots and its others the combat slots; choosing another set replaces the earlier set's skills, and a utility skill the reader made stays. Pack skill sets are unchanged (they have no type field). The fight pre-check's raw tier answer (`tierSaid`, null when it named none) is now kept in the fight record's setup, beside the tier the fight uses, so a chosen "standard" can be told from the fallback; it is diagnostic only, not shown and not sent to the model.
- **Add to party on a People page.** A person's page in the new view has an **Add to party** button when RPG is on, the person is alive, is not You, is not in the party (nor a companion who left) and the party has room for another of 4 active. It opens the join card a `+ party` line gives (`rpg.partyAdd` builds it from `joinCard`): **Add to party** sends the same reader action (`dm.join`, cause `ui`), so it goes with its reply on a swipe, a delete, a rewind, a Re-read and a reload; **Edit first** opens the New character window filled in (name; class I and no best stat, since the dossier holds neither), through a page event the old view answers (`port.open`); **Not now** only closes the card and answers no story offer (it never sends `rpg.joinNo`). The button label on the card is the card's own, "Add to party". When the New character window from **Edit first** closes with the companion created, the open person page redraws (no button, "in your party"); closed without Create, it stays as it was. The refusal note on the card shows in the warning colour. Nothing changes with RPG off.
- **One-pass capture is on by default.** NPC State's `narrationCapture` (the change lines in the narrator's reply, with the rolling Refresh) is on for new installs (settings default, getter and the settings-defaults golden); a stored `false` stays off and nothing is migrated. Settings › People has the "One-pass capture" switch. The static-prompt golden's default scenarios now carry NPC State's one-pass rules (`story_suite_static_npc_state_delta`, the text the one-pass scenario already pinned); that scenario is now the default, and a new `npc_state_delta_one_pass_off` scenario pins the switched-off prompt. The old panel's "Refresh every" box kept turning a typed 0 into 4; 0 (off) is now kept (`cutSettingNumber`).
- **Settings › People in the new view.** The page draws mock v7's People groups with live values and stores them under the same NPC State settings the old panel's controls store (`storySuite.modules['npc-state-delta']`; one value serves both views): Starting values (`relationshipBaseline`), How far one moment moves a feeling (`relationshipCaps`), Moments per story day (`relationshipAllowance`, blank is no limit), Safety ceiling (`changeCap`), Replies before a stage is lost (`demoteAfter`), the repeated-approach window and strength (`gateDays`, `gateStrength`), tension hold (`conflictAt`), Who gets a dossier (`admissionMode`), Dormant after (`staleArchiveAfter`, and Auto-delete after moves up with it as before), Custom fields (`customFieldNames`; Level is added by itself while the RPG is on), Refresh every (`narrationCaptureCatchUp`, N8's rolling Refresh: each person waits this many replies between Refreshes, 0 to 50, 0 = off, default 4), NPCs refreshed per reply (`narrationCaptureCatchUpPeople`, 1 to 3, default 1; both Refresh rows say they apply when one-pass capture is on, and it is on by default now), Refresh reads back (`scanDepth`), Short one-pass instruction (`narrationCaptureCompact`), People the narrator is told about (`injectLimit`), People block budget (`injectBudgetTokens`), Most people kept active (`maxNpcs`), Generate portraits (`portraitGenerationEnabled`) and the two Map rows (`relationshipMap`). Numbers are cut as the old boxes cut them. Reset People stores the defaults of these rows. What differs from the mock: **Who gets a dossier** offers Conservative, Balanced and Manual only (the engine has no "Generous"); **Custom fields** is one text box; Custom meters, Meter presets, Per-meter stages, Stage ladder, Style preset, Prompt, Leave out, Composition, Prompt format, Saved portrait presets and the three portrait switches (mood, place, gallery) are shown with their live values but are changed in the old view for now. **Refresh every** shows N8's default 4 (the mock drew 5) and a typed 0 is kept as off (the old box turned 0 into 4); **NPCs refreshed per reply** is a row the mock has not got (after Refresh every). Scan every (`scanEvery`, the scan cadence only when one-pass capture is off; a hosted one-pass chat has no scan by cadence) has no row here: the mock lists it as removed. A changed injection setting (people told about, block budget) reaches the next reply's prompt when the people part next rebuilds it, not at the click. The Hidden notes setting (`layeredSecrets`) has no row in the mock and no row here. **One-pass capture** (`narrationCapture`) is a row the mock has not got, first in Refresh and the narrator: the narrator writes NPC State's changes in its reply; off = background scans. The old panel's checkbox writes the same key.
- **Fresh start.** Nothing from the three old extensions is carried over: no settings, chats or sidecar files are migrated. See `docs/INSTALL.md` for the install steps and for removing the old three.
- **One bundle set.** The release and play builds pack the one extension from the root `index.js` and `manifest.json` into one bundle set (the RPG library and its screens stay separate lazy chunks; the People part is packed into the entry; the gate allows ten requests at load and counts any chunk the entry awaits at start; a build is 9 requests today, JS and CSS together). The bundle is a build output (`npm run release`, or `npm run play:dist` for a play build); it is not published yet, so installing from the repository's URL loads the source files one by one. In a build, `suite.css` and every `@import` it pulls in become one stylesheet (rules in the same order, fonts copied to the same paths); the source sheets are unchanged, and a source install still loads them through `@import`. The gate counts the stylesheet among the requests. Host imports (`extensions.js`, `script.js`, `utils.js`, `shared.js`) in `modules/people` are written for the depth of the file in the one extension folder, so a checkout installed as it is loads; the bundler keeps each at the depth of the file that names it.
- **Code layout.** `core/` (host), `modules/` (scene, inventory, data modules, assist, RPG, time, atlas, world, cast, arc, people), `ui/` (the new view and `ui/legacy`, the old view), `packs/`, `testkit/`. See `docs/codemap.md`.
- **The version people see is 0.1.0.** The console line and the Extensions drawer read `Story Suite 0.1.0` (`SUITE_VERSION`). The number modules and packs check their "needs Story Block 2.x" ranges against stays 2.33.0 as `HOST_VERSION` and in `StoryBlock.version`.
- **NPC State cannot stop the rest.** Its start (`startPeople`) runs through `startIsolated` after the modules: a failure is a MODULE_START Problem in Diagnostics and the view still starts.
- **Models page.** It has the Calls group (one call at a time, calls the reply waits on, the fallback call, People's output limit, what the fallback may write). The fallback call (the safety net that reads changes a reply forgot) now runs on the Background connection and is listed on that card, as the design says; before it ran on Quick helpers. This changes where that call goes for anyone who set the two connections differently.
- **Models page: Quick helpers list.** The Quick helpers card's fight item now reads "fight set-up from your attack: the pre-check (up to 20 s)", naming the call that runs as a quick helper (one item, not two). Text only; no call changes connection. The Assist safety net is not listed there: it runs on the Background connection and is covered by "the fallback call" on that card.
- **Quote sources read as #N.** A stored quote or memory that starts with a `[m12]` tag shows as `#12` (the message number SillyTavern shows) in the NPC State dossier: the relationship change reasons, the board tiles, the mood section, the Past section and the select view. Display only: what is stored, and the key a memory's date stamp is found by, keep the `[m12]` text.
- **Models page saves.** The queue switch, the fallback switch, what the fallback may write and each connection's pick are stored through the host's own settings and feature stores (`StoryBlock.writes`, for the new view only); the public sdk has no setter, and before this those controls saved nothing. The one-line note under a connection picker (no profiles, Connection Manager off) now has its style.
- **Story › Trackers works.** Show on Now writes the block's `show.card` (`dm.redefine`, as the old Blocks page did); Edit opens a sheet that changes values by hand (`dm.set`, only the fields you changed, numbers checked against their limits) and has Remove for a tracker this chat made (`dm.remove`, after a confirm); "Add from a template" makes one of the three built-in starters (`dm.define`; it starts hidden from Now). The commands are `tracker.pin`, `tracker.set`, `tracker.remove` and `tracker.add`, answered in `ui/port/trackers.js`. The Trackers list now reads the active block definition (`state`, field labels, `show`) instead of the pack's offer, so bars and Show on Now follow what is pinned. Not built: the old Customize/layout editor and editing a tracker's fields (the mock has neither); the Settings › Packs and trackers page is still not live.
- **No "Scene" tab for activities.** The Suite view's tab for an open interaction is named after the activity (its title, "Activity" if none, cut to 16 characters), shown only while one is open, with a small "Basic view" note when its module registered no renderer; Send and Discard work as before. A developer-only Template page (Settings → Tools → Developer tools, a per-device switch, off by default) shows the generic renderer on a sample for module authors. The word Scene stays for the Here card and the Scene sheet. The old view is untouched.
- **Fight pre-check names a tier.** On a Combat Rules v1 sheet (every new sheet) the pre-check's Assist prompt now asks each foe group for its tier (mob, minion, standard, elite, boss or apex, with examples; standard when unsure) and says the size rule (an even fight is at most 4 standard foes, a group of elites at most 3; bigger crowds are several groups). The reply shape gains `"tier"`. A sheet that is not v1 gets the old prompt unchanged.
- **The card's pack offer in the new view.** When the open character card ships a pack, the new view's Now tab shows it in the Needs you card (Install or Update, the "Also apply" tick, Not now; Dismiss on the result), and the chat's Needs you chip counts it ("1 pack offer"). It is the old panel's own offer, not a second one: the same controller answers both, so Install, Not now, the remembered "Not now" and the Diagnostics lines are the same. The Packs page is unchanged: its "Read from the character's lorebook" button is not wired to anything in the new view yet. With no offer, Now and the chip are unchanged.
- **Reply changes in the new view.** A new query `reply.changes({ index? })` (Story Block core, `modules/scene/domains/core/reply-changes.js`) lists what one reply changed, built only from that reply's receipts: items gained or lost, the coin (net), a move to a new place and who arrived or left, with the place and weather of the state asked on; the head reply by default, `{ asOf, index }` for an earlier one; null when the reply has no receipts. The ✦ reply menu and the inline row now read it (before they asked two names nothing answered, so they showed no lines or counts); the chat view takes `{ index }` to show an earlier reply's menu. The query's `refused` list is always empty: the lines a reply did not use are told once by the turn and are not saved anywhere a reload can read, so "not applied" shows 0 until they are. The reader's own edits (`ui` receipts) are not counted. Wiring the open fight into the inline row is a separate change.
- **Fixes.** The old view's `size.css` imported a stylesheet from a path that does not exist (a 404 at every start; the data-module screens lost their number-list, guild-tile and Enable all styles); `npm run check` now fails on any `@import` that names no file. The install guide names the Assist page's Extra calls for the default old view and says to run `npm ci`.
- **Deaths once (Key people).** The Director no longer teaches the narrator `- key Name | died`: the fixed prompt loses exactly two pieces (the `- key Name | died (anyone who dies)` grammar line and the last sentence of the Key people paragraph, `Write - key Name | died only for a death.`), and nothing else. The rest of the Key people paragraph (`Key people are the people {{user}} follows ...` up to `bring them back.`) stays, word for word. The `away` grammar line stays, and the away line in the tail does too. Deaths are read from NPC State (the same reading as before: `npc.known` / `npc.dossier`, and the Story Suite api). The `key` kind stays registered with the head `- key` so replies that already carry `- key Name | died` replay unchanged (swipe, swipe back, an edited Re-read, a middle delete, rewind, reload). Without NPC State a pinned person leaves only by Unpin (or by an old `- key` line when a reply is read again); no new death is recorded, so a dead person's own threads stay open; the narrator is not asked for one.
- **One Story Suite drawer.** Extensions shows one drawer headed `Story Suite 0.1.0` (the old "Story Block" header is gone). NPC State's settings are a section of it headed "NPC State", not a second "NPC State Delta v1.18.0" drawer; if NPC State mounts before the drawer exists, the drawer moves it in when it is drawn.
- **Character card picture without a party hero.** In a chat with no party hero the card shows the people row named like your persona, else your persona's avatar, instead of the initial; the card now draws a safe picture path (before, only the dev harness did).
- **The Fight tab in the new view.** A fight that is open now shows on the Fight tab of the Suite view (it was never reached before): the fight board is drawn by the activity host's fight renderer, you choose for every party member, and Send and Discard are the host's. Now's "Needs you" card and the chat row's fight chip open it; a sent or taken-back fight keeps the tab but no longer asks for you. Any other open interaction still has its own named tab. The fight's dice are the same after a swipe and back, a Re-read, a rewind, a middle delete, a reload and a chat change. The new view's Fight tab has no stance switch (Combat Rules v1 has none).
- **Tools page (new view).** Settings › Tools now shows, read from the live engine each time it is drawn: the budget bars and the text sent with the next reply (with the three budget rows under the bars, and the footer meter), the Inspector (a sheet: pick an older or newer reply; its changed lines, what code did, its problems and the state after; read again at each move), Problems (the turn's problems, and a blocked branch's "dossiers can't be restored" entry with the same two choices, the last 40 replies or the whole chat, which replaces the old banner on this page) and Diagnostics (Copy report). The Developer card and its Template page are unchanged. `StoryBlock.tools` is a new read-only handle for these (it also carries the rebuild button's call); the old view is untouched. Not built because nothing in the engine backs them yet: the Prompts rows (read, edit and reset the People and Arc planner prompts), Chat data (export and import this chat's suite data, "Rewind when a middle message is deleted", "Re-read changed branches") and Diagnostics › Pack events. Save points have no page in the new view (restart from a message instead).
- **RPG read model for Party, Card and Now (G5a).** Two new queries, both gated by the RPG feature (null with it off): `rpg.sheet` answers one row per member of the party (not those who left) with `id`, `name`, `level`, `line` ("Lv 14 · Guild F"), `className`, `bars` (hp, mp, sp), `xpPct`, `points`, `soakPct` (VIT plus the worn gear's soak, capped at 50; the Defence stance adds its mastery only in a fight), `stats` (STR VIT AGI DEX INT WIS CHA on a Combat Rules v1 Character, `null` on an earlier one) and `skills` (kind, stat, cost, rank and mastery from the v1 tables); `party.terms` answers `{ levelName: "Lv" }`, which the game header and the Card were already asking for and nothing answered. The new view's Party tab, Card (level, class line, bars, exp, soak chip) and Now tab (a "points to spend" row that opens Party) read them. Not built: `away` and `effects` stay empty (nothing keeps where an absent companion went or a timed condition), a member's picture and the per-item soak stay gaps, and the Fight board is not changed.
- **Pack format 3 (pack format 3 job).** A pack says `"format": 3` (left out it is format 2; a newer number than 3 is refused) and names the suite it needs with `requires.suite` (`"^0.1"`; `requires.storyBlock` still loads, checked against the host number 2.33.0 or the suite version). Skills in a pack use Combat Rules v1's words: stats with WIS (LUK is read as WIS), the eight types (`combat` is read as `attack`), `uses` sp or mp (stamina and mana still accepted), ranks novice to legend; a cast member's skill type now reaches the Skills block when they join or return (before, only `utility` did), and a join receipt's tile names the rank with v1's seven words. `content.rpg.formula` is `"v1"` (`"standard"` accepted); `content.rpg.numbers` is accepted with a warning and ignored, so a pack cannot tune combat numbers. `creatures, shops, recipes, quests, races, talents, presets, terms, ranks, crafts` and the top-level `layouts` and `screens` are accepted with a warning and left out (their checks and tests are removed). New `content.foes`: a bestiary of name, aliases, tier (mob to apex), level, about and tags; the fight pre-check uses a listed foe's tier and level, and an unlisted foe keeps the pre-check's tier. Ersveil is 3.0.0 (a minimal conversion: every skill has an explicit v1 type, the two LUK items give DEX, no lore was rewritten); its add-ons are 2.0.0 and survival asks for `director`, not `story-director`. `riverside-starter` is now a test fixture in `tests/packs/fixtures`, not a shipped example. `docs/v2/PACKS.md` "Pack format 3".

</details>
